# Mathematical review of the lean-DLMF project (formerly lean-AAR)

## Scope and degree of completion

**Current status, 2026-10-25:** 890 explicit source admissions after the acquisition
of DLMF Chapters 20–23 (theta, Riemann theta, Jacobian elliptic, Weierstrass and modular
functions), which adds 418: Theta 70, RiemannTheta 10, JacobiElliptic 215 and

audit finds 15,623 project theorems, with 1,025 depending on `sorryAx`, and no other
nonstandard axiom. `ToMathlib` has no admissions.
See [the Chapters 20–23 acquisition](#chapters-20-23-acquisition-2026-10-25).

which proved 319 admissions and corrected 16 false or mis-transcribed statements.
The status paragraph of 2026-10-19 follows: 1,609 explicit admissions in 118 Lean files.
The [reuse review](#reuse-of-the-proved-special-function-core-2026-10-19)
prioritizes specialization and bridge proofs. The
[Chapters 11, 12 and 14 acquisition](DLMF111214Acquisition.md) adds
601 admissions after completing 25 of 626 newly acquired statements. The previous
1,008 admissions are unchanged. The compiled audit finds no completed public proof
depending on an admission. Earlier inventories and validation reports below describe
their dated snapshots, not this enlarged acquisition scope.

This project develops special functions in Lean, using Andrews, Askey and Roy's
*Special Functions* and the NIST Digital Library of Mathematical Functions as its
main mathematical sources. Its purpose is to extend Mathlib's coverage, rather
than reproduce either reference sequentially. The principal subjects are Gamma
and Beta functions, Gaussian and confluent hypergeometric functions, Bessel
functions, and orthogonal polynomials. Smaller developments concern Selberg
integrals, spherical harmonics, basic hypergeometric series, partitions, and Bailey
chains. The entry point [DLMF.lean](DLMF.lean) imports all these areas.

The project has a substantial formally proved core, especially in hypergeometric
continuation, differential and recurrence identities, and general orthogonal
polynomial theory. It also contains a substantial collection of proposed theorem
statements whose proofs are admitted with `sorry`. Some of these statements need
mathematical correction before they can be proved. Thus the project is a mixture
of established formal mathematics and a statement collection under development.

The admission column was last updated on 2026-10-17 (169 admissions, counted from the
build's `sorry` warnings). The declaration counts and the audit statements in the next
paragraphs refer to 2026-10-08, when the total was 319.

There are 102 Lean files under `DLMF/`, including the subject import files; 53 contain
admissions. All 319 source admissions occur in theorem declarations, not in
definitions. The other 1,757 theorem and lemma declarations have proof bodies.
These numbers measure the source inventory, not the proportion of AAR or DLMF
formalized: a short specialization and a long foundational theorem each count as
one declaration.

The complete project builds with its pinned Lean and Mathlib version
`v4.35.0-rc3`. An axiom-dependency audit of the compiled project declarations finds
only Lean's standard `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`, together with
`sorryAx` for admissions. Among publicly named declarations, the declarations
depending on `sorryAx` are precisely the 319 explicitly admitted theorems; there
are no additional completed public proofs concealing a dependency on an admitted
result. No project `axiom` declarations occur. A successful build nevertheless
does not certify an admitted statement, or that a definition agrees with its
classical interpretation outside the hypotheses of the proved theorems.

## Mathematical conventions that matter

Several distinctions are essential when reading the code.

**A series and its analytic continuation are different objects.** Mathlib's
ordinary Gaussian hypergeometric function is defined by its power series. An
infinite-sum expression is not, by itself, analytic continuation beyond the
convergence region. The project supplies a separate regularized continuation
`DLMF.Hypergeometric.regularizedGaussHG` on
\(\mathbb C\setminus[1,\infty)\). Identities involving transformed arguments must
either use this continued function or impose convergence conditions on each
series argument. This corresponds to the distinction made in
[DLMF §15.2](https://dlmf.nist.gov/15.2).

**Regularization is essential at exceptional parameters.** The ordinary Kummer
function `kummerM` is a totalized series. Its quotient by \(\Gamma(b)\) agrees
with the regularized function away from nonpositive integer \(b\), but its literal
value at a pole need not be the limiting regularized value. Mathlib's
`regularizedHGFun` supplies the appropriate entire coefficient expressions.
Similarly, a product containing a vanishing factor and a Gamma pole cannot be
interpreted as a removable limit merely by multiplying the totalized values.
The normalization follows [DLMF §13.2](https://dlmf.nist.gov/13.2).

**Complex powers use a specified branch.** `Complex.slitPlane` excludes the
nonpositive real axis. Powers and logarithms on this domain represent principal
branches; identities obtained by rotations require the corresponding argument
conditions. The logarithmic-argument versions of Tricomi and Whittaker functions
retain information about continuation around the origin that would be lost by
simply replacing an argument by its complex exponential.

**Improper integrals and unordered sums require care.** A Bochner integral is not
a conditionally convergent improper integral. Likewise, an unordered `tsum` is not
automatically the limit of the usual ordered partial sums. The project uses
explicit limits for several oscillatory integrals and boundary series. These
distinctions remain important when adding further integral and asymptotic results.

**Classical polynomial parameters have a restricted interpretation.** The
Laguerre and Jacobi functions are defined by terminating hypergeometric formulas
with Pochhammer prefactors. These formulas give the standard families on the
regular parameter domains, including the usual orthogonality ranges. They do not
automatically provide polynomial continuation in all parameters. For example,
the current definition gives `laguerrePolyL (-1) 1 x = 0`, whereas the classically
continued polynomial is \(L_1^{(-1)}(x)=-x\). The degree-zero definitions still give
one. Any extension to exceptional parameters needs a denominator-free finite sum
or a regularized definition, together with comparison theorems.

## Gamma and Beta functions

The [Gamma cluster](DLMF/Gamma.lean) builds on Mathlib's Gamma function, Euler and
Beta integrals, reflection formula, duplication formula, and logarithmic
convexity. Its proved contributions include the Weierstrass product for reciprocal
Gamma, Gauss multiplication, digamma recurrence and reflection identities,
several rational and complex special values, polygamma recurrence and reflection,
and the entire Taylor expansion of reciprocal Gamma. The expansion has a
`HasSum` theorem for every complex argument, rather than only a formal coefficient
identity.

The analytic results include Beta integrals on transformed intervals and
half-lines, trigonometric and hyperbolic Beta integrals, Gaussian and Laplace
Gamma integrals, and the asymptotic Gamma ratio
\[
  \frac{\Gamma(t+a)}{\Gamma(t+b)t^{a-b}}\longrightarrow 1
  \qquad(t\to+\infty),
\]
for fixed complex shifts. The Stirling development proves the usual factorial
asymptotic and its extension along fixed translates \(n+t\), with
\(0<t\leq1\). This should not be confused with a complete complex-sector Stirling
theory with arbitrary-order remainder estimates.

Of the 58 admissions counted on 2026-10-08, two remain (2026-10-17): the complex
Gamma-ratio bound 5.6.8 and the de Branges–Wilson integral 5.13.5. The Binet, Barnes
\(G\), Mellin–Barnes, multidimensional, \(q\)-Gamma and series-expansion admissions
were proved in the passes recorded below.

[ContinuedFractions.lean](DLMF/Gamma/ContinuedFractions.lean) records seven
tabulated partial numerators for the continued fraction associated with Binet's
function and assigns zero to the remaining coefficients. It therefore represents
a finite truncation; it contains no proof that these convergents approach Binet's
function. A full coefficient sequence is missing mathematical data, rather than
merely a missing convergence proof.

## Gaussian hypergeometric functions

The [hypergeometric cluster](DLMF/Hypergeometric.lean) is the most nearly complete
of the large subjects. Its proved theory includes coefficient and terminating-sum
identities, differentiation, the hypergeometric differential equation, contiguous
relations, Euler's integral, Euler and Pfaff transformations, numerous quadratic
transformations and elementary evaluations, and substantial connection-formula
theory, including logarithmic cases.

The principal analytic construction is in
[ArgumentContinuation.lean](DLMF/Hypergeometric/ArgumentContinuation.lean).
The regularized function is constructed on the cut plane using Euler integrals
in a suitable parameter region and contiguous relations to shift the parameters.
The code proves analyticity in the argument, agreement with the series on the
unit disk, and uniqueness. Derivative formulas, contiguous relations, Euler's
transformation, and Pfaff's transformation are available on the continued domain.
This is an actual construction of the continued function, not a declaration that
the original series is to be interpreted differently.

Parameter continuation is also developed: the regularized disk function is
entire along entire parameter curves, with separate analyticity and parameter
limits at denominator poles. The single theorem expressing joint complex
differentiability as a map of three complex parameters remains admitted.
The Riemann-equation material includes a hypergeometric solution, gauge shifts,
and Möbius covariance. Boundary-series convergence and limiting behavior near
\(z=1\) have substantial proved treatments with explicit parameter hypotheses.

The 15 admissions consist of joint parameter differentiability, two asymptotic
results, a cubic connection formula, three continued-fraction limits, four special
values, Whipple's terminating \({}_7F_6\) transformation, and three integral
representations. Dixon's \({}_3F_2\) and Dougall's bilateral formula were proved
later via Carlson's theorem (see the section on Carlson's theorem below). The classical series formulas and
the cut-plane API also coexist: not every theorem about the series is stated in
the strongest possible continued form.

## Confluent hypergeometric and Whittaker functions

The [confluent cluster](DLMF/Confluent.lean) defines Kummer's \(M\), Tricomi's
\(U\), and Whittaker's \(M_{\kappa,\mu}\) and \(W_{\kappa,\mu}\). The proved
Kummer theory includes entire convergence in the argument at regular denominator
parameters, the confluent limit of \({}_2F_1\), Euler's integral, the differential
equation, Kummer's transformation, differentiation, and contiguous relations.
For the regularized function, parameter analyticity permits derivative and
contiguous identities without excluding denominator poles.

Tricomi's function is defined by the connection formula at noninteger second
parameters and by parameter limits at integers. Here the limits are supported by
proofs: [BasicProperties.lean](DLMF/Confluent/BasicProperties.lean) proves their
existence at every nonzero argument, including integer second parameters. It also
provides continuity in the parameters, joint continuity on the argument slit
plane, and locally uniform convergence needed for differentiation and parameter
passage. Thus the integer-parameter definition is backed by analysis, rather than
only by a formal limit operator.

Further proved material includes Tricomi transformations, differential and
recurrence identities, terminating cases, several Wronskians, continuation and
jump formulas, and Taylor-based addition formulas. The Whittaker results transfer
much of this structure through their defining transformations. Logarithmic-argument
definitions make the monodromy statements mathematically meaningful.

The full Kummer Laplace transform is proved throughout `Re z > max (0, Re k)`;
[Growth.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Growth.lean) supplies the polynomial exponential bound
and holomorphic integral dependence, including denominator poles. The normalized
Tricomi Laplace representation is also proved for `Re a > 0`, `Re z > 0`, and
every complex second parameter. A Gauss confluence limit identifies the integral
with the connection-formula definition, and continuity extends it to integer
parameters. The Whittaker W Laplace representation follows by an affine substitution.

Tricomi's Mellin transform is proved for
`max (0, Re (b - 1)) < Re λ < Re a`, together with absolute integrability.

The admissions (115 in the original inventory, 69 on 2026-10-17) are concentrated in integral representations and transforms,
asymptotics, zero distributions, products and additions, and Bessel expansions.
The proved positive-half-plane Laplace representation of \(U\) now supplies a
foundation for positivity, integral estimates, and asymptotic arguments. Many
Mellin and Laplace transforms, Stieltjes representations, and further Whittaker
integrals still require justified exchanges of integrals or continuation.
All four continued-fraction
limit statements remain admitted. The exceptional-parameter defects found in two Bessel-sum statements have been
repaired as described below.

## Bessel functions

The [Bessel cluster](DLMF/Bessel.lean) defines \(J_\nu\) and \(I_\nu\) using
regularized \({}_0F_1\) series. It proves their differential equations and
slit-plane analyticity, recurrences and derivative identities for \(J\), its
half-integer evaluations and Poisson integral, and its relation to Kummer's
function. Integer-order reflection and the bilateral generating function are
proved, with a `HasSum` theorem supplying convergence for every complex argument
and every nonzero generating parameter.

[Sonine.lean](DLMF/Bessel/Sonine.lean) proves
\[
 \int_0^\infty J_\nu(at)e^{-p^2t^2}t^{\nu+1}\,dt
 =\frac{(a/(2p^2))^\nu}{2p^2}
   e^{-a^2/(4p^2)},
 \qquad \Re\nu>-1,\quad a,p>0.
\]
Its proof supplies reusable Gamma-weighted \({}_0F_1\) integrals and justifies
termwise integration by absolute convergence of a regularized \({}_1F_1\)
majorant series.

The two admissions are Gegenbauer's addition theorem and Nicholson's integral. Strict
monotonicity of the first positive zero for nonnegative order is proved in
[Zeros.lean](DLMF/Bessel/Zeros.lean) by Sturm comparison: `W = J_ν B_μ - B_ν J_μ`, with
`B_ν = z J_ν'`, has `W' = (μ²-ν²)/z · J_ν J_μ` (Watson §15.6). The statement moved there unchanged. Simplicity of the
positive zeros in the stated range is proved, and so is the existence of infinitely many
positive zeros (see the Bessel pass below). The integer-order values of \(Y_\nu\) and
\(K_\nu\), defined by parameter limits, are now known to exist and to be jointly continuous.

The definitions of \(Y_\nu\) and \(K_\nu\) use parameter limits at integer
orders. Unlike the corresponding Tricomi construction, existence of these limits
is not yet proved. Their differential-equation theorems consequently cover
noninteger orders. This is a foundational gap particularly relevant to Nicholson's
integral, which uses \(K_0\). Mathlib itself also defines `Complex.besselJ`; the
project's definition uses the same normalization but is a separate declaration.

## Orthogonal polynomials

The [orthogonal-polynomial cluster](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials.lean) has both an
abstract measure-theoretic theory and a large collection of named families.
`IsOrthogonalPolynomials` requires exact degree, integrability of squares,
strictly positive squared norms, and pairwise orthogonality. These hypotheses
exclude degenerate measures and provide a polynomial basis.

The abstract results include the three-term recurrence, real and simple roots,
positive-weight Gauss quadrature exact below degree \(2n\), and the
Christoffel–Darboux identity. Additional proved results concern reproducing
kernels, positive Hankel determinants, recurrence coefficients and squared norms,
and parity for symmetric measures. Parseval and convergence of the finite
expansions are proved for compactly supported measures, as is orthogonality of
the kernel polynomials with the modified weight. Favard's converse theorem,
interlacing, and several determinant formulas remain admitted. Completeness
is not claimed for an arbitrary orthogonality measure.

For the classical families, the physicists' Hermite polynomials are genuine
polynomials over the integers and are related to Mathlib's probabilists' Hermite
polynomials. The code proves Rodrigues and generating-function identities,
orthogonality and norms, derivative and recurrence formulas, and several
addition, multiplication, linearization, and integral identities. Laguerre and
Jacobi orthogonality are proved in their classical real parameter ranges.
Laguerre generating functions and squared norms are proved, as are the weighted
Laguerre convolution, Jacobi squared norms, both Rodrigues formulas, and Gegenbauer
orthogonality, including its degree-zero norm. The differential
equations and a substantial set of raising, lowering, reflection, and derivative
formulas for the classical families are proved. There are explicit bridges to
Mathlib's Chebyshev and shifted Legendre polynomials. Normalized Gegenbauer and
Legendre Turán inequalities are also proved.

The wider family coverage includes Hahn, Krawtchouk, Meixner, Charlier, continuous
Hahn, Meixner–Pollaczek, Wilson and continuous dual Hahn polynomials, several
\(q\)-families, associated and exceptional families, disk and triangle
polynomials, and orthogonal polynomials on the unit circle. Much of the finite
algebra is available, but many orthogonality measures, norms, and generating
functions remain to be established. On the unit circle, Szegő recurrences,
the bound on Verblunsky coefficients, and norm ratios are proved; the converse
existence theorem is admitted.

There were 102 admissions in the original inventory (69 on 2026-10-17). The largest concentrations are bounds and zeros (23),
integral and transform identities (22), and series identities (11). All four
Mehler–Heine limit statements are admitted. General root theory
does not automatically discharge the separate statements locating the zeros of
each named family in its natural interval. Establishing systematic connections
between the abstract polynomial API and the function-valued classical definitions
would make this proved general theory more broadly usable.

## Other subjects

**Basic hypergeometric series.** The [\(q\)-series development](DLMF/QSeries.lean)
contains finite and infinite \(q\)-Pochhammer symbols, convergence and
nonvanishing results for the products, Jackson integration, and definitions of
basic hypergeometric series. The \(q\)-binomial theorem, Heine's transformation,
and the \(q\)-Beta integral are proved. Jacobi's triple product and its
theta-function form are proved (2026-10-16), as is the finite \(q\)-Pfaff–Saalschütz
sum. Ramanujan's \({}_1\psi_1\) summation is the one remaining admission.

**Selberg integrals.** The [Selberg files](DLMF/Selberg.lean) define the integral
and record its evaluation, Aomoto's extension, discriminant and inequality
applications, a terminating summation, and finite-field identities. The
one-variable Beta specialization is proved. Since 2026-10-17 the general Selberg
and Aomoto evaluations are proved, as are the Jacobi discriminant (8.5.3) and the
nearly poised \({}_3F_2\) (8.9.1(a)); Siegel's inequality, Hasse–Davenport, and the
finite-field Selberg integral remain admitted. The related Dirichlet-simplex,
Laguerre, Hermite (Mehta), and circular (Dyson) integrals in the Gamma cluster are
all proved.

**Spherical harmonics.** The [spherical-harmonic files](DLMF/SphericalHarmonics.lean)
define the polynomial Laplacian, homogeneous harmonic polynomials, and the
surface-measure inner product on the Euclidean sphere. The surface-area
normalization in three dimensions is proved. The function
`harmonicHomogeneousDim` is a combinatorial formula, with its value \(2k+1\)
proved in three variables; it is not yet identified with the vector-space
dimension of harmonic homogeneous polynomials. Orthogonality of different
degrees, the addition theorem, Funk–Hecke, the Gegenbauer addition formula,
Poisson reproduction, and the double covering \(SU(2)\to SO(3)\) remain admitted.

**Partitions and Bailey chains.** [Partitions.lean](DLMF/Partitions.lean) uses
Mathlib's partition type and imports its generating-function, Glaisher, and
pentagonal-number theory. It defines the partition-counting function and proves
its value at zero. The three Ramanujan congruences are admitted.
[Bailey.lean](DLMF/Bailey.lean) defines Bailey pairs; both Rogers–Ramanujan
identities, Bailey's lemma, and Watson's terminating transformation are proved
(2026-10-16).

## Statement defects repaired on 2026-10-07

The review identified the following concrete counterexamples. Their statements
have now been repaired. The first was later proved; the two Kummer–Bessel sums
remain admitted.

1. **The Gamma sine integral at zero.** In
   [Gamma/IntegralRepresentations.lean](DLMF/Gamma/IntegralRepresentations.lean),
   `DLMF.Gamma.Gamma_sin` formerly permitted \(-1<x<1\), including \(x=0\), and gave the
   limit as `Real.Gamma x * Real.sin (π * x / 2)`. At zero that expression is zero
   in Lean, whereas the improper integral is
   \(\int_0^\infty\sin(t)/t\,dt=\pi/2\).
   The classical formula [DLMF 5.9.7](https://dlmf.nist.gov/5.9.E7) requires a
   removable-singularity interpretation there; compare the sine-integral
   definitions and limit in [DLMF §6.2(ii)](https://dlmf.nist.gov/6.2.ii).
   The repaired statement uses the regular expression
   \(\pi/(2\Gamma(1-x)\cos(\pi x/2))\) throughout the original interval,
   giving \(\pi/2\) at zero.

2. **Two finite Kummer–Bessel sums at a Gamma pole.** In
   [Confluent/Relations.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Relations.lean), both
   `DLMF.Confluent.kummerM_besselI_sum` and
   `DLMF.Confluent.kummerM_besselI_sum_neg` formerly allowed \(\nu=0,n=0,z=1\).
   Their denominator-parameter hypotheses were satisfied. Each right-hand side
   is zero because it contains the totalized factor \(\Gamma(0)\), while the
   left-hand side is \(M(1/2,1,2)>0\): its convergent defining series has constant
   term one and positive remaining terms. The repaired statements exclude every
   nonpositive integer \(\nu\), or \(\nu-n\), respectively, through the added
   `hΓ` hypothesis. Their denominator and argument hypotheses are preserved.

These examples are not an exhaustive certification or rejection of the other
admissions. Further checking is particularly important for endpoint cases,
Gamma-quotient cancellations, branch-dependent transformations, asymptotic
remainder scales, and empty or singleton index sets in multivariate formulas.
DLMF equation attributes document a source association; they do not certify the
translation of its conventions into Lean.

## Completed priority groups 1–4 (2026-10-07)

Ten admissions were replaced with proofs, preserving their mathematical hypotheses:

| Group | Completed theorems | Proof ingredients |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `kummerM_negZeros_eq`, `kummerM_posZeros_nonneg` | Kummer transformation and reflection of the zero set; nonnegative series coefficients, including the totalized `b = 0` case |
| 2 | `compact_support_L2_expansion`, `kernel_polynomials_orthogonal` | TauCeti polynomial completeness and Mathlib Hilbert-basis convergence; Christoffel–Darboux and orthogonality on the measure's support |
| 3 | `hermite_product_cosine_transform`, `hermite_product_sine_transform`, `hermitePolyH_convolution` | Finite Hermite linearization, Laguerre coefficients and single-polynomial transforms; integration by parts and Gaussian scaling |
| 4 | `regularized_parameter_integral`, `whittakerM_parameter_integral`, `whittakerM_euler` | Dominated termwise Beta integration; positive-real scaling of principal complex powers and affine Euler substitution |

The source admission count decreased from 347 to 337. The three repaired identities
above remain among the 337. Full builds, direct checks of the six changed Lean files,
and the compiled axiom audit validate the completed proofs without `sorryAx`.

## Further proofs and a statement correction (2026-10-08)

The project-wide review closed four further admissions without changing their
statements or hypotheses:

| Completed theorem | Proof ingredients |
| --- | --- |
| `tendsto_whittakerM_div_Gamma` | Joint continuity of regularized Kummer M, its proved denominator-pole formula, and a punctured affine parameter substitution |
| `qGegenbauer_limit_ultrasphPoly` | Finite `q`-Pochhammer ratio limits and Gegenbauer generating-series coefficients |
| `laguerrePolyL_fractional_integral` | Gamma normalization, the regularized Kummer parameter integral, and positive-real scaling by `y = x t` |
| `laguerrePolyL_fractional_tail_integral` | Translation by `x`, Laguerre addition, and the proved Laguerre moments; all terms except degree zero vanish |

Two supporting lemmas were added: `whittakerW_zero_half` and
`laguerrePolyL_div_Gamma_eq_regularized`. The source admission count decreased from
337 to 333. The full build, direct file checks, entry-module check, and compiled
axiom audits verify the new proofs and the previous priority proofs without
`sorryAx`.

The still-admitted `whittakerW_integral_besselK` had a concrete counterexample at
`κ = 0`, `μ = 1/2`, `z = 1`. The old parameter hypothesis holds, but its prefactor
contains the totalized `Γ(0) = 0`, so the right side is zero for any totalized value
of the integral. The new proved specialization gives
`W_{0,1/2}(1) = exp (-1/2) ≠ 0`; this counterexample was also checked in Lean.
The theorem now requires both
`0 < Re (μ - κ) + 1/2` and `0 < Re (-μ - κ) + 1/2`, the ordinary convergence region
`Re κ + |Re μ| < 1/2`. Although [DLMF 13.16.8](https://dlmf.nist.gov/13.16.E8)
prints only the first inequality, the omitted bound is required for this literal
Bochner-integral statement. The argument conditions are preserved, and a proof
of the corrected identity remains open.

The Gauss-kernel integral formulas were also checked against
[DLMF 13.4.8](https://dlmf.nist.gov/13.4.E8) and
[13.16.9](https://dlmf.nist.gov/13.16.E9). Their Gamma normalization is correct:
`regularizedGaussHG` already denotes the regularized Gauss function.

## Next priority proofs (2026-10-08)

Seven more admissions were closed with their original statements and hypotheses:

| Completed theorem | Proof ingredients |
| --- | --- |
| `jacobi_fractional_integral` | Gamma normalization, the proved regularized Gauss fractional integral, and the substitution `y = 1 - (1-x)t` |
| `jacobi_rodrigues` | Induction using the Jacobi weighted derivative; Mathlib's iterated derivative and real-scalar restriction APIs |
| `laguerrePolyL_rodrigues` | Induction using the Laguerre weighted derivative and the same restriction APIs |
| `legendre_orthogonality` | Proved Jacobi orthogonality for unequal degrees, followed by the three-term recurrence for the diagonal norms |
| `laguerrePolyL_unweighted_convolution` | Interval integration by parts and the Laguerre derivative recurrence; valid for every real endpoint, including negative ones |
| `laplace_regularizedHGFun_self` | Absolute summability of the integrated Kummer terms, Gamma moments, and the binomial series |
| `laplace_whittakerM_closed` | The self-transform and an explicit principal-logarithm identity for the quotient of two right-half-plane arguments |

Five supporting lemmas were added: `iteratedDeriv_jacobi_weight`,
`iteratedDeriv_laguerrePolyL_weight`, `integral_cpow_mul_sub_cpow`,
`kummerU_laplace_integrable`, and `kummerU_laplace_shift_one`.
The Tricomi kernel is now proved absolutely integrable throughout `Re a > 0`,
`Re z > 0`, for every complex `b`. Its identity with `U` is proved at `b = a + 1`;
the general representation remains admitted. These specializations are supporting
results, not additional closed admissions.

The source admission count decreased from 333 to 326. The full 3,988-job build,
direct checks of all three edited modules, entry-module check, and axiom audits
validate all seven proofs and five supporting lemmas. They use only the standard
axioms and do not depend on `sorryAx`. No theorem statements were changed in this pass.

## Open priority continuation (2026-10-08)

Three further admissions were closed without changing their statements or assumptions:

| Completed theorem | Proof ingredients |
| --- | --- |
| `laguerrePolyL_convolution` | Finite Gamma-normalized Laguerre expansions, scaled Beta moments, and Vandermonde convolution via a polynomial additive functional |
| `jacobi_norm` | Weighted integration by parts reduces the degree while shifting both parameters; the degree-zero Beta integral starts the induction |
| `ultrasphPoly_orthogonality` | Symmetric Jacobi orthogonality and norms, positive-real factorization of the weight, and Mathlib's Gamma duplication formula |

The Jacobi proof also establishes `jacobi_norm_all`, whose Gamma denominator is
regular even when the degree is zero. Weight and polynomial-product integrability
are explicit, so endpoint singularities remain covered by the original parameter
range. `ultrasphPoly_norm` includes degree zero. Finite Laguerre normalization and
scaled Beta integrability are reusable supporting lemmas.

The general Kummer Laplace identity is now proved as
`laplace_regularizedHGFun_of_norm_lt` for arbitrary complex `a, b, c, k, z` with
`Re b > 0`, `Re z > 0`, and `‖k‖ < Re z`, including singular denominator parameters.
It sums absolute integrated terms using a regularized Gauss majorant. The existing
self-transform now specializes this lemma. Tricomi's Laplace representation is
proved as `kummerU_laplace_shift_nat` for every `b = a + N + 1`, with nonnegative
integer `N`; its former `b = a + 1` helper is the degree-zero specialization.
These two extensions are supporting results, not additional closed admissions.

The source admission count decreased from 326 to 323. Thirteen supporting lemmas
were added, including two private normalization/summation helpers. The full project
build, direct checks of all three changed modules, `Main.lean`, and compiled axiom
audits verify the new proofs and both refactored special cases. They depend only
on Lean's standard axioms, with no dependency on an admitted theorem.

The two outstanding targets after that pass were:

- `laplace_regularizedHGFun`: extend the proved series-region transform to the full
  region `Re z > Re k`, `Re z > 0`. This includes cases with `|k/z| ≥ 1`. A Kummer
  growth bound on positive rays and holomorphic integral dependence would support
  continuation; a disk-only replacement would lose intended cases.
- `kummerU_laplace`: identify the absolutely convergent integral with the
  connection-formula definition of `U` for general complex `b`, including integer
  second parameters. The finite shifted family supplies checks and useful special
  cases, but agreement at discrete shifts alone does not prove general parameter
  continuation.

## Full half-plane Kummer transform (2026-10-08)

`laplace_regularizedHGFun` is now proved with its original hypotheses
`Re b > 0`, `Re z > Re k`, and `Re z > 0`. Its region includes transformed
arguments outside the unit disk, and arbitrary denominator parameters remain
covered. No statement or assumption was changed.

The new [Growth.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Growth.lean) starts with Euler's integral and
the all-parameter reciprocal-Gamma recurrence. Denominator lowering, followed by
integration of the Kummer derivative, proves a bound
`C * (1+t)^N * exp (max (0, Re k) * t)` for every positive ray and every complex
pair of parameters. Finite Gamma majorants then prove absolute convergence and
holomorphic dependence on `z` throughout the full half-plane. Analytic uniqueness
extends the earlier series-region transform, and an explicit argument calculation
keeps `k/z` on the Gauss cut plane.

For the remaining `kummerU_laplace` priority, the normalized integral is now
proved continuous in the second parameter. A fixed polynomial majorant controls
a neighborhood of every parameter. The proved lemma
`kummerU_laplace_of_eqOn_integerComplement` therefore reduces the general
representation to its connection-formula identification at noninteger second
parameters. That identification remains open; the reduction is a conditional
supporting result, not a proof of the admitted general representation.

The source admission count decreased from 323 to 322. Thirteen new supporting
lemmas and one private growth predicate were added. The full 3,989-job build,
direct checks of the three changed modules and `Main.lean`, and axiom audits
validate the completed transform and all eight new public supporting proofs.
They use only Lean's standard axioms. The compiled inventory contains 5,550
declarations and exactly 322 declarations dependent on `sorryAx`, matching the
322 explicit admissions without additional hidden admitted dependencies.

## Tricomi and Whittaker Laplace representations (2026-10-08)

`kummerU_laplace` and `whittakerW_laplace` are now proved with their original
hypotheses. Tricomi's formula covers `Re a > 0`, `Re z > 0`, and every complex
second parameter, including integers. Whittaker's formula retains
`Re κ < Re μ + 1/2` and `Re z > 0`. No statement or assumption was changed.

The new [Confluence.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Confluence.lean) proves Gauss-to-Kummer
confluence for a large real upper parameter with a fixed complex shift, including
totalized denominator parameters. In
[IntegralRepresentations.lean](DLMF/Confluent/IntegralRepresentations.lean), the
proved Gauss connection formula and Gamma-ratio asymptotic identify the limit
with Tricomi's defining connection formula. Euler's integral has the same limit:
a real Bernoulli inequality and TauCeti's second Beta integrability theorem give
a fixed integrable majorant for dominated convergence. Holomorphic uniqueness
extends the identity from a positive real ray to the full right half-plane;
the previously proved parameter continuity supplies integer second parameters.
The substitution `t = 1 + 2u` then proves the Whittaker W formula. The shifted
Tricomi special cases now directly specialize the general theorem.

The source admission count decreased from 322 to 320. Eleven supporting lemmas
were added, including eight private helpers. The full 3,990-job build, direct
checks of both changed modules and `Main.lean`, and transitive axiom audits
validate the new proofs and their upstream foundations. They depend only on
Lean's standard axioms. The compiled inventory contains 5,564 declarations and
exactly 320 declarations dependent on `sorryAx`, matching the explicit admissions
without additional hidden admitted dependencies.
This completes the outstanding integral priorities;
Tricomi Mellin and Laplace transforms are natural next uses of the new representation.

## Tricomi Mellin transform (2026-10-08)

`mellin_kummerU` is now proved with all three original hypotheses:
`Re (b - 1) < Re λ`, `0 < Re λ`, and `Re λ < Re a`. No statement or
assumption was changed. The new public lemma `mellin_kummerU_integrable`
also establishes absolute convergence throughout this strip.

The proof in [Integrals.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Integrals.lean) inserts the proved
Tricomi Laplace representation. A private helper proves absolute integrability
of the double kernel using a real Gamma moment and TauCeti's second Beta
integrability theorem, with positive parameters `Re (a - λ)` and
`Re (λ - b + 1)`. Mathlib's Fubini theorem then justifies exchanging integrals;
the project's proved complex Gamma and Beta formulas give the stated Gamma quotient.

The source admission count decreased from 320 to 319. Two supporting lemmas
were added, one private. The full 3,990-job build, direct checks of the changed
module and `Main.lean`, and the transitive axiom audit pass. Both new completed
proofs and their Gamma, Beta, and Tricomi foundations use only Lean's standard
axioms, without dependence on admitted results. The compiled inventory contains
5,568 declarations and exactly 319 declarations dependent on `sorryAx`, matching
the explicit admissions without additional hidden admitted dependencies.

## Gamma pass from Temme's Chapter 3 (2026-10-15)

Using the derivations and hints of N. M. Temme, *Special Functions* (Wiley, 1996),
Chapter 3, together with DLMF Chapter 5, 24 Gamma admissions were proved without
changing their hypotheses. The Gamma admission count decreased from 58 to 34, and
the source admission count from 319 to 295.

| File | Completed theorems | Proof ingredients |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralRepresentations` | `log_Gamma_binet_exp`, `log_Gamma_binet_arctan`, `log_Gamma_binet_log` | Integration of Binet's digamma formula, constant from Stirling along the integers; partial fractions of `coth` from Mathlib's `cot_series_rep'`; symmetric Laplace identities by Fubini |
| `IntegralRepresentations` | `digamma_coth`, `digamma_frullani`, `eulerMascheroni_integrals` | Termwise integration of the `coth` expansion; the substitution `u = eᵗ - 1`; explicit `ε`-truncations for Euler's constant |
| `IntegralRepresentations` | `Gamma_incomplete_sum`, `Gamma_truncated_exp` | Prym's decomposition via the recursion of `Γ` (no continuation needed); Cauchy–Saalschütz from Prym |
| `IntegralRepresentations` | `Gamma_cos`, `Gamma_sin`, `Gamma_cos_pow`, `Gamma_sin_pow` | Laplace representation of `t^{x-1}`, Fubini on `(0,R) × (0,∞)`, dominated convergence, and the Beta integral `∫ u^{b-1}/(1+u) = Γ(b)Γ(1-b)` |
| `IntegralRepresentations` | `iteratedDeriv_Gamma`, `integral_Gamma` | Differentiation under the integral with `|log t|ᵏ ≤ (t^{kε} + t^{-kε})/εᵏ`; fundamental theorem of calculus |
| `Products` | `Gamma_div_Gamma_add_mul_I_sq`, `Gamma_balanced_product` | Gauss's limit; multipliability from `exp Lₙ` with `Lₙ = O(1/n²)` |
| `Inequalities` | `Gamma_stirling_bounds`, `Gamma_ratio_bounds`, `Gamma_ratio_digamma_bounds`, `norm_Gamma_add_mul_I_ge` | Strict Binet bounds `0 < Φ(x) < 1/(12x)`; strict log-convexity; telescoping with logarithmic-mean inequalities; product comparison at `x = 1/2` |
| `Asymptotics`, `Polygamma` | `log_Gamma_stirling`, `digamma_stirling`, `trigamma_asymptotic`, `polygamma_asymptotic` | Bernoulli expansion of `β(t)/t` with remainder `2 Sₙ t^{2n}` (Temme (3.26)) integrated against `e^{-zt}` |

**Statement correction.** In `Gamma_incomplete_sum` (DLMF 5.9.4) the integral binder
extended over the following `+ ∑' k, …`, so the series, a constant in `t`, was
integrated over `(1, ∞)`. The integrand was then not integrable, Lean's integral
was zero, and the statement asserted `Γ(z) = 0`. The integral is now parenthesized;
the hypotheses are unchanged.

All 24 proofs depend only on `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`.
The remaining Gamma admissions are the Hankel-loop formula `recip_Gamma_binet`, the
complex Stirling statements of §5.11 (`Gamma_linear_argument`,
`norm_Gamma_imag_large`, `Gamma_ratio_expansion`, `Gamma_ratio_three`), four
inequalities (5.6.2, 5.6.3, 5.6.8, 5.6.9), Mellin–Barnes and multidimensional
integrals, the Barnes `G` theory, the `q`-Gamma limits, the zeta expansions of
`SeriesExpansions`, Gauss's digamma theorem `digamma_rat`, and three Beta integrals.

## Mathematical priorities

The most useful next steps are those that connect the existing proved theories
and discharge common analytic obligations.

- **Continue reviewing exceptional parameters.**
  Regularized Gamma and hypergeometric expressions should replace literal
  pole–zero products when the intended assertion includes a removable limit.
  Classical polynomial continuation likewise needs a deliberate definition.
- **Use Tricomi's Laplace representation.** Its Mellin transform is now proved;
  further Laplace transforms, positivity, and large-argument estimates remain.
  These require integrable majorants for exchanging integrals or passing to
  limits. The corresponding Whittaker \(W\) formulas can then reuse the defining
  transformation and the proved Laplace representation.
- **Connect classical polynomial families to the abstract orthogonality API.**
  The now-proved Jacobi norms and Rodrigues formulas, polynomial-valued
  realizations, and completeness on compact intervals would make the quadrature and root
  theory easier to apply. Several finite-sum and generating-function admissions
  are also accessible through the established coefficient methods.
- **Establish Gamma remainder and Mellin–Barnes tools.** Bounds for Gamma ratios,
  vertical-line decay, and justified contour or improper-integral operations
  would serve several subjects at once. The higher asymptotic assertions need
  quantified remainders, not just leading-order heuristics.
- **Finish the Bessel zero and addition theory.** The integer-order foundations of
  \(Y\) and \(K\) and the large-argument expansions are in place. Monotonicity of the
  first zero in the order needs a comparison argument or Watson's formula, Nicholson's
  integral needs a further integral representation, and Gegenbauer's addition theorem
  needs the Gegenbauer expansion of plane waves.
- **Treat the smaller subjects as substantive developments.** Favard and
  Verblunsky existence, general Selberg evaluation, spherical-harmonic dimension
  and addition theory, and the bilateral \(q\)-identities each require a coherent
  mathematical argument. Their small numbers of admissions do not indicate small
  amounts of missing theory.

For navigation, the subject import files and linked implementation files describe
the available objects, while the statements and their hypotheses determine the
precise scope of each theorem. The strongest reusable assets are the regularized
hypergeometric continuation machinery, the coefficient and analytic-uniqueness
methods for differential equations, the Gamma-weighted integration techniques,
and the measure-theoretic theory of orthogonal polynomials.

## Legendre and Chebyshev pass from Temme's Chapter 6 (2026-10-15)

Following Temme, *Special Functions*, §§6.8–6.9, the admitted Laplace integral
`legendre_laplace_integral` (DLMF 18.10.5) is now proved: the integrals
`∫₀^π (cos θ + i sin θ cos φ)ⁿ dφ` satisfy the Legendre recurrence, obtained by integrating the
exact derivative of `wⁿ⁺¹ sin φ`. The orthogonal-polynomial admission count decreased from 102
to 101, and the source admission count from 289 to 288.

New admission-free modules:

* [Legendre.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Legendre.lean): `|Pₙ(x)| ≤ 1` (Temme (6.52)),
  Temme's bound `|Pₙ(cos θ)| ≤ √(π/(2n))/sin θ` (6.53), the mean-square error identity for
  truncated Legendre expansions (6.60)–(6.61) with optimality of the Legendre coefficients and
  Bessel's inequality (6.62), and the Laplace–Heine formula
  `√n Pₙ(cos θ) - √(2/(π sin θ)) cos((n + 1/2)θ - π/4) → 0` (§6.8.4, leading term).
* [ChebyshevExpansion.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/ChebyshevExpansion.lean): Chebyshev
  coefficients (6.66) and their weighted-integral form, convergence of Chebyshev series with
  absolutely summable coefficients (via Mathlib's Fourier series on `AddCircle`), the decay
  `|cₙ| ≤ 2Mρ⁻ⁿ` for functions analytic in a Bernstein ellipse (contour shift with Cauchy's
  theorem for rectangles), the uniform truncation error, Clenshaw's algorithm, and Temme's
  Example 6.3 (the expansion of `1/(x+a)`).

All new theorems depend only on `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. Not formalized:
the Dirichlet–Mehler formula (still admitted as `legendre_dirichlet_mehler`), Stieltjes' full
asymptotic expansion, and Bauer's expansion (6.64).

## Connection formula 13.2.41 and Wronskians (2026-10-16)

Six confluent admissions were proved, reducing that count from 109 to 103 and the source
admission count from 286 to 280. No statement was changed. Five Wronskian statements moved
unchanged from `BasicProperties.lean` to the new
[ConnectionWronskians.lean](DLMF/Confluent/ConnectionWronskians.lean), because they use the
derivative of `U` from `Recurrence.lean`. Unused hypotheses are kept with underscore names.

| Theorem | DLMF | Proof route |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `kummerM_eq_kummerU_connection` | 13.2.41 | For noninteger `b`: 13.2.42 for both `U` functions, Kummer's transformation 13.2.39, `(-z)^{1-b} = z^{1-b} e^{±πi(1-b)}`, and the totalized reflection formula `(Γ(s)Γ(1-s))⁻¹ = sin(πs)/π`, which holds for every `a`. Continuity in `b` of `𝐌` and `U` gives the regularized form `regularizedHGFun_eq_kummerU_connection` for every `b`. |
| `wronskian_kummerU_kummerUReflected` | 13.2.38 | Off the real axis, `𝐌 = c₁U + c₂F` near `z` with `F(w) = e^w U(b-a,b,-w)`, so `c₂ W{U,F} = -W{𝐌,U}`; continuity in `a` covers the poles of `Γ(a)` |
| `wronskian_regularizedHGFun_kummerUReflected` | 13.2.35 | `W{𝐌,F} = c₁ W{U,F}` |
| `wronskian_shifted_regularized_kummerUReflected` | 13.2.37 | 13.2.41 at `(a-b+1, 2-b)` with Kummer's transformation 13.2.40 for both `U` functions |
| `wronskian_kummerU_kummer_neg`, `wronskian_kummerU_kummer_pos` | 13.2.24, 13.2.25 | Nonvanishing of 13.2.38; `z` and `-z` in the slit plane force `z ∉ ℝ` |

Applications to Coulomb functions are in the new admission-free module
[CoulombIrregular.lean](DLMF/Confluent/CoulombIrregular.lean). `conj_kummerU` shows that `U`
commutes with complex conjugation on the slit plane, by continuity in `b` at integer `b`. Hence
`H^-_ℓ = conj H^+_ℓ`. Applied at `z = 2iρ`, 13.2.41 gives `F_ℓ = (H^+_ℓ - H^-_ℓ)/(2i)`. With
`G_ℓ := Re H^+_ℓ` (DLMF 33.2.11 defines `G_ℓ` this way), this proves `H^±_ℓ = G_ℓ ± iF_ℓ`. It also
proves the Wronskians `W{G_ℓ,F_ℓ} = W{H^±_ℓ,F_ℓ} = 1` (33.2.12), by reduction to `W{𝐌,U}`, and
the limits `H^+_ℓ - e^{iθ_ℓ} → 0`, `F_ℓ - sin θ_ℓ → 0`, `G_ℓ - cos θ_ℓ → 0` (33.10.1–33.10.2).
All are for `ρ > 0`. The phase `σ_ℓ` uses the principal argument; DLMF's continuous branch
differs by a multiple of `2π` and gives the same functions.

New admission-free results for Temme's Chapter 5:
* [Generalized.lean](DLMF/Hypergeometric/Generalized.lean): the generalized hypergeometric
  differential equation `θ ∏(θ+bⱼ-1) w = z ∏(θ+aᵢ) w` (DLMF 16.8.3, Temme (5.30)) for Mathlib's
  regularized `ₚFq`, on the disc of convergence and for all parameters.
* [LoopIntegral.lean](DLMF/Hypergeometric/LoopIntegral.lean): the loop integral 15.6.2 over an
  explicit loop of radius `ρ` about `1`, for `re b > 0`, `c - b ∉ {1,2,…}` and `|z|(1+ρ) < 1`.
  Independence of `ρ` comes from Cauchy's theorem for a rectangle in `w = log(t-1)`. The limit
  `ρ → 0` gives Euler's integral for `re(c-b) > 0`, and continuation in `c` covers the remaining
  `c`.

## Bessel functions from Temme, Chapter 9 (2026-10-16)

Three admissions were proved, reducing the source admission count from 266 to 263 (confluent
91 → 89, Bessel 4 → 3): Kummer's Bessel-K relation 13.6.10 (`kummerU_besselK`), the
Whittaker form 13.18.9 (`whittakerW_besselK`), and infinitely many positive zeros of
\(J_\nu\) (`besselJ_infinite_zeros`). The last statement moved unchanged from `Theorems.lean`
to [Asymptotics.lean](DLMF/Bessel/Asymptotics.lean); its hypothesis `-1 < ν` is unused and is
kept with an underscore name.

New admission-free modules:
* [SecondKind.lean](DLMF/Bessel/SecondKind.lean): joint continuity of `₀F̃₁` in its parameter and
  of `J_ν(z)` in `(ν, z)`. At integer `n`, `Y_μ = dslope D n μ / dslope (sin(·π)) n μ` with
  `D_μ = J_μ cos μπ - J_{-μ}`, so the parameter limits defining `Y_n` and `K_n` exist and are
  jointly continuous. The recurrences, derivatives and differential equations of `Y` and `K`
  (10.2.1, 10.6.1–2, 10.25.1, 10.29.1–2), `I_{-n} = I_n` (10.27.1), the Hankel functions
  (10.4.3), and the Wronskians 10.5.1, 10.5.2, 10.5.5, 10.28.1 and 10.28.2 hold for all orders. The
  Wronskians come from the entire series `F(ν+1)F(-ν) - wF(ν+2)F(1-ν)`, which is constant by the
  contiguous relation.
* `MathlibBridge.lean` (removed 2026-10-16, when the project switched to Mathlib's
  `Complex.besselJ`): `besselJ = Complex.besselJ`, and Mathlib's parity (10.11.1), entireness at
  integer order and value at `0`.
* [BesselIntegral.lean](DLMF/Bessel/BesselIntegral.lean): the Jacobi–Anger expansion (10.12.1)
  and Bessel's integrals 10.9.1–10.9.2, by Fourier extraction from the generating function.
* [KIntegrals.lean](DLMF/Bessel/KIntegrals.lean): `K_ν(z) = √π (z/2)^ν/Γ(ν+½) ∫₁^∞ e^{-zu}(u²-1)^{ν-½} du`
  (10.32.8), from 13.6.10 and the Laplace integral of `U`.
* [Asymptotics.lean](DLMF/Bessel/Asymptotics.lean): Hankel's expansion of `K_ν` (10.40.2) in every
  sector `|arg z| ≤ π - ε`, from 13.6.10 and Tricomi's rotated-sector expansion. The connection
  `H^{(1)}_ν(z) = (2/(πi)) e^{-iνπ/2} K_ν(-iz)` (10.27.8) for `-π/2 < arg z < π`, and
  `J_ν = (πi)⁻¹(e^{-iνπ/2}K_ν(-iz) - e^{iνπ/2}K_ν(iz))` for `re z > 0`. These give Hankel's expansion
  of `H^{(1)}_ν` on the positive axis (10.17.5) and
  `J_ν(x) = √(2/(πx)) cos(x - νπ/2 - π/4) + O(x^{-3/2})` for every complex `ν` (10.7.8). For real
  `ν`, `J_ν` is real on `(0,∞)` and changes sign on each interval `[x_k, x_k+π]` with
  `x_k = νπ/2 + π/4 + 2kπ`, so it has infinitely many positive zeros for every real order. Rolle's
  theorem with `(z^{-ν}J_ν)' = -z^{-ν}J_{ν+1}` and `(z^{ν+1}J_{ν+1})' = z^{ν+1}J_ν` puts a zero of
  each of `J_ν` and `J_{ν+1}` between two positive zeros of the other (10.21.3).
* [Lommel.lean](DLMF/Bessel/Lommel.lean): Lommel's integral
  `∫₀¹ t J_ν(αt)J_ν(βt) dt = (βJ_ν(α)J_{ν-1}(β) - αJ_{ν-1}(α)J_ν(β))/(α²-β²)` for `re ν > -1`,
  `α ≠ β` positive (10.22.5). For `-1 < re ν ≤ 0` the two products in the primitive cancel to
  leading order at `t = 0`. The corollary is the orthogonality of `J_ν(jt)` over distinct positive
  zeros (10.22.37).

## Legendre, statistical and elliptic functions from Temme, Chapters 8, 11 and 12 (2026-10-16)

Three new admission-free areas were added; the source admission count is unchanged at 263. The
declaration counts in the inventory table for these rows are from 2026-10-16.

[Legendre](DLMF/Legendre.lean) (Temme Ch. 8, DLMF 14). Mathlib has no Legendre functions.
`legendreP ν z = 𝐅(-ν, ν+1; 1; (1-z)/2)` and
`legendreQ ν z = √π Γ(ν+1) (2z)^{-ν-1} 𝐅((ν+2)/2, (ν+1)/2; ν+3/2; 1/z²)` use the
continuation `regularizedGaussHG` of `₂F₁` to the cut plane, so they are analytic off `(-∞,-1]` and
`(-∞,1]` for every complex `ν`. `ferrersP ν μ x` is the Ferrers function on `(-1,1)` (14.3.1).
Proved:
* Legendre's equation for `P_ν` and `Q_ν`, and the associated equation for `𝖯^μ_ν` (14.2.1–2).
  These come from the hypergeometric equation on the cut plane, which is the hypothesis-free
  diagonal contiguous relation times `ab`.
* `P_ν(1) = 1`, `P_{-ν-1} = P_ν` (14.9.5), `P_n = legendrePolyP n`, `P_ν(0)` (14.5.1, by Gauss's second
  summation theorem).
* The recurrence 14.10.3 and `(1-z²)P_ν' = ν(P_{ν-1} - zP_ν)`, as combinations of contiguous
  relations.
* `Q₀(z) = ½ log((z+1)/(z-1))`, from 15.4.2 near `z = 2` and the identity principle.
* `𝖯^m_ν = (-1)ᵐ(1-x²)^{m/2} P_ν⁽ᵐ⁾` (14.6.1), via 15.2.3_5 lifted to the cut plane.
* `P_ν(z) = z^ν 𝐅(-ν/2, (1-ν)/2; 1; 1-1/z²)` on `Re z > 0` (quadratic transformation 15.8.18 and
  Pfaff), and Laplace's first integral `P_ν(x) = π⁻¹∫₀^π (x + √(x²-1) cos φ)^ν dφ` for `x ≥ 1`,
  by termwise integration of the binomial series.
Later the same day:
* The recurrence `(ν+1)Q_{ν+1} - (2ν+1)zQ_ν + νQ_{ν-1} = 0` for `ν ≠ 0`, from a three-term identity
  for `𝐅(a,b;a+b;u)` proved from the series, and `Q₁ = zQ₀ - 1`.
* `Q_n = P_nQ₀ - R_n` with polynomials `R_n` (Temme (8.25)).
* The Wronskian `𝒲{P_n,Q_n} = 1/(1-z²)`: `(1-z²)𝒲` is constant by Legendre's equation and tends
  to `P_n(1)² = 1`.
* Neumann's integral `Q_n(z) = ½∫₋₁¹ P_n(t)/(z-t) dt` (Temme (8.33)).

Not done: `𝒲{P_ν,Q_ν}` for non-integer `ν` (it needs the connection formula at infinity, Temme
(8.15)), Heine's integral for `Q_ν`, and the Ferrers function of the second kind.

[Statistical](DLMF/Statistical.lean) (Temme Ch. 11, DLMF 8). TauCeti already has the real `erf`,
the real regularized incomplete Gamma and Beta functions and closed-form CDFs; this area adds:
* Complex `P(a,z)`, `Q(a,z)` with `P + Q = 1` (8.2.5), the recurrences 8.8.1 and 8.8.5–6, the
  Poisson sums 8.4.10–11, the series 8.7.1, the derivative 8.8.13 and bridges to TauCeti.
* `B_x(a,b)` and `I_x(a,b)` for complex parameters with positive real part: symmetry 8.17.4, the
  recurrences 8.17.20–21, `I_x(a,1) = x^a`, the binomial sum 8.17.5 and the bridge to TauCeti.
* Temme's noncentral chi-squared functions `P_μ(x,y)`, `Q_μ(x,y)` as Poisson mixtures. Proved are
  `P_μ + Q_μ = 1`, the Bessel recurrences (11.64)–(11.65), and the integral representation (11.63)
  with `I_{μ-1}(2√(xt))`.
Later the same day:
* The hypergeometric form 8.17.7.
* Tricomi's `γ*(a,z)` (8.2.6–7, 8.7.1, 8.4.12).
* The continued fraction 8.9.2 for `Γ(a,x)`, for real `a < 1` and `x > 0`. It comes from a general
  convergence theorem for continued fractions with positive elements and a positive backward solution
  (`posContFract_tendsto`, Pincherle and Seidel–Stern), with the backward solution given by tail
  integrals of `t^m (1+t)^σ e^{-xt}`.
Not done: 8.9.2 for complex `a`, and Temme's uniform asymptotic expansions. `posContFract_tendsto`
could serve the admitted continued fractions in Confluent and Hypergeometric once positive backward
solutions are supplied.

[Elliptic](DLMF/Elliptic.lean) (Temme Ch. 12, DLMF 19, 20, 22), for a real parameter `m = k² < 1`:
* `K(m)`, `E(m)`, their derivatives (19.4.1–2), Legendre's relation (19.7.1), the hypergeometric
  forms (19.5.1–2) and agreement with the existing `compEllIntKkSeries`.
* Gauss's theorem `K(m) = π/(2M(1, √(1-m)))` via the invariance of `∫₀^∞((t²+a²)(t²+b²))^{-1/2}`
  under the AGM step (Temme Thm 12.1). It uses Mathlib's `NNReal.agm`, with the descending and
  ascending Landen transformations of `K`.
* `F(φ|m)`, `E(φ|m)` with quasi-periodicity. The amplitude is the inverse of `F` (an order
  isomorphism of `ℝ`). For `sn`, `cn`, `dn`: 22.6.1, the derivatives 22.13.1–3, half-period and
  period relations, parity and the `m = 0` limit.
* `θ₁, …, θ₄` in DLMF normalization from Mathlib's `jacobiTheta₂`, with series forms 20.2.3–4,
  periodicity, parity and `θ₃(0|-1/τ) = (-iτ)^{1/2} θ₃(0|τ)`.
Later the same day:
* `Π(φ, n, m)` for `n, m < 1` (19.2.7): the derivative, oddness and quasi-periodicity, and the
  closed form at `m = 0` with `Π(n, 0) = π/(2√(1-n))`.
* The descending and ascending Landen transformations of `E` (Temme (12.13)–(12.14)), and `dK/dk`,
  `dE/dk` (19.4.1–2).
* Jacobi's `K = (π/2) θ₃(q)²` with `k' = θ₄²/θ₃²` (20.9.2). It follows from the doubling identities
  `θ₃² + θ₄² = 2θ₃(q²)²` and `θ₃θ₄ = θ₄(q²)²`, so that `M(θ₃², θ₄²) = 1`.
Not done: `∂F/∂m`, complex modulus, and `sn` through theta quotients.

## Separation of the wave equation, Temme Chapter 10, and follow-ups (2026-10-16)

No admission changed (263). New admission-free module
[WaveEquation/Helmholtz.lean](DLMF/WaveEquation/Helmholtz.lean). The separated solutions with
integer azimuthal order are written in Cartesian form, `(x+iy)ⁿ ₀F̃₁(n + d/2; -k²r²/4)`. These are
entire, so the Helmholtz equation `Δu + k²u = 0` holds at every point, with `Δ` written through
iterated one-variable derivatives (`laplacian2`, `laplacian3`).
* Cylinder waves solve it on `ℝ²`, and with the axial factor `e^{iβz}` the equation
  `Δu + (κ²+β²)u = 0` holds on `ℝ³`. In polar coordinates they are `(2/k)ⁿ J_n(kr) e^{inφ}`.
* Spherical waves solve it on `ℝ³`. In spherical coordinates they are
  `(r sin θ)ⁿ e^{inφ} (kr/2)^{-(n+1/2)} J_{n+1/2}(kr)`.
* The heat modes `J₀(jr) e^{-j²t}` of the cooled cylinder (Temme §10.3.1) solve
  `u_rr + u_r/r = u_t`, and vanish at `r = 1` when `J₀(j) = 0`.
Every computation reduces to the contiguous relation `₀F̃₁(c) = c ₀F̃₁(c+1) + w ₀F̃₁(c+2)`. The
general coordinate formulas of Temme §10.1 and the Mathieu, Weber and Lamé separations are not
formalized.

Follow-ups:
* [Lommel.lean](DLMF/Bessel/Lommel.lean) now has the Fourier–Bessel normalization
  `∫₀¹ t J_ν(jt)² dt = J_{ν+1}(j)²/2` for `re ν > -1` (10.22.37, `ℓ = m`). The proof uses a primitive
  whose `ν²` terms cancel, so that it vanishes at `0⁺`. With the existing orthogonality this gives
  the coefficient formula for finite Fourier–Bessel sums, which supplies the coefficients in Temme's
  heat-conduction series.
* [IncompleteBeta.lean](DLMF/Statistical/IncompleteBeta.lean) now has the hypergeometric form 8.17.7.

## Duverney, *An Introduction to Hypergeometric Functions* (2026-10-16)

A coverage review of Duverney (Birkhäuser 2024) against Mathlib, TauCeti and this project found
Chapters 1, 4, 6, 7 and 9 almost fully covered. The new material lay in Chapter 8
(polylogarithms, constants, BBP formulas), Chapter 10 (q-series), §§2.2–2.5, and the general
`ₚF_q` theory of Chapter 3. Tiers 1–2 of the resulting priority list were implemented without new
admissions; the admission count stays at 262.

* [Polylog](DLMF/Polylog.lean) (new):
  * `Li_n` as a power series on the closed disc: its derivative, the values at `±1`, duplication,
    and the link to Mathlib's `expZeta` on the circle.
  * The real `Li₂` and `Li₃` as integrals: Euler reflection and `Li₂(½)` (25.12.6); Landen and
    inversion for `Li₂`; the golden-ratio values; Landen's identity for `Li₃`; and
    `Li₃(½) = (7/8)ζ(3) - (π²/12)ln 2 + (ln 2)³/6`.
* [Constants](DLMF/Constants.lean) (new):
  * The original BBP formula for `π`, and BBP-type formulas for `ln 2`, `ln 3`, `ln 5`.
  * Gauss's Machin formula.
  * `Σ(-1)^{k+1}/k = ln 2`, which is absent from Mathlib.
  * Dirichlet `η` and `λ`, with `η(1) = ln 2` and `ζ(s) → 1`.
  * Catalan's constant, with `ψ'(¼) = π² + 8G` and `ψ'(¾) = π² - 8G`.
* [QSeries](DLMF/QSeries.lean):
  * Gaussian binomials and Cauchy's finite `q`-binomial theorem.
  * The `q`-Gauss and `q`-Chu–Vandermonde sums.
  * Heine's second and third transformations.
  * Euler's two `q`-exponential identities.
  * The `q`-derivative and both halves of the `q`-fundamental theorem for the Jackson integral.
* [Hypergeometric](DLMF/Hypergeometric.lean):
  * Pfaff–Saalschütz (16.4.3).
  * The `ₚF_q` derivative (16.3.1) and uniqueness of power-series solutions of its equation.
  * Clausen's formula (16.12.2), with `arcsin²` and `Σ1/(n²C(2n,n)) = π²/18`.
  * The general Euler–Pochhammer integral (16.5.2).
  * `Si = x·₁F₂`, and Duverney Ex. 4.10.
* [Gamma](DLMF/Gamma.lean) and ToMathlib:
  * Rational series `Σ P(n)/Q(n)` via `ψ`, and their alternating versions.
  * `ψ⁽ⁿ⁾(a) = (-1)^{n+1}n! ζ(n+1,a)` (25.11.12), with `ζ(n+1,½)` and the Hurwitz Laplace integral.
  * The `tan`, `csc`, `sec` partial fractions, and the Bernoulli Taylor series of `x cot x`,
    `tan x`, `x/sin x` and `z/(e^z-1)`.
  * Euler numbers: the Bernoulli relation, `β(2k+1)`, and the `sech` and `sec` generating
    functions on `|z| < 1/2`.
* [Elliptic](DLMF/Elliptic.lean):
  * The addition formulas for `sn`, `cn`, `dn`, and their first- and second-order equations.
  * Shifts by `K` and the values at `K/2`, and the pendulum.
  * `arcsn` and the algebraic integral form, with `K(½) = Γ(¼)²/(4√π)` and `E(½)`.
  * The lemniscate integral and the ellipse perimeter as a `₂F₁`.
* [Bessel](DLMF/Bessel/WeberSeries.lean):
  * `Y_{-n} = (-1)ⁿY_n`, and `Y_n` via `∂_ν J_ν`.
  * The series for `Y_n` and `Y₀` (10.8.1–2) and the product `J_μJ_ν` (10.8.3).
  * The cosine and sine Jacobi–Anger series.
  * Every solution of Bessel's equation is `A J_ν + B Y_ν`.

Not done:
* q-Pfaff–Saalschütz, the `q`-Leibniz rule, and Jackson's `₂φ₁ → ₂φ₂`.
* `∫₀^{π/4} t cot t`, which needs the Clausen function.
* General confluence for `ₚF_q` (`dixon_sum` was proved later via Carlson's theorem).
* `digamma_rat` and the asymptotics of the Bernoulli numbers.
* The limits of `sn` as `m → 0` and `m → 1`.
* The integral representations of `Y`.
* Duverney Tier 3: analytic ODE solutions, stationary phase, Airy and Struve, Lerch.

## Admission pass after the Temme and Duverney work (2026-10-16)

A review of all 262 admissions against the new material found about 40 that could now be proved.
Twenty-eight were proved, reducing the source admission count from 262 to 234.

* [Confluent](DLMF/Confluent.lean), 20:
  * The addition theorems 13.13.2 and 13.13.6 for every `x` in `slitPlane`, from the Taylor
    expansion of `(w/x)^{b-1}[e^{-w}]M(a,b,w)`, and the Whittaker additions 13.26.1, 13.26.2,
    13.26.4, 13.26.5, 13.26.7, 13.26.8, 13.26.10, 13.26.11.
  * `∫₀^∞ e^{-t} t^{a-1} ₀F̃₁(;c;wt) dt = Γ(a) 𝐌(a,c,w)`, giving 13.4.3, 13.16.3, 13.16.4.
  * The Mellin transform of `W` (13.23.5) and the Laplace transform of `M` (13.23.1).
  * `U` near `0` for `1 < re b < 2` and `0 < re b < 1` (13.2.18, 13.2.20).
  * No positive zeros of `U` for `a ≥ 0` (13.9.13), exactly one positive zero of `M` for `a > 0`,
    `-1 < b < 0` (13.9.3), and `M(a,b,z)` for large `b` (13.8.1).
* [Gamma](DLMF/Gamma.lean), 6:
  * Barnes' first lemma (5.13.3) and `Σ ψ'(k+2)/(k+1) = ζ(3)` (5.16.2).
  * The Glaisher–Kinkelin constant in terms of `ζ'(-1)`, and `Γ(az+b)` for large `z` (5.11.7).
  * The Dirichlet simplex integrals 5.14.1–2, from the multivariate Beta integral of lean-codes
    (see CREDITS).
* [QSeries](DLMF/QSeries.lean), 2: the Jacobi triple product (10.4.1), from the finite Rothe
  identity and Tannery's theorem, and `θ(τ) = ∏(1-q^{2n})(1+q^{2n-1})²`.

Statement repair: `kummerU_negZeros_none` (13.9.15) was false when `a` is a nonpositive integer
(`a = b = -1` gives `U = x + 1`, which vanishes at `x = -1`). It now assumes `a ∉ -ℕ` and stays
admitted.

## Namespace pass (2026-10-16)

Every project declaration then lived under `AAR` (renamed `DLMF` on 2026-10-21; the names below
are given in the current form). The files that were in the root namespace moved into
their area's namespace:
* `DLMF.QSeries` (`QSeries/Pochhammer`, `QSeries/Identities`), `DLMF.Bailey`, `DLMF.Partitions`.
* `DLMF.Selberg`, `DLMF.SphericalHarmonics`.
* `DLMF.OrthogonalPolynomials` (`Classical`, `Topics`, `General`, including `IsOrthogonalPolynomials`).
* `DLMF.Hypergeometric` (`Basic`, `Contiguous`, `DiffEq`, `EulerIntegral`, `Series`,
  `Transformations`, and `gauss_quadratic`, `kummer_quadratic`).
* `DLMF.Confluent` (`Basic`, and the Whittaker definitions), `DLMF.Bessel` (nine files).

Two name collisions surfaced and were resolved by renaming: `ultrasphPoly_param_one_eq` (the
`λ = 1` case in `Classical`) and `cpow_mul_cpow_of_re_pos` (in `Elementary`). The only remaining
declarations outside `AAR` deliberately extend Mathlib namespaces (`Real.*`, `Complex.*` in the
Gamma area, `HasFPowerSeriesOnBall.*`, `HasSum.*`, `MeasureTheory.*`, and
`tendsto_ordinaryHypergeometric_nhdsWithin_one`); these are candidates for `ToMathlib`.

## Bessel `J` from Mathlib (2026-10-16)

The project's own `besselJ` was the same function as Mathlib's `Complex.besselJ` on all of `ℂ × ℂ`
(same regularized `₀F₁`, same principal branch, same values at `0` and at negative integer
orders), so it was removed and `Complex.besselJ` is used directly. `besselJ_eq_regularized`
(DLMF 10.2.2) gives the series form `(z/2)^ν ₀F̃₁(;ν+1;-z²/4)` used by the proofs that unfolded
the old definition. `MathlibBridge.lean` and the local `analyticAt_besselJ` were removed as
duplicates of Mathlib; `besselJ_neg_int` is now Mathlib's lemma, restated to keep its DLMF tag.

## Orthogonal-polynomial admissions (2026-10-16)

Six admissions were proved, reducing the source admission count from 234 to 228.
* The Mellin transforms of Jacobi, Legendre (even and odd) and Gegenbauer polynomials
  (18.17.36–39), by termwise Beta integrals and the Pfaff–Saalschütz sum; the Gegenbauer case
  reduces to Jacobi via `s = 1 - 2x²` and Legendre duplication.
* The Gegenbauer–Bessel generating function (18.12.6), from Laplace's and Poisson's integrals. The
  statement moved unchanged from `Series.lean` to `Legendre.lean`, beside the Legendre case,
  because its ingredients are downstream of `Series.lean`.
* The Morse oscillator equation for `morsePolynomialFunction` (18.34.7), from the Bessel-polynomial
  differential equation.

The six Fourier and Laplace transforms 18.17.16, 18.17.16_5, 18.17.17–19 and 18.17.33 were then
proved, reducing the count from 228 to 222. No integration by parts was needed: the Jacobi
moments follow from the Mellin transform 18.17.36, termwise integration of the exponential gives
`zⁿ M(β+n+1, α+β+2n+2, -2z)`, Kummer's transformation gives the Fourier form, and the
Gegenbauer, cosine, sine and Legendre cases follow through `besselJ_eq_kummerM`, Legendre
duplication and parity.

## q-Pfaff–Saalschütz and the Bailey chain (2026-10-16)

The new module [QSeries/Saalschutz.lean](DLMF/QSeries/Saalschutz.lean) proves the q-Pfaff–Saalschütz
sum as a finite identity valid for every `q` (`sum_qBinom_saalschutz`, by induction with an
explicit telescoping certificate), with the quotient form `sum_saalschutz_div`. From it,
[Bailey.lean](DLMF/Bailey.lean) is now admission-free, reducing the count from 222 to 218:
* Bailey's lemma, by interchanging the finite double sum.
* Watson's `₈φ₇ → ₄φ₃` transformation, from the unit Bailey pair and two Bailey steps, with the
  degenerate values `aq^j = 1` covered by continuity in `a`.
* Both Rogers–Ramanujan identities, from the limiting Bailey relation (`ρ₁, ρ₂ → ∞`), Tannery's
  theorem, and the Jacobi triple product at base `q⁵`.

The three copies of `(a;q)_{m+k} = (a;q)_m (aq^m;q)_k` were merged into
`DLMF.QSeries.qPochhammer_add` in `QSeries/Pochhammer.lean`.

## Dixon and Dougall via Carlson's theorem (2026-10-16)

Carlson's theorem was copied unchanged from lean-codes to
[ToMathlib/Analysis/Complex/Carlson.lean](ToMathlib/Analysis/Complex/Carlson.lean).
With it, `dixon_sum` and `dougall_bilateral` (15.4.E25) in
[DLMF/Hypergeometric/Identities.lean](DLMF/Hypergeometric/Identities.lean) are proved
(218 → 216 admissions). Term-by-term bounds give only type π on vertical lines, so both
proofs use periodicity:

* Dougall: the sum `L(d)` satisfies `(d−a)(d−b)L(d+1) = (c+d−a−b−1)L(d)`. After
  normalization by Γ factors it is 1-periodic, of type π/2 on a strip, and correct at
  integer `d` by Gauss's sum. Carlson and the identity theorem give the result.
* Dixon: a WZ certificate gives a first-order recurrence in `c`. The normalized series
  is 1-periodic and correct at `c = −j` (the terminating case). Carlson and the
  recurrence give the result.

The helpers (`CarlsonAux`, `DougallAux`, `DixonAux`, about 1600 lines) include a periodic
form of Carlson's theorem and Γ-ratio and reciprocal-Γ bounds on strips. These are
ToMathlib candidates. `whipple_7F6` remains admitted.

## Gamma and digamma admissions (2026-10-17)

Twelve Gamma admissions were proved (216 → 204 project-wide; 27 → 15 in `DLMF/Gamma`).

* [BarnesG.lean](DLMF/Gamma/BarnesG.lean): the functional equation 5.17.1, integer values
  5.17.2, and the integral formula 5.17.4. The product converges for all `z`; the leftover
  factor of the shift identity is identified via `GammaSeq` and Stirling. 5.17.5 remains.
* [BetaFunction.lean](DLMF/Gamma/BetaFunction.lean): 5.12.5 and 5.12.6, by the binomial
  series of `(1 + r e^{2ix})^{a-1}`, termwise integration, `r → 1⁻`, and a Beta integral;
  the range `|b| ≤ a - 1` by an integration-by-parts recurrence. 5.12.8 remains.
* [SeriesExpansions.lean](DLMF/Gamma/SeriesExpansions.lean): 5.7.2 by coefficient comparison
  in `(1/Γ)' = -ψ/Γ`, and 5.7.3 by `exp R = Γ(1+·)` with `|Im R| < π`. The file is
  admission-free.
* [SpecialValues.lean](DLMF/Gamma/SpecialValues.lean): Gauss's digamma theorem 5.4.19, by a
  roots-of-unity filter on the digamma series, the Dirichlet test, and Abel's theorem.
* [QGamma.lean](DLMF/Gamma/QGamma.lean): `Γ_q(x) → Γ(x)` (5.18.10), by a concavity sandwich
  against `GammaSeq`. The monotonicity statements 5.18.8–9 remain.
* New [ComplexBinet.lean](DLMF/Gamma/ComplexBinet.lean): Binet's function in Stieltjes form,
  holomorphic on the slit plane, with `Γ = exp((z-½)log z - z + ½ log 2π + μ)` and
  `|μ(z)| ≤ 1/(6(|z| + Re z))`. This gives 5.6.9 and 5.11.9; with Watson's lemma it gives
  the shifted Stirling expansion (5.11.8 for complex shifts) and the ratio expansion 5.11.13.

Unused hypotheses (statements kept): `habs` in 5.6.9, `x`/`hx` in 5.11.13 and 5.11.19, and
`n` in 5.17.5.

Still admitted (15): the inequalities 5.6.2, 5.6.3, 5.6.8; `recip_Gamma_binet` (Hankel loop);
5.11.19; 5.12.8; Mellin–Barnes 5.13.2, 5.13.4, 5.13.5; the Selberg-type 5.14.5–7 (the
first two follow from the still-admitted Selberg integral); 5.17.5; 5.18.8–9.

## Remaining Gamma admissions, second round (2026-10-17)

Unused hypotheses were dropped from 5.6.9 (`habs`), 5.11.13 and 5.11.19 (`x`, `hx`) and
5.17.5 (`x`, `hx`, `n`; now stated in `t` directly). Twelve further admissions were proved
(204 → 192 project-wide; `DLMF/Gamma` 15 → 5; `DLMF/Selberg/Basic.lean` is admission-free).

* Inequalities 5.6.3 (then 5.6.2 by QM ≥ AM): Taylor lower bounds for `log Γ(1±t)` from
  trigamma monotonicity, `ζ(3) ≤ 4/3` and `1/2 < γ < 3/5`, closed by a polynomial
  inequality; `x ≥ 2` is trivial.
* `recip_Gamma_binet` (5.9.2_5) without contours: on the steepest-descent path an
  integration-by-parts recursion and a scaling identity give `W(a) = a W(a+1)`; the
  periodic `W·Γ` is fixed as `π` by Laplace's method and Stirling.
* 5.11.19: a Beta-integral form of the remainder with a binomial-remainder bound for
  `Re(c-a) > 0`, then an exact shift identity in `a`.
* 5.13.2: the Mellin–Barnes Beta integral, continued in `v` to `e^{i(π/2-b)}`. 5.13.4
  (Ramanujan): Fourier/Parseval with the continued 5.12.5 kernel for real parameters, then
  continuation in each parameter with locally uniform tail majorants.
* 5.17.5: `log A` from an Euler–Maclaurin continuation of `ζ` to `Re s > -2`, plus a
  Binet bound and an affine-function argument for real `t`.
* 5.18.8–9: strict concavity of `log(1-p^t) - log(1-q^t)` (sinh monotonicity) applied to
  the product truncations.
* Selberg integral and Aomoto's integral (Selberg/Basic.lean): Aomoto's recurrence,
  Carlson in `a`, a dimension recursion fixing the constant, and continuation in `c`.
  5.14.5 is its `b → ∞` limit by dominated convergence.

Remaining (5): 5.6.8 (the fractional complex case needs Ismail–Lorch–Muldoon complete
monotonicity); 5.12.8, false as stated, since `betaIntegral a b = 0` for `Re a ≤ 0`
(counterexample `a = -1/2, b = 2, w = z = 1`: left side `-0.3536`, Lean right side `0`);
5.13.5 (needs Bailey's very-well-poised Barnes integrals); 5.14.6 (done for `Re c > 0` in
scratch, the strip `-1/n < Re c ≤ 0` needs an integrability lemma); 5.14.7 (Dyson).

## 5.12.8 and the Selberg files (2026-10-17)

5.12.8 (`beta_vertical`) was false as stated: Mathlib's `betaIntegral a b` is the literal
integral, which is `0` for `Re a ≤ 0`. With the user's approval its right side now reads
`(w+z)^{1-a-b} Γ(a+b)/((a+b-1)Γ(a)Γ(b))`. It is proved by Fourier inversion and Fubini for
`Re a > 0, Re b > 1`, the identity theorem in `b`, and the symmetry `t ↦ -t`.

Five more admissions were proved (192 → 187); `DLMF/Gamma` now has 2 (5.6.8, 5.13.5).

* 5.14.6 (Mehta): the limit of Selberg with `a = b = N+1`; integrability in the strip
  `-1/n < Re c ≤ 0` by Fatou.
* 5.14.7 (Dyson): Good's proof of the constant-term identity for integer `b`, Carlson's
  theorem, and continuation with integrability from Landau's theorem.
* 8.5.3 (`jacobiPolyP_discriminant`): Schur's resultant method from the three-term
  recurrence, without the Selberg integral. `jacobi_discriminant` in
  `OrthogonalPolynomials/BoundsZeros.lean` is the same result up to the factor
  `k_n^{2n-2}`; it could be derived from the `JacobiDisc` section, which needs only
  `OrthogonalPolynomials.Differential`.
* 8.9.1(a) (`nearly_poised_3F2`): AAR's proof from Aomoto's integral with `n = 2`.

Still admitted in `DLMF/Selberg/Applications.lean`: Siegel's inequality 8.6.2 (needs the
constrained maximum of the discriminant and the Laguerre discriminant), Hasse–Davenport
8.10.4 (not in Mathlib), and the finite-field Selberg integral 8.11.2.

The Schur resultant section for the Jacobi zeros moved from `Selberg/Applications.lean` to
