noted in the 2026-10-16 assessment.

## Beals–Wong cheap group: ODE tools, classical instances, Bessel and parabolic cylinder (2026-10-17)

A gap review of Beals and Wong, *Special Functions and Orthogonal Polynomials* (2015), was
followed by its cheapest high-leverage items. Admissions went from 184 to 169.

* New [ToMathlib/Analysis/ODE](ToMathlib/Analysis/ODE): Sturm separation and comparison and the
  at-most-one-zero theorem for `(p y')' + q y = 0` (`SturmComparison.lean`), and the
  Sonine–Pólya theorem with Sonine's function `y² + (k y')²/(k φ)` (`SoninePolya.lean`).
* [ClassicalInstances.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/ClassicalInstances.lean): Jacobi,
  Laguerre, Hermite, Gegenbauer, Legendre and Chebyshev `T` are instances of
  `IsOrthogonalPolynomials`; zeros lie inside the support (general theorem); Gauss quadrature,
  Christoffel–Darboux, and completeness under a finite exponential moment (TauCeti). The
  admitted `jacobiPolyP_complete` (18.18.i) moved here from `Classical.lean` with its statement
  unchanged and is proved. `zeros_interlace` (GeneralIdentities) is proved.
* [BoundsZeros.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/BoundsZeros.lean): the zero theorems for Jacobi,
  Laguerre and Hermite (including `|x| < √(2n+1)`), the Jacobi endpoint bounds 18.14.1–2
  (Sonine–Pólya), the Gegenbauer bounds 18.14.5–6, and the Laguerre zero bounds 18.16.12–13
  (Laguerre's inequality) are proved; 11 admissions remain (Bernstein-type and Turán
  inequalities, positive sums, Darboux).
* [Hahn.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Hahn.lean): orthogonality of Charlier, Meixner,
  Krawtchouk and Hahn (summation by parts for the difference operator), the Meixner,
  Krawtchouk and Hahn recurrences, the Hahn weight sum and the explicit Meixner sum; the
  Charlier–Laguerre identity is in `Wilson.lean`. Continuous Hahn, Meixner–Pollaczek, Wilson
  and continuous dual Hahn orthogonality remain admitted.
* New [Bessel/Elementary.lean](DLMF/Bessel/Elementary.lean): half-integer `Y` and `H`,
  `H_{-ν}`, Hankel recurrences, 10.6.6, `|J_n| ≤ 1`, Neumann's addition, the `I_n` generating
  function and cosine series, positivity of `I` and `K`, Poisson's integral for `I`, the leading
  behaviour of `H¹` and `Y`. New [Bessel/SturmZeros.lean](DLMF/Bessel/SturmZeros.lean):
  `I_ν(x) ~ eˣ/√(2πx)`, reality of the zeros of `J_ν` (`ν > -1`), zero spacing relative to `π`,
  infinitely many zeros of real cylinder functions, and interlacing of the zeros of `J_ν`,
  `J_{ν+1}`.
* [ParabolicCylinder.lean](DLMF/Confluent/ParabolicCylinder.lean): `D_n = e^{-z²/4} He_n`
  (12.7.2), recurrences 12.8.1–8, the integral 12.5.1, the second solution `V` with 12.2.8–10,
  12.2.13–17, 12.2.20, and the Wronskians 12.2.11–12 (sign checked against the DLMF site).

## Airy functions (2026-10-17)

The new area [DLMF/Airy](DLMF/Airy.lean) (DLMF chapter 9, Beals–Wong §9.9) defines `airyAi` and
`airyBi` as entire functions through `₀F̃₁` (9.4.1–4) and proves the differential equation, the
values at 0, the Wronskian `1/π`, uniqueness from initial values, the rotation relations
9.2.10–13, and reality on ℝ. For real `x > 0` it proves the Bessel relations 9.6.2, 9.6.4,
9.6.6 and 9.6.8, positivity on `[0, ∞)`, and the leading behaviour of `Ai` and `Bi` as
`x → +∞` (9.7.5, 9.7.7). Not yet done: the integral 9.5.1, the full asymptotic series,
complex sectors, and the zeros. See [DLMF/Airy/DLMFCoverage.md](DLMF/Airy/DLMFCoverage.md).

## Volterra lemma and large-parameter asymptotics, first round (2026-10-17)

* New [DLMFMathlib/Analysis/ODE/Volterra.lean](DLMFMathlib/Analysis/ODE/Volterra.lean): for a given
  solution of `u'' + ω²u = g·u` (Banach-valued), the Volterra integral equation, the bound
  `‖u − u₀‖ ≤ M (exp(∫|g|/|ω|) − 1)` against the free oscillation, Gronwall's inequality in
  integral form, and on a half-line with `g` integrable the oscillation `cos(ωx)A + sin(ωx)B`
  with error `O(∫ₓ^∞ |g|)`.
* [HermiteFixedX.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/HermiteFixedX.lean): Hermite asymptotics at
  fixed `x` with an explicit error bound, in DLMF's normalization 18.15.27 (with `λ_n` of
  18.15.25) and in Szegő's (8.22.8), both `O(n^{-1/2})`.
* [Bessel/LargeOrder.lean](DLMF/Bessel/LargeOrder.lean): Beals–Wong (9.1.16) for `J_ν(√ν x)` and
  its `I_ν` companion, the bound `|J_ν(z)| ≤ |(z/2)^ν| e^{|z|²/4}/Γ(ν+1)` (`ν ≥ 0`), and
  `J_ν(z) ~ (ez/2ν)^ν/√(2πν)` (10.19.1).

## Kummer functions for large negative `a` (2026-10-17)

[LargeA.lean](DLMF/Confluent/LargeA.lean) proves the Erdélyi–Schmidt–Fejér asymptotics with error
`o(1)`: for fixed `x > 0` and `κ = c/2 − a → ∞`, the regularized `𝐌(a,c,x)` in Bessel form
(compare DLMF 13.8.9) and in cosine form (Beals–Wong (8.1.12)), `U(a,c,x)` for non-integer `c`
(Beals–Wong (8.2.10), compare DLMF 13.8.10), and Fejér's formula for `L_n^{(α)}(x)` (Szegő
(8.22.4), compare DLMF 18.15.14). The route is a Liouville transformation to `u'' + u = g u`,
a Tannery limit to the Bessel equation, and matching on `[s₀, 2√(κx)]` with the Volterra lemma.
These `o(1)` theorems carry no `@[dlmf]` tags. The DLMF rates were proved the same day in
[LargeASharp.lean](DLMF/Confluent/LargeASharp.lean) by applying the oscillator Volterra lemma to
the difference `u_κ − u₀` (so the `1/s²` term costs only a fixed factor) with a quantitative
Tannery estimate `O(κ⁻¹)` for the data: 13.8.9 (`𝐌` in Bessel form, all real `c`), 13.8.10
(`U`, non-integer `c`), 18.15.14 with `M = 1` (Fejér, `O(n^{-1/2})`), and the cosine form of
Beals–Wong (13.2.8). DLMF's `env J · O(|a|^{-1/2})` is written as `O(S^{-1/2}|a|^{-1/2})`,
using `env J_ν(S) ≍ S^{-1/2}`.

## Beals–Wong additions (2026-10-18)

Five new modules supply eleven main proved results, all exported by the existing
area umbrellas:

- [DiscreteAddition.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/DiscreteAddition.lean):
  `charlier_addition`, `charlier_addition_convolution`, `krawtchouk_addition`,
  and `meixner_addition`, corresponding to (6.3.10)–(6.3.11), (6.4.12),
  and (6.5.11). They use the existing normalizations and complex arguments;
  Krawtchouk requires `n ≤ N`, and Meixner requires `0 < Re β`.
  Formal power series avoid adding analytic generating-function assumptions.
  In `Hahn.lean`, `poch_mul_meixner_eq_sum_of_ne_zero` generalizes the
  coefficient lemma to explicit finite nonvanishing assumptions; the original
  `poch_mul_meixner_eq_sum` retains its statement as a wrapper.
- [DiscreteRodrigues.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/DiscreteRodrigues.lean):
  `charlier_rodrigues`, using Mathlib's `fwdDiff (-1)` and the zero-extended
  Charlier weight. It holds for all integer arguments and nonzero complex `a`,
  including the boundary cases with difference order greater than the argument.
- [HermiteZeroBounds.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/HermiteZeroBounds.lean):
  `hermite_even_positive_zero_bounds` and `hermite_odd_positive_zero_bounds`,
  the strict indexed bounds (5.3.18)–(5.3.19), together with complete sorted
  enumerations of the positive roots. Existing Hermite–Laguerre identities,
  outermost-root bounds, and Sturm comparison provide the proof ingredients.
- [JacobiZeroBounds.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/JacobiZeroBounds.lean):
  `jacobi_zero_angle_bounds` for `α², β² ≤ 1/4`, with a complete increasing
  angle enumeration and both non-strict bounds from Theorem 5.5.1.
  The proof supplies the Jacobi-specific angular Liouville transformation.
  The strict refinement away from the simultaneous boundary case remains open.
- [IntegralTransforms.lean](DLMF/Bessel/IntegralTransforms.lean):
  `besselJ_gaussian_integral` and `besselJ_gaussian_integral_kummerM` for
  positive real scale/frequency and `Re(μ + ν) > 0`; the regularized version
  includes exceptional orders. `besselJ_laplace_integral_kummer` holds for
  complex `s` with `Re s > 0`, positive real frequency, and the same condition
  on `μ + ν`, excluding nonpositive integral values of `ν + 1` and `2ν + 1`.
  It uses the continued Gauss function, so has no series-radius restriction.
  Those exclusions concern the representation; no integral singularity is
  asserted there. Complex Gaussian scales, removal of the Laplace exclusions,
  and the conventional quadratic-transform form remain open.

The full `lake build` passed (4271 jobs). Direct `lake env lean` checks cover
all eight changed Lean files and `Main.lean`. A thirteen-declaration axiom audit
covers the eleven main results, the generalized Meixner lemma, and its unchanged
wrapper: every dependency list contains only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and
`Quot.sound`. The new modules contain no `sorry` or axioms; the three existing
admissions in `Hahn.lean` remain. `git diff --check` passes. No existing theorem
assumptions, dependency pins, or build settings were changed.

The [inventory](BealsWongInventory.md) records the remaining special-function
targets. This batch adds only the algebra and ODE transformations needed by
these concrete formulas and bounds.


## Beals–Wong refinements (2026-10-18)

The refinements identified after the first batch are now proved in their stated
parameter ranges. No earlier public theorem statement was changed.

- `jacobi_zero_angle_bounds_strict` in
  [JacobiZeroBounds.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/JacobiZeroBounds.lean)
  makes both angle inequalities strict whenever at least one of `α², β²` is
  less than `1/4`. A uniform positive improvement of the comparison
  coefficient avoids endpoint differentiability assumptions.
- `meixner_rodrigues` and `krawtchouk_rodrigues` in
  [DiscreteRodrigues.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/DiscreteRodrigues.lean)
  extend the Charlier development. Meixner uses `Re β > 0`, `c ≠ 0`; a
  finite nonvanishing version also permits the Krawtchouk specialization.
  Krawtchouk uses `n ≤ N`, `p ≠ 0, 1`, and its weight is identified with the
  binomial weight. The shifted weights are identified with their Pochhammer
  multipliers. All integer arguments are included, including shifts across
  support boundaries.
- `hahn_rodrigues` in the new
  [HahnRodrigues.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/HahnRodrigues.lean) covers
  `n ≤ N`, `Re α, Re β > -1`, and every integer argument. The proof uses
  the existing Hahn difference equation and forward difference for the
  weighted raising step, then iterates it. `hahnRodriguesWeight_eq` identifies
  the shifted weight with the original weight times the forward-coefficient
  product. Hahn's other real regime `α, β < -N` is not covered here.
- `besselJ_gaussian_integral_complex` and its ordinary Kummer wrapper in
  [IntegralTransforms.lean](DLMF/Bessel/IntegralTransforms.lean) allow complex
  Gaussian scale `s` with `Re s > 0`, positive real frequency, and
  `Re(μ + ν) > 0`. The regularized statement retains all orders. The real-scale
  theorem keeps its original statement and now wraps the complex result.
- `besselJ_laplace_integral` in the new
  [LaplaceTransform.lean](DLMF/Bessel/LaplaceTransform.lean) gives the conventional
  Gauss form with argument `-w²/s²`. It assumes only `Re s > 0`, positive real
  frequency, and `Re(μ + ν) > 0`, with no order exclusions.
  `besselJ_laplace_integrable` proves absolute convergence. The underlying
  `laplace_regularized0F1` includes every complex denominator parameter.
  Kummer's transform and the quadratic transformation establish a starting
  parameter range; continuation in the Laplace variable and regularized
  contiguous relations give the full result. The existing Kummer formula
  retains its statement. Its argument-domain lemma in `Confluent/Integrals.lean`
  was made public for reuse, without changing its proof.

## Beals–Wong Painlevé II (2026-10-18)

The new [Painlevé umbrella](DLMF/Painleve.lean), also imported by `DLMF.lean`,
contains eight proved modules following Chapter 15. The convention is
`PII(c): u'' = 2u³ + xu - c`; the common parameter `α` is `-c`.

- [Basic.lean](DLMF/Painleve/Basic.lean) defines `IsPIIOn` with actual first
  and second differentiability. It proves restriction, open-set transport,
  sign reflection, the zero and reciprocal solutions, and
  `IsPIIOn.hasDerivAt_energy` for
  `(u')² - u⁴ - xu² + 2cu`, whose derivative is `-u²`.
- [Backlund.lean](DLMF/Painleve/Backlund.lean) proves `IsPIIOn.reflect` for
  `u + (1-2c)/(x+2u²+2u')`, at parameter `1-c`. The denominator is preserved,
  and `IsPIIOn.backlundReflect_involutive` proves involutivity. The
  `shiftUp`, `shiftDown`, `backlundUp_down`, and `backlundDown_up` theorems
  establish the integer shifts and inverse identities. All transformation
  results specify nonvanishing denominators on an open solution domain.
  These local statements allow `c = 1/2` when that domain exists; the
  nonempty-domain lemma separately assumes `1-2c ≠ 0`.
- [Rational.lean](DLMF/Painleve/Rational.lean) defines the explicit polynomial
  sequence `rationalPIIPair`. `rationalPIIPair_spec` proves that its
  denominator is nonzero and its quotient solves `PII(n)` on the full
  denominator regular set. `exists_rational_solution_int` gives existence
  for every integer parameter, including negative parameters by reflection.
  The first two nonzero pairs are checked explicitly, yielding `1/x` and
  `(2x³-4)/(x(x³+4))`. This proves the existence direction of Theorem 15.3.1,
  without asserting its necessity or uniqueness directions.
- [Airy.lean](DLMF/Painleve/Airy.lean) proves the Riccati reduction and the
  logarithmic derivative of `a Ai(kz) + b Bi(kz)` at parameter `1/2`, where
  `k³ = -1/2`. `airyPIIJet` is a finite rational arithmetic recurrence in
  the Airy seed and its first derivative.
  `exists_open_isPIIOn_airyPIIHierarchy` gives a nonempty open solution
  domain for each parameter `n+1/2`, `n : ℤ`, using the `Ai` seed.
  `meromorphic_airyPIIHierarchy` proves plane-meromorphicity of the explicit
  functions. The equation is asserted on the constructed open domains,
  rather than at poles or every potentially removable denominator zero.
- [LaxPair.lean](DLMF/Painleve/LaxPair.lean) defines the book's two matrices.
  `lax_curvature` identifies their curvature with the scalar PII residual;
  `lax_compatibility_iff` gives the converse at nonzero spectral parameter.
  `IsPIIOn.hasDerivAt_laxA` is the actual matrix derivative along a solution.
  Both traces and the spectral reflection symmetries are also proved.
- [LocalTheory.lean](DLMF/Painleve/LocalTheory.lean) proves
  `exists_local_real_solution` for arbitrary real initial position, value,
  and derivative. `IsPIIOn.eventuallyEq_real` proves uniqueness of real
  solution germs. The proof applies Mathlib's ODE results to the polynomial
  autonomous vector field `(x,u,v)' = (1,v,2u³+xu-c)`.
- [Poles.lean](DLMF/Painleve/Poles.lean) proves `pole_order_eq_neg_one`,
  `pole_analytic_factor`, and `pole_residue_limit`: if a solution is
  meromorphic at a pole and satisfies PII in a punctured neighborhood,
  that pole is simple and has residue `+1` or `-1`.
- [Tau.lean](DLMF/Painleve/Tau.lean) proves `isPIIOn_quotient_of_tau`, the
  converse from (15.3.10)–(15.3.11) to PII, assuming twice differentiable
  numerator and denominator and restricting to an open set avoiding the
  denominator's zeros. Entire tau-pair existence is not assumed or proved.

The substitution printed on page 407, `φ(x) = ψ(i·2^(-1/2)x)`, does not
transform `φ'' + (x/2)φ = 0` into the book's Airy equation. Direct
differentiation requires `k³ = -1/2`. The formalization uses this corrected
condition and proves that such a complex scale exists.

Remaining Chapter 15 targets include complex analytic local existence,
the convergent Laurent family and its free coefficient, the converse and
uniqueness of the rational classification and pole counts, sectorial
fundamental matrices, Stokes and isomonodromy results, global meromorphic
continuation for arbitrary initial data, and the real asymptotics and
connection constants in §15.8. The new conditional pole theorems do not
establish that global continuation.

The 38-declaration audit in `/tmp/AAR-Painleve-AxiomAudit.lean` reports only

remain. Dependency pins, the Lake symlink, build settings, and earlier
theorem statements are unchanged.


## Shared Lean and Mathlib upgrade (2026-10-18)

The shared installation and all seven `lean-*` projects with `.lake`,
`lakefile.toml`, and `lake-manifest.json` are synchronized: AAR, CA, DB, LCS,
PR, SCV, and codes. The snapshot is Lean `v4.36.0-rc1`, Mathlib
`911003dd6dea5df215dff57f2717be762e11a6ca`, and, in the five projects that
declare it, TauCeti `5cdecf40e99d5a5ad0833afff31f5b007d68a149`. Doc-gen4 is
`v4.36.0-rc1` (`0656b7ebd305f089c8d6bd8684a0797f19414cc2`) where declared.
PR and DB retain Mathlib-only configurations. Shared transitive dependencies
have identical revisions; Lake regenerated each manifest from its own graph.

TauCeti's own Mathlib pin is 65 commits earlier than this Mathlib snapshot.
One imported source file needs a compatibility edit:
`ContinuousMap.coe_coe` becomes `ContinuousMap.coe_ofClass` in
`TauCeti/MeasureTheory/Measure/FiniteMeasureExt.lean`. The applied edit is saved
as [TauCeti-4.36.patch](TauCeti-4.36.patch) in every TauCeti-using project;
the README explains application after a fresh checkout. This is an explicit
local patch, so the shared TauCeti checkout has that one tracked modification.

The source migration adds direct imports for APIs no longer publicly
re-exported, makes set domains and product derivatives explicit for the new
elaborator, uses the dependent-family argument of `contDiff_apply`, updates
renamed finite-sum lemmas, and supplies finite-dimensional completeness or
properness instances from the existing hypotheses. The contour wrapper now
uses TauCeti's generalized absolutely continuous winding-number theorem.
CA's duplicate `AnalyticAt.dslope` was removed in favor of Mathlib's more
general theorem; its caller supplies the base point explicitly. No theorem
was weakened and no admission or axiom was added. Existing admission counts
in all changed source files are preserved.

Validation:

- Full ordinary builds passed in AAR (9031 jobs), CA (7551), LCS (6569),
  PR (6306), SCV (7245), and codes (9749).
- DB's pins and toolchain are synchronized. Its pre-existing Lake root
  `LeanDB.lean` is absent; the project contains no Lean implementation to
  build. No placeholder implementation was introduced.
- All 57 directly imported TauCeti modules and their dependencies passed a
  joint build (7859 jobs), with the package's strict warning settings.
  Doc-gen4 and its executable also built successfully (269 jobs).
- 93 direct file/entry checks passed. Axiom audits of 66 declarations,
  including the 38 Painlevé results and the patched TauCeti theorem, report
  only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`.
- 94 mandated shared-source comparisons passed, including CA/SCV/codes
  copies; AAR's two shared support files still match codes. Diff whitespace,
  dependency consistency, admission counts, and documentation links were
  checked. The TauCeti patch is identical in all five copies.

Every original `.lake` symlink and per-project `buildDir` is preserved.
Existing work was retained and no commits were made. The pre-upgrade source,
configuration, and working-tree snapshots, migration scripts, and validation
logs are under `/tmp/lean-upgrade-436`; baselines are in its `before` directory.


## Admission review after Beals–Wong, Temme, and the dependency upgrade (2026-10-18)

This section supersedes earlier admission counts and proof-priority lists in this
file. It reviews the current working tree, including untracked new modules. This
was a review: no project Lean source, theorem statement, dependency pin, or Lake
configuration was changed.

### Census and interpretation

There are **169 admitted theorem proofs in 39 files**, across 223 Lean source
files under `DLMF/` and `ToMathlib/`. The distribution is:

- Orthogonal polynomials: 69.
- Confluent functions: 69.
- Hypergeometric functions: 14.
- Spherical harmonics: 6.
- Selberg applications: 3.
- Partition congruences: 3.
- Gamma functions: 2.
- Bessel functions: 2.
- Basic hypergeometric series: 1.

The count excludes comments containing the word `sorry`. `ToMathlib/` has no
admissions. Neither do the Airy and Painlevé libraries, or the Legendre,
statistical-function, elliptic-integral, and wave-equation directories. In
particular, the unformalized global Painlevé results are missing coverage,
rather than existing admissions to remove.

The review uses the installed Mathlib `911003dd6dea5df215dff57f2717be762e11a6ca`
and TauCeti `5cdecf40e99d5a5ad0833afff31f5b007d68a149`, with Lean
`v4.36.0-rc1`. Searches proceeded through Mathlib, TauCeti, and then local code.
There is no wholesale upstream replacement for the remaining special-function
statements. The largest practical improvement comes from the local proved
confluent limits, transforms, polynomial identities, and parameter continuation.
TauCeti contributes two especially useful additional structural results below.

Priorities in the inventory mean:

- **F**: a statement defect was found; repair its mathematical formulation before
  attempting a proof.
- **P1**: a concrete proof route uses existing, audited ingredients; recommended
  first. These are proof plans, not claims that all adapter proofs are finished.
- **P2**: a worthwhile next batch, with identifiable ingredients but additional
  finite identities, analytic bounds, or integration arguments to develop.
  Some entries are corollaries of other still-admitted entries in the same batch.
- **P3**: substantial work remains; the recent additions do not make it an
  immediate removal candidate.
- **P4**: defer relative to the special-functions objective, because the target
  requires a substantial separate foundational or number-theoretic development.

### First repair five Whittaker transform statements

Five admissions in [Confluent/Integrals.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Integrals.lean) confuse
regularized Kummer functions with ordinary Kummer functions divided by Gamma at
an exceptional parameter. In Lean, division by `Gamma (-n) = 0` is zero. It does
not supply the analytic continuation of the regularized function. These are
counterexamples to the current statements, not just missing proof techniques:

- `mellin_whittakerM`: take `κ = 1`, `μ = -1/2`, `ν = 1/2`. Both hypotheses hold.
  The integral is zero because its denominator is `Gamma 0`; the asserted value
  is `Gamma(1/2)^2 / Gamma(-1/2) = -sqrt(pi)/2`, which is nonzero. The inequality
  between the two displayed sides was checked in Lean without `sorryAx`.
- `cosine_whittakerM`: take `κ = 1`, `μ = -1/2`, `x = 1`. The left side is zero
  for the same reason; the right side is `sqrt(pi)/(4e)`. The Whittaker factor
  is `W(5/4,1/4,1) = exp(-1/2)/2`; its nonvanishing was checked in Lean.
- `bessel_whittakerM_W`: take `κ = 1`, `μ = -1/2`, `ν = 0`, `x = 2`. Again the
  left side is zero, whereas the right side is `exp(-2)`. The relevant factor
  `W(3/2,0,2)` was checked to be nonzero in Lean.
- `bessel_whittakerW_M`: take `κ = 2`, `μ = 1/2`, `ν = 1`, `x = 2`. Here the
  *right* side is zero because its denominator is `Gamma 0`. Using
  `U(-1,2,t) = t-2`, the left side becomes
  `integral exp(-t) t^(1/2) (t-2) J_1(2 sqrt(2t)) dt`, equal to
  `-2^(3/2) exp(-2)`. This evaluation follows by splitting the polynomial and
  applying the proved Gamma-weighted Bessel integral and Kummer transformation.
- `bessel_whittakerM`: take `κ = 5/2`, `μ = 0`, `ν = -4`, `x = 1`. Its hypotheses
  allow this negative integer Bessel order. The right side contains division by
  `Gamma (-1)` and is zero. Using `J_{-4} = J_4` and
  `M(-2,1,t) = 1-2t+t^2/2`, the left side is
  `integral exp(-t) t^2 (1-2t+t^2/2) J_4(2 sqrt(t)) dt = 5/(2e)`.
  The last two evaluations were also checked numerically at 30-digit precision;
  those numerical checks are corroboration, not Lean proof certificates.

The preferred repair is to formulate these identities using regularized
Whittaker M expressions, directly through `regularizedHGFun`, retaining the
intended parameter ranges. Ordinary-M corollaries can then carry explicit
nonpole assumptions. Simply importing the admitted Kummer versions and rewriting
`M/Gamma` would conceal the defect. No repair has been made in this review.

### P1: the first eleven proof targets

1. **Three Mehler–Heine limits** in
   [Limits.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Limits.lean):
   `laguerrePolyL_mehler_heine`, `hermite_even_mehler_heine`, and
   `hermite_odd_mehler_heine`. The new
   `DLMF.Confluent.tendsto_regularizedConfluent_sub_div` gives
   `regularizedHGFun {-n} {α+1} (z/n) → regularizedHGFun ∅ {α+1} (-z)`.
   That exact specialization to natural-number degree was checked in Lean.
   Combine it with `DLMF.Gamma.Gamma_ratio_power` and the Laguerre definition.
   Prove the intermediate Laguerre limit for complex scaled arguments in the
   entire `0F1` formulation, so that the existing even/odd Hermite–Laguerre
   identities give both Hermite statements for every complex `z`, including zero.
   The real positive-argument Bessel statement alone would not suffice for those
   complex Hermite conclusions. This is the clearest benefit of the Beals–Wong
   large-parameter work. Expected effort: small to moderate normalization proofs.

2. **The Laguerre–Bessel integral** `laguerrePolyL_bessel_integral` in
   [Integrals.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Integrals.lean). Specialize the
   already-proved `DLMF.Confluent.regularized_bessel_integral` with
   `a = n+α+1`, `b = α+1`, and `z = x`. Apply the regularized Kummer
   transformation and the Gamma/Pochhammer formula for Laguerre. All required
   positivity assumptions follow from `α > -1`, `n ≥ 0`, `x > 0`. The newer
   Gaussian–Bessel transform provides another route, but no new transform theory
   is needed. Expected effort: small.

3. **The uniform Laguerre bound** `laguerrePolyL_bound` in
   [BoundsZeros.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/BoundsZeros.lean). Set the degree
   gap `m = 0` in the proved `hermite_product_cosine_transform`. Its integrand is
   a nonnegative Gaussian times a Hermite square, so `abs cos ≤ 1` bounds the
   transform by its value at zero. This gives the desired bound for `α = 0`,
   with `x = y^2`. Extend to `α ≥ 0` using the proved
   `laguerrePolyL_connection`: its coefficients are nonnegative, and their sum
   is `(α+1)_n/n!`. Handle `α = 0` separately rather than invoking strict
   positivity of `(α)_k`. Expected effort: moderate, with no sharp new ODE bound.

4. **Three moment determinant formulas** in
   [GeneralIdentities.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/GeneralIdentities.lean):
   `monic_moment_determinant`, `monic_diagonal_moment_determinant`, and
   `monic_offdiagonal_moment_determinant`. Exact-degree polynomial bases,
   integrability of all polynomial products, recurrence coordinates, and
   `hankelDeterminant_pos` are already proved. A triangular monic change of basis
   identifies the moment determinant with the product of squared norms; this
   supplies the offdiagonal formula. Orthogonality and determinant expansion
   identify the bordered determinant polynomial; its next coefficient gives
   the diagonal formula. Include the empty determinant and `n = 0` cases.
   Expected effort: moderate finite linear algebra, confined to the OP API.

5. **The disk-polynomial polar formula** `diskPolynomial_polar` in
   [Multivariate.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Multivariate.lean). Both sides
   are finite polynomial sums. Split `m ≤ n` and `n ≤ m`, expand the Jacobi
   polynomial, and use conjugation of `r exp(iθ)`. Check `r = 0` and `m = n`
   explicitly. There is no new analytic prerequisite. Expected effort: small
   to moderate finite-sum algebra.

6. **Two upstream-backed structural admissions** in
   [SphericalHarmonics/Theorems.lean](DLMF/SphericalHarmonics/Theorems.lean):
   `poissonKernelBall_reproduces` and `su2_covers_so3`.
   TauCeti's `PoissonIntegral.Ball` supplies harmonicity and boundary attainment;
   `Laplacian.Comparison` supplies uniqueness. Identify the local kernel as sphere
   area times the normalized TauCeti kernel, using
   `sum (x_i-y_i)^2 = norm(x-y)^2`, then apply uniqueness of the Dirichlet problem.
   For the cover, combine `realSpinThreeEquivSpecialUnitary` with
   `spinToSpecialOrthogonal_surjective_of_posDef` and the compact Spin double
   cover. There remains a genuine adapter from the quadratic-form orthogonal
   group to the project's matrix group, and the kernel must be transported to
   the two matrices `±1`. These are not one-line name substitutions. Expected
   effort: moderate adapters. The cover is ancillary to special functions and
   can follow the direct special-function targets despite its upstream readiness.

### P2: the next coherent proof batches

**Finite identities and generating functions.** Start with the two finite
Kummer–Bessel I sums in [Relations.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Relations.lean), using
`kummerM_besselI`, contiguous relations, and finite induction. The Kummer product
identity in [ProductsAddition.lean](DLMF/Confluent/ProductsAddition.lean) is another
bounded target using the proved differential/contiguous API; its Whittaker
product is then an adapter. The first-parameter addition identities require a
new generating/Taylor rearrangement and summability argument, after which their
Kummer-transformed and Whittaker forms should be handled together. Do not count
the latter as independent upstream solutions while their Kummer premise is
still admitted.

The finite Gegenbauer connection, monomial expansion and linearization in
[Series.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Series.lean), and the general Jacobi
connection in [Topics.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Topics.lean), are good
algebraic batches. The finite expansions, recurrences, and Chu–Vandermonde/
Pfaff–Saalschütz tools exist. The Laurent formulation in
[QFamilies.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/QFamilies.lean) likewise makes
`askeyWilson_difference_equation` a finite coefficient/telescoping problem,
without requiring Askey–Wilson orthogonality. `whipple_7F6` is a reasonable
terminating-sum target now that the smaller terminating identities are proved;
it still needs a new finite-sum argument.

The Jacobi generating-function family, Mehler's Hermite formula, and Legendre's
addition formula are promising next analytic/algebraic developments. They need
coefficient identities plus actual convergence proofs. TauCeti's ordinary
Hermite generating function, already adapted locally, is not Mehler's bilinear
formula. Jacobi's Brafman product generating function is a later target rather
than an immediate corollary of the univariate generating function.

**Classical integral identities.** The nine remaining OP integral statements
other than the P1 Laguerre–Bessel formula form manageable, but uneven, batches.
Use finite polynomial expansions and Beta/Gamma moments for the Jacobi and
Laguerre double integrals and the Laguerre product Laplace transform. Use
finite addition formulas and trigonometric moments for Legendre/Gegenbauer
product integrals. The Dirichlet–Mehler and ultraspherical Dirichlet integrals
share a singular-endpoint substitution argument; prove integrability rather
than treating the endpoint as an ordinary continuous substitution. The
Legendre singular integral has cancellation in its numerator and can be
approached by polynomial division and recurrence. The Bessel-square identity
also needs a product-series argument and attention to negative `y`.

**Orthogonality beyond the classical families.** Continuous Hahn and
Meixner–Pollaczek orthogonality in [Hahn.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Hahn.lean)
are better candidates than Wilson orthogonality: the proved
`DLMF.Gamma.mellin_barnes_four` gives the needed shifted Gamma moments for Hahn;
`integral_Gamma_mul_Gamma_mul_cpow` supplies the Fourier moments for
Meixner–Pollaczek. Finish with finite polynomial expansions and terminating
summations. The continuous Hahn, Wilson and continuous dual Hahn generating
functions are separate Cauchy-product/coefficient problems and need not wait
for their orthogonality integrals.

For the q-families, prioritize finite q-Hahn orthogonality, little-q-Jacobi
orthogonality using q-Gauss summation, continuous q-Hermite orthogonality using
the proved Jacobi triple product, and Stieltjes–Wigert orthogonality using
Gaussian moments after `x = exp u`. TauCeti also has log-normal real-power
moments; matching its probability density to the project's unnormalized weight
is essential. These latter source-level leads were inspected, but their
additional TauCeti imports were not compiled or axiom-audited in this review.
The existing reciprocal-variable Laguerre identity makes finite Bessel-polynomial
orthogonality attractive. Morse orthogonality can use the proved Morse ODE,
Laguerre expressions and endpoint decay. Exceptional Laguerre/Hermite require
factorization and integration by parts, including their exceptional lowest mode;
they are feasible developments, not inherited from standard orthogonality alone.
Szegő–Askey orthogonality has an ordinary trigonometric Beta-moment route.

**Remaining limits and logarithmic expansions.** `jacobi_mehler_heine` should
follow a direct dominated terminating-series limit with *both* numerator
parameters varying. The new confluent Tannery result handles only one varying
parameter and cannot be substituted verbatim. For the six admissions comprising
`kummerU_nat_denom` and the five remaining small-argument U bounds in
[BasicProperties.lean](DLMF/Confluent/BasicProperties.lean), first derive the
integer-parameter logarithmic series from the proved parameter continuation and
Gamma/digamma derivatives. Noninteger cases reuse the already-proved connection
formula and neighboring strip estimates. Resonant integer cases require the
logarithmic terms. The final `re_nonpos` statement uses `nhds 0`, not a punctured
filter, so also establish the actual totalized value at zero.

The joint entire-parameter theorem for regularized Gauss functions is worth
finishing, but the proved analyticity along entire curves is not yet the stated
Fréchet differentiability on `Complex × Complex × Complex`. Reuse the coefficient
majorant in `Continuation.lean` with a derivative or multivariable analyticity
argument; do not replace the theorem by separate analyticity. The shifted
large-denominator Gauss expansion and Kummer large-b Poincaré expansion can
reuse the finite-tail expansion, Gamma-ratio expansion and Temme's Watson
machinery. Keep any extra asymptotic lemmas tied to these specific estimates.

**Other analytic identities.** `kummerU_integral_besselK` is a sensible next
Fubini/integrability target; its Whittaker form is an adapter. The Whittaker
Gauss, Barnes, Stieltjes, Laplace and Bessel formulas marked P2 in the inventory
are conditional follow-ups to their Kummer counterparts. This is a development
order, not evidence that those underlying admissions have been proved.
`ramanujan_1psi1` can build on q-binomial, q-Gauss and triple-product results,
but needs bilateral summability and justified limiting/reindexing of negative
indices. It is a focused q-series project rather than an upstream import.

### What should wait

- The remaining sharp Jacobi/Gegenbauer/Hermite bounds, positivity sums, and
  general Jacobi Turán inequality need their specific sharp constants or
  positivity arguments. Sonine–Pólya and Sturm comparison do not automatically
  prove them. Laguerre Turán is a more contained P2 recurrence/kernel target.
- The ten confluent zero admissions need oscillation counts, parameter-zero
  analysis, or complex zero-location arguments. Proved Laguerre/Hermite zeros
  cover special parameter slices, not the full real/complex parameters in
  these statements. The new large-a asymptotics can assist later, but existence,
  enumeration and error control for zeros remain to be supplied.
- The Kummer/Whittaker transition expansions and simultaneous large-a,b
  expansions need new rescaled saddle/endpoint analysis and the stated remainder
  orders. Having parabolic-cylinder/Airy definitions and leading Laplace bounds
  does not prove the displayed two-term formulas. The new large-a modules hold
  the second parameter fixed and do not solve these simultaneous limits.
- The seven continued-fraction limits need identification of their convergents,
  minimal-solution or contraction estimates, and convergence. Mathlib's
  `GenContFract` datatype is not that proof. Recheck the unrestricted Whittaker
  parameter ranges at zeros of their denominator functions; the two Whittaker
  statements do not impose the positive Kummer parameters used in the simpler
  Kummer convergence statements.
- The Bessel addition theorem needs a convergent Gegenbauer–Bessel expansion;
  Nicholson's integral needs an appropriate Bessel-product argument. Neither
  follows immediately from the new one-function Gaussian/Laplace transforms.
- The Barnes–Selberg Gamma integral is still the substantial missing
  normalization behind a natural Wilson-orthogonality route. The already-proved
  four-Gamma Barnes lemma is not this eight-Gamma/denominator integral. Askey–Wilson
  orthogonality likewise needs its own q-integral evaluation, not the ordinary
  Selberg integral or the q→1 limit. General Pollaczek and the remaining q-weight
  orthogonalities also retain analytic work.
- Spherical harmonic orthogonality, addition, and Funk–Hecke need a bridge from
  polynomial harmonicity to the analytic Laplacian plus spherical integration
  or representation theory. The combinatorial dimension *formula* currently
  defined locally is not a proof of the dimension of the harmonic subspace.
- Verblunsky is better supported by TauCeti's Herglotz representation of positive
  definite sequences, but still needs construction of positive moments from
  the prescribed coefficients and uniqueness. General Favard still needs a
  representing-measure construction for unrestricted recurrence coefficients;
  compact-support polynomial completeness is insufficient.
- Ramanujan's three partition congruences, Apéry irrationality, Hasse–Davenport,
  finite-field Selberg and the general Siegel inequality remain separate major
  developments. I would defer these P4 entries while focusing on special
  functions themselves. None was found as a ready replacement in the pinned
  upstream sources.

## Whittaker corrections and the first prioritized proof batch (2026-10-18)

This implementation supersedes the F and P1 statuses in the preceding admission
review and its historical line-number inventory. All eleven P1 proofs are now
complete. The source census is **158 admitted proofs in 38 files**, down from
169 in 39 files. No new admission or axiom was introduced, and the five repaired
Whittaker transform identities are still among those 158 admissions.

### Whittaker statement corrections

[Whittaker.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Whittaker.lean) now defines
`regularizedWhittakerM κ μ z` using `regularizedHGFun`. The proved bridge
`regularizedWhittakerM_eq_div_Gamma` identifies it with
`whittakerM κ μ z / Gamma (1 + 2 * μ)` away from Gamma poles. The definition retains
the same principal complex power and exponential prefactor.

The five false transform statements identified in the review were corrected in
[Integrals.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Integrals.lean):

- `mellin_whittakerM` and `cosine_whittakerM` use regularized input `M`.
- `bessel_whittakerM` uses regularized input and output `M`, with the corresponding
  Gamma quotient removed.
- `bessel_whittakerM_W` uses regularized input `M` instead of the ordinary quotient.
- `bessel_whittakerW_M` uses regularized output `M`; the output Gamma denominator
  is absorbed into that regularization.

Their parameter and convergence hypotheses were retained. These changes repair
the exceptional-parameter errors rather than excluding those parameters. The five
transform proofs remain future work; the regularization bridge has no `sorryAx`
dependency.

### Completed P1 proofs

- [Limits.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Limits.lean):
  `laguerrePolyL_mehler_heine`, `hermite_even_mehler_heine`, and
  `hermite_odd_mehler_heine`. A complex-argument regularized Laguerre limit handles
  both Hermite parity formulas, including the zero argument.
- [Integrals.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Integrals.lean):
  `laguerrePolyL_bessel_integral`, from the regularized Bessel integral and Kummer
  transformation, with explicit real-square-root normalization.
- [BoundsZeros.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/BoundsZeros.lean):
  `laguerrePolyL_bound`, first at parameter zero through the positive
  Hermite-square cosine transform, then for all nonnegative parameters through
  nonnegative Laguerre connection coefficients.
- [GeneralIdentities.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/GeneralIdentities.lean):
  `monic_moment_determinant`, `monic_diagonal_moment_determinant`, and
  `monic_offdiagonal_moment_determinant`. Column replacement, orthogonality and a
  corner-minor expansion yield the norm and next-to-leading coefficient formulas.
  This file now has no admissions.
- [Multivariate.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Multivariate.lean):
  `diskPolynomial_polar`, through reflected finite Jacobi coefficients and degree
  exchange under conjugation. The existing nonnegative-radius hypothesis is
  retained, although the proof also works for negative radii.
- [Theorems.lean](DLMF/SphericalHarmonics/Theorems.lean):
  `poissonKernelBall_reproduces` and `su2_covers_so3`. The first uses TauCeti’s
  Poisson extension and Dirichlet uniqueness, matching the unnormalized kernel to
  surface area times the normalized kernel. The second transports TauCeti’s Spin
  cover and proves that its kernel is exactly the two specified matrices.

The completed statements retain their original hypotheses and normalizations.
The remaining admissions in these areas include the Jacobi Mehler–Heine limit,
triangle-polynomial orthogonality and norms, spherical addition, Funk–Hecke and
Gegenbauer addition. The preceding P2–P4 analysis continues to apply; the corrected
Whittaker transforms are now viable proof targets using regularized confluent
integrals and Bessel transforms.

### Validation

The ordinary full build, `lake build > /tmp/DLMF-build.log 2>&1`, passed with
9,573 jobs. Direct `lake env lean` checks passed for all nine changed Lean files
and for `Main.lean`. An axiom audit of the eleven completed former admissions
and the new Whittaker regularization bridge reports only `propext`,
`Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, with no `sorryAx`. The source admission census
and `git diff --check` were also checked. Dependency pins, toolchain, shared
`.lake` link, and `.lake/build-DLMF` setting were unchanged by this batch.


## Finite identities priority batch (2026-10-18)

All eight targets in the next proposed P2 batch are proved. The source census is
now **150 admitted theorem proofs in 37 files**, down from 158 in 38 files at the
end of the first prioritized batch. No theorem statement was corrected or
restricted in this batch, and no admission or axiom was added.

### Completed statements

- [Relations.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Relations.lean): `kummerM_besselI_sum` and
  `kummerM_besselI_sum_neg`. This module now has no admissions.
- [ProductsAddition.lean](DLMF/Confluent/ProductsAddition.lean): `kummerM_product`
  and `whittakerM_product`.
- [Series.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Series.lean):
  `monomial_ultrasphPoly_expansion`, `ultrasphPoly_connection` and
  `ultrasphPoly_linearization`.
- [Topics.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Topics.lean): `jacobiPolyP_connection`.

### Proof structure and exceptional parameters

The two Kummer–Bessel identities are first proved in a normalized finite `₀F₁`
form. Reciprocal Gamma functions make its coefficients entire in the order
parameter. A finite contiguous-relation induction proves the formula on a
parameter half-plane, and the complex identity principle extends it to every
order. The original ordinary-Kummer statements follow under their original
nonpole assumptions. Kummer transformation gives the negative-shift formula in
the entire `₀F₁` form before conversion back to Bessel I, avoiding a branch change
at a negated complex argument.

The Kummer product is proved by showing that its left side has zero derivative
everywhere, using contiguous relations, and evaluating the constant at zero.
The Whittaker product then follows from the Kummer transformation and explicit
cancellation of its exponential and complex-power prefactors.

The new finite Pochhammer inversion and triangular sum regrouping yield the
Gegenbauer monomial expansion. Pochhammer convolution gives its connection
formula. The Jacobi inverse coefficient matrix is treated with a regular head
term rather than division by `α + β + 1`; consequently the original
`α + β = -1` cases are retained. Substitution into the finite Jacobi series gives
the displayed terminating `₃F₂` connection coefficients. The Gegenbauer
linearization proof checks the interior and two endpoint coefficient recurrences,
uses induction when one degree is smaller, and then exchanges the two degrees.
The degree-zero and equal-degree cases are included.

### Validation and remaining work

The ordinary full build, `lake build > /tmp/DLMF-build.log 2>&1`, passed with
9,573 jobs. Direct Lean checks passed for the four changed Lean files and for
`Main.lean`. An axiom audit of all eight completed former admissions and the
main new inverse/regularized lemmas reports only `propext`, `Classical.choice`
and `Quot.sound`, with no `sorryAx`. The source census and `git diff --check`
were also checked. This batch leaves the dependency pins, toolchain, shared
`.lake` link and build directory unchanged.

The five corrected Whittaker transforms remain admitted. Other nearby remaining
work includes the classical polynomial integral identities, first-parameter
addition formulas with their convergence arguments, Jacobi generating functions,
Mehler’s formula and the remaining Jacobi Mehler–Heine limit. Their P2 ordering in
the earlier review still applies after removal of these eight finite identities.


## Further admission priorities (2026-10-19)

This review follows the completed eight-proof finite-identity batch. A fresh scan
of Lean sources confirms **150 admissions in 37 files**: 65 confluent, 56
orthogonal-polynomial, 14 hypergeometric, four spherical-harmonic, two Bessel,
two Gamma, one q-series, three partition and three Selberg-application admissions.
There are no admissions in `ToMathlib`, `DLMF/Airy` or `DLMF/Painleve` in this census.
This is a count of the present formalized statements, not a claim that those
subjects or their reference chapters have complete coverage.

The Mathlib and TauCeti pins are unchanged from the preceding review. The
shortlist below was compared with their checked-out sources. No direct upstream
replacement was found for these target statements. The recommended work now
consists mainly of new finite coefficient arguments, explicit changes of
variables, and justified integration or summation using proved APIs.

### Recommended next batch: six admissions

The order below starts with the most concrete small target, groups the finite
moment calculations, and keeps the Morse result after its Bessel-polynomial
prerequisite. These are estimates of feasibility, not completed target proofs.

1. **`jacobi_adjacent_generating_function`** in
   [Series.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Series.lean).
   This can be separated from the general Jacobi generating function. For
   `Re β > -1`, the proved `jacobi_raise_beta` and
   `ultrasphPoly_eq_jacobiPolyP` give the coefficient identity

   `(2β+2)_{n+1}/(β+1)_{n+1} P_{n+1}^{(β+1,β)}(x)
   = C_{n+1}^{β+3/2}(x) + C_n^{β+3/2}(x)`.

   This exact complex-parameter identity was proved in a temporary Lean probe,
   retaining the original parameter range. Summing the two consecutive
   Gegenbauer sequences with the already-proved
   `ultrasphPoly_generating_function`, and handling degree zero separately,
   should prove the admission without a new convergence estimate. This is the
   strongest immediate candidate. Expected effort: small.

2. **`laguerrePolyL_product_laplace_transform`** in
   [Integrals.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Integrals.lean).
   Expand both polynomials with `laguerrePolyL_eq_sum_coeff`, integrate their
   finitely many Gamma moments using
   `integrableOn_cpow_mul_cexp_neg` and
   `DLMF.Gamma.integral_cpow_mul_exp_neg_mul_Ioi`, and resum into the terminating
   Gauss polynomial on the right. The existing one-polynomial
   `laguerrePolyL_laplace_aux` is a checked model for the complex Laplace rate
   and its principal powers. The coefficient reduction is the main new step.
   Retain `Re z > -1` and `z ≠ 0`; the terminating Gauss argument `z⁻²` need not
   be inside the unit disk. Expected effort: moderate.

3. **`laguerrePolyL_double_laplace_integral`**, in the same file.
   Expand the finite power in the integrand. Odd cosine moments vanish and
   even cosine moments against `sin^(2α)` reduce to Beta integrals; the radial
   Gaussian moments are Gamma integrals after `u = r²`. Resumming should give
   the existing finite Laguerre expansion. The proved trigonometric Beta
   integrability and `integral_sin_rpow_aux` provide a starting point, but the
   cosine moments and the radial change of variables must still be written.
   Include `x = 0` and the singular, integrable angular weights when
   `-1/2 < α < 0`. Expected effort: moderate.

4. **`jacobi_double_laplace_integral`**, in the same file.
   Use the same angular moments, a finite multinomial expansion, and the
   radial Beta integral after `u = r²`. The hypotheses `α > β > -1/2` ensure
   integrability of both endpoint weights and nonvanishing of the displayed
   normalization. The remaining algebra can be compared with
   `jacobi_finite_sum` or `jacobi_binomial_sum`. This shares useful lemmas with
   the preceding target and has no infinite-series interchange. Expected
   effort: moderate, with more coefficient algebra than the Laguerre case.

5. **`besselPolynomial_finite_orthogonality`** in
   [Associated.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Associated.lean).
   There is a direct finite-moment route, avoiding a new spectral theory.
   For each monomial degree `j ≤ m+n`, the substitution `u = 2/x` gives

   `∫₀∞ x^(a-2+j) exp(-2/x) dx = 2^(a-1+j) Γ(1-a-j)`.

   The existing assumption `a < 1 - 2 max(m,n)` makes every such Gamma
   moment convergent. Expand the finite Bessel polynomials, then use
   terminating summation or their Rodrigues identity to establish vanishing
   and the stated diagonal norm. The already-proved reciprocal-variable
   Laguerre expression is another algebraic route, but its degree-dependent
   parameter prevents a direct application of ordinary Laguerre orthogonality.
   Expected effort: moderate.

6. **`morsePolynomialFunction_orthogonality`**, in the same file.
   After the preceding theorem, use `u = exp(x)/λ`. The product of the two
   Morse functions times `dx` becomes the Bessel-polynomial product with
   parameter `a = 2 - 2λ`, weight `u^(-2λ) exp(-2/u)`, and factor
   `2^(2λ-1)/(m! n!)`. The original degree assumptions imply `λ > 1/2` and
   exactly the finite Bessel orthogonality range. The Gamma/Pochhammer
   normalization then gives the stated norm. This route avoids a separate
   integration-by-parts argument for the Morse ODE. Expected effort: small
   to moderate after the Bessel theorem, including the change of variables.

Completing all six would reduce the census to 144. The first four are independent
of the last two; the last two should be implemented as one dependency chain.

### Following priorities

- **Jacobi Mehler–Heine** in
  [Limits.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Limits.lean). This would finish that
  file's remaining admission. Reuse the regularized Bessel identification,
  Gamma-ratio limit and the Tannery proof pattern in `Confluent/LargeA`, but
  supply a new uniform majorant for the terminating Jacobi series: both
  numerator parameters vary with the degree. The existing confluent limit
  is not an exact specialization of this limit. Expected effort: moderate
  to substantial; high mathematical value.
- **Disk and triangle orthogonality** in
  [Multivariate.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Multivariate.lean). The proved
  disk polar formula now makes the disk problem a polar-coordinate integral,
  angular Fourier orthogonality, and a radial Jacobi integral. For the triangle
  use `y = x u`, apply inner Jacobi orthogonality if the inner degrees differ,
  and outer Jacobi orthogonality if they agree. These are good contained
  targets, with the remaining work concentrated in change of variables,
  integrability and boundary handling. They would finish this file.
- **Continuous Hahn and continuous dual Hahn generating functions**, followed
  by **Wilson's generating function**. Use `hasSum_kummerM` or the convergent
  Gauss/binomial series, their Cauchy products, and finite terminating
  coefficient transformations. They do not require the admitted
  orthogonality integrals. Continuous Hahn has entire Kummer factors and
  therefore avoids the Gauss disk restriction. A finite coefficient
  transformation remains to be proved; the new Kummer product theorem alone
  does not supply it.
- **Legendre addition and its product integral**. Prove the finite addition
  theorem in [Series.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Series.lean), then integrate
  over the angular variable to prove `legendre_product_integral`. This is a
  useful two-admission chain. The newly proved Gegenbauer linearization
  expands a product at the same argument; it is not this angular addition
  theorem. The Gegenbauer product integral is a related further development.
- **The Legendre singular integral**. Polynomial divided differences on each
  side of `t = x` remove the apparent singularity. Finite integration and the
  Legendre recurrence can then identify the harmonic-number eigenvalue. This
  is more contained than the Dirichlet–Mehler integral with a genuine
  singular endpoint.
- **Mehler's Hermite formula**. The Hermite generating function, finite product
  formula and Gaussian integrals provide good prerequisites. It remains a
  bilinear series theorem, requiring an actual summability/interchange proof;
  an L² expansion alone would not establish the stated pointwise `HasSum`.
- **Whipple's terminating `7F6` transformation**. The proved Pfaff–Saalschütz
  theorem and finite triangle-sum machinery make a double-sum proof a sensible
  independent algebraic project. It has no convergence obstacle, but the
  terminating coefficient transformation is still substantial.
- **Further orthogonality**. Continuous Hahn can use the proved four-Gamma
  Barnes lemma; Meixner–Pollaczek needs exponentially tilted Gamma moments
  with absolute-integrability bounds. The existing positive-real-frequency
  Fourier moment is a starting point, not yet the exponential tilt. Among
  q-families, prioritize finite q-Hahn, Stieltjes–Wigert, little-q-Jacobi and
  continuous q-Hermite before Askey–Wilson. Stieltjes–Wigert reduces to Gaussian
  moments under `x = exp u`; TauCeti's log-normal moments offer an alternative
  once the density normalization is matched.

### Confluent priorities and dependency chains

There are still 65 confluent admissions, but several Whittaker targets are
adapters whose Kummer prerequisites are also admitted. They should be counted
as completed only once the whole chain has no `sorryAx` dependency.

The most useful first transform pair is `mellin_regularizedHGFun_exp` followed
by the corrected `mellin_whittakerM`. The first needs a boundary Gauss summation
argument with absolute integrability, including exceptional second parameters;
the second follows by substituting the regularized Whittaker definition and
combining positive-real powers. The already-proved Laplace transform has a
strict half-plane hypothesis and cannot simply be specialized to its boundary.
Next consider `laplace_kummerU` followed by `laplace_whittakerW`, with parameter
continuation where the original hypotheses do not permit a direct Euler
integral proof. The five repaired Whittaker transforms are now correctly
formulated, but their full proof batch still includes cosine/Bessel transforms
and is larger than five normalization adapters.

The four first-parameter Kummer addition series and their four Whittaker
counterparts form another useful chain. The first M identity can use a
binomial generating function with `t = y/(x+y)`; the stated real-part bound
implies `‖t‖ < 1`. Its exponential companion should follow by Kummer
transformation. The U series need their own summability and continuation
arguments. Do not infer the infinite Kummer–Bessel expansions from the recently
proved finite expansions: the infinite sums still need majorants and justified
rearrangements.

For local U behavior, start with `kummerU_nat_denom` and derive the resonant
logarithmic estimates from it. This is a six-admission development with the
five remaining small-argument estimates. The parameter-continuation formulas
are proved, but differentiation at integer parameters and convergence of the
logarithmic series still have to be formalized. The final nonpositive-real-part
bound also uses the unpunctured neighborhood of zero, so its totalized value
at zero matters.

The joint entire-parameter Gauss theorem remains useful but is not a quick
consequence of the proved analyticity along entire curves. The Mathlib
summable-holomorphic-series theorem currently used locally has a one-complex-
variable domain. The stated theorem is Fréchet differentiability on
`ℂ × ℂ × ℂ`; it needs a multivariable argument or locally uniform control of
partial derivatives. Keep that work tied to this special-function result.

### Work to defer

The earlier cautions continue to apply to the sharp polynomial inequalities,
positivity sums, zero-counting and complex zero-location theorems, transition
asymptotics and simultaneous large-parameter estimates, and continued-fraction
limits. Laguerre Turán is a more contained inequality target, but its precise
unnormalized form still needs a positivity identity. Neither the new connection
formulas nor generic Sturm comparison automatically supplies these inequalities.
The Barnes–Selberg integral, Wilson/Askey–Wilson orthogonality, general Favard,
Verblunsky and the spherical-harmonic bridge remain substantial infrastructure
projects. The partition congruences, Apéry irrationality, finite-field Selberg,
Hasse–Davenport and the general Siegel inequality remain low priorities under
the project's special-functions focus.

Pfaff–Saalschütz. All 21 likewise have no `sorryAx` dependency.

These checks validate the named prerequisites and one finite coefficient
prototype, not the proposed complete target proofs. The extra TauCeti log-normal
moment API was inspected at source level only; it was not newly imported or

project. No admission was removed, and no Lean source, theorem statement,

the source census and `git diff --check` were checked; no project rebuild was
needed for these documentation-only changes.


## Six-proof integral and orthogonality batch (2026-10-19)

All six targets recommended in the preceding review are implemented. The source
census is now **144 admissions in 37 files**, down from 150. All six original
theorem statements, parameter restrictions, branches and normalizations are
unchanged. No admission or axiom was introduced.

### Completed statements

- [Series.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Series.lean):
  `jacobi_adjacent_generating_function`.
- [Integrals.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Integrals.lean):
  `laguerrePolyL_product_laplace_transform`,
  `laguerrePolyL_double_laplace_integral`, and
  `jacobi_double_laplace_integral`.
- [Associated.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Associated.lean):
  `besselPolynomial_finite_orthogonality` and
  `morsePolynomialFunction_orthogonality`.

### Proofs and boundary cases

The adjacent Jacobi generating function uses the proved finite coefficient
identity from the review. Mathlib's shifted-series theorem then combines two
consecutive Gegenbauer series. Degree zero is included explicitly, and no
additional convergence restriction was needed.

For the Laguerre product transform, finite Gamma moments show how a polynomial
Laplace integral changes under a complex scaling. This transports Laguerre
orthogonality to a complex rate with positive real part. Expanding both factors
in the scaled Laguerre basis contracts the double sum to its diagonal; those
coefficients are precisely the terminating Gauss coefficients. This retains
`Re z > -1` and `z ≠ 0`, including transformed arguments outside the unit disk.

The two double-integral proofs share a homogeneous angular integral derived
from the proved Gegenbauer Laplace representation. The angle of
`(x + i y)²` identifies its normalized symmetric Jacobi polynomial, and the
finite binomial formula produces the homogeneous coefficients. The radial
integrals are Gaussian Gamma moments for Laguerre and square-substituted Beta
moments for Jacobi. Integrability of the radial terms is proved before finite
termwise integration. The angular endpoint weights retain the full allowed
range above `-1/2`. The Laguerre case includes `x = 0`; the Jacobi case allows
every real angle, including vanishing half-angle sine or cosine.

Finite Bessel orthogonality follows from a useful new monomial moment formula:
for `a + j + m < 1`,

`∫₀∞ y_m(x;a) x^j x^(a-2) exp(-2/x) dx
 = 2^(a+j-1) Γ(1-a-j-m) (-j)_m`.

Mathlib's reciprocal-power substitution proves the reciprocal Gamma moments and
their integrability. The finite Pochhammer Vandermonde convolution gives the
formula without dividing by a possibly singular denominator parameter. It
vanishes when `j < m`; the top coefficient gives the squared norm. The original
strict finite-family restriction guarantees convergence throughout, and the
zero-degree norm is included.

The Morse substitution is `u = exp(x)/λ`. Its product integral becomes
`2^(2λ-1)/(m! n!)` times the finite Bessel product integral at `a = 2 - 2λ`.
The original degree assumptions imply `λ > 1/2` and the required Bessel range.
Positive-real complex-power identities and Gamma recurrences give the original
Morse normalization.

## Jacobi endpoint limit and multivariate orthogonality (2026-10-19)

The next three prioritized admissions are proved:

- `jacobi_mehler_heine` in
  [Limits.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Limits.lean), DLMF 18.11.5.
- `diskPolynomial_orthogonal` in
  [Multivariate.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Multivariate.lean), DLMF 18.37.2.
- `trianglePolynomial_orthogonal` in the same file, DLMF 18.37.8.

The source census decreases from **144 to 141 admissions in 35 Lean files**.
Both changed modules are now admission-free. All three theorem statements,
parameter ranges, normalizations and integral domains are unchanged. In
particular, the Jacobi result retains both original parameter assumptions and
the multivariate results retain the full range of integrable endpoint weights.

### Proofs

The Jacobi argument supplies the previously missing uniform majorant for two
varying numerator parameters. For positive degree `n`, the factor
`|(-n)_k/n^k|` is at most one; for `k ≤ n`,
`|(n+c)_k/n^k| ≤ (2+|c|)^k`. Terms with `k > n` vanish. A bounded scaled
argument therefore gives an entire regularized Bessel-series majorant, and
Mathlib's Tannery theorem justifies passing the limit through the sum.
The existing Gamma-ratio limit handles `n^(-α)`. Continuity of sinc gives
`n²(1-cos(z/n))/2 → z²/4`, and the positive-real Bessel normalization gives
the original conclusion. The useful auxiliary result
`jacobi_mehler_heine_regularized` permits an arbitrary complex second parameter
and a convergent complex scaled-argument sequence.

For the disk, Mathlib's polar-coordinate formula reduces the integral to a
product of radial and angular factors. Distinct integral Fourier frequencies
have zero angular integral. Equal frequencies have equal absolute differences
of degrees and distinct minimum degrees; `t = r²` gives the shifted Jacobi
orthogonality integral with weight `(1-t)^α t^|m-n|`.

For the triangle, the map `(x,u) ↦ (x,xu)` has determinant `x` and maps the open
unit square bijectively to the stated open triangle. Unequal inner degrees give
zero by Jacobi orthogonality in `u`. Equal inner degrees give distinct outer
degrees and Jacobi orthogonality in `x` with second parameter
`β+γ+2n+1`. Mathlib's change-of-variables and separated-product formulas apply
directly to the totalized integrals, so no extra integrability assumptions are
introduced. The geometric helpers are private; `jacobi_unit_orthogonal` exposes
the reusable shifted Jacobi result.

### Validation and next priorities

The ordinary full build, `lake build > /tmp/DLMF-build.log 2>&1`, passed with
9,573 jobs. Direct `lake env lean` checks passed for both changed Lean files and
`Main.lean`. The integrated transitive axiom audit of the three former admissions,
`jacobi_mehler_heine_regularized` and `jacobi_unit_orthogonal` reports only
`propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, with no `sorryAx`.

All three theorem statements were compared byte-for-byte with the versions at
the start of this batch. The affected-source admission scan and
`git diff --check` passed. The source census excludes the explanatory occurrence
of the word `sorry` in the documentation header of `Hypergeometric/Elementary`.
Dependency pins, toolchain, shared `.lake` link and build directory are unchanged.
Working proofs and validation logs are under `/tmp/AAR-next-batch`; no scratch
file was added to the project.

The next candidates are the continuous Hahn, continuous dual Hahn and Wilson
generating functions, followed by the Legendre addition/product-integral chain,
as discussed in the preceding priority review. The five previously corrected
Whittaker transforms remain admitted.


## Generating functions and Legendre identities (2026-10-19)

All six targets in the latest recommended batch are proved. The source census
is **135 admissions in 35 Lean files**, down from 141. Orthogonal polynomials
now account for 41 admissions; the other subject counts are unchanged. All six
original theorem statements, parameter ranges, normalizations and integral
interpretations are unchanged. No admission or axiom was introduced.

### Completed statements

- `continuousDualHahn_generating_function` and `wilson_generating_function` in
  [Wilson.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Wilson.lean), DLMF 18.26.19 and 18.26.18.
- `continuousHahn_generating_function` in
  [Hahn.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Hahn.lean), DLMF 18.23.6.
- `legendre_addition` in
  [Series.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Series.lean), DLMF 18.18.9.
- `legendre_product_integral` and `legendre_singular_integral` in
  [Integrals.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Integrals.lean), DLMF 18.17.6 and
  18.17.44.

### Proofs

[FiniteConvolution.lean](DLMF/Hypergeometric/FiniteConvolution.lean) derives a
terminating `3F2` transformation from Chu–Vandermonde and finite binomial
moments. Continuous dual Hahn uses it directly; continuous Hahn specializes
one upper parameter to a reflected denominator parameter. The latter
reflection is cancelled only against denominators already regular under the
original hypotheses. Neither proof divides by an upper-parameter Pochhammer
symbol.

Wilson uses a balanced terminating `4F3` coefficient transformation. Euler's
finite convolution expands two upper Pochhammer factors, Mathlib's triangular
sum reindexing reverses the order, and the proved Pfaff–Saalschütz theorem
evaluates the inner sum. Reflected factors then give the convolution of the
two Gauss coefficient sequences. This is a finite balanced `4F3` transformation;
the separate admitted Whipple `7F6` theorem is not used or discharged.

The generating functions follow from the proved convergence of their binomial,
Kummer or Gauss factors and the absolutely summable Cauchy-product theorem.
The existing OP `hasSum_scalar_convolution` interface is retained as an adapter
to the helper in the earlier hypergeometric import layer. All three generating
functions retain their original complex arguments and upper parameters.

[LegendreAddition.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/LegendreAddition.lean) proves
derivative ladders and the differentiated Legendre equation. Their bilinear
identities give the degree recurrence of the finite angular kernel. The first
two kernels identify it with `P_n(xy+st cos φ)` whenever `s²=1-x²` and
`t²=1-y²`. The proof handles index zero, both top indices and the vanishing tail
explicitly, without division by `s` or `t`. The derivative/Jacobi normalization
gives the original addition theorem for every real angle. Integrating its
finite cosine sum proves the product integral, since every positive integer
cosine frequency has zero integral on `[0,π]`.

[LegendreSingular.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/LegendreSingular.lean) proves
interval integrability of `(P_n(x)-P_n(t))/|x-t|` by matching it with the continuous
divided slope on either side of `t=x`, up to sign. The value at the diagonal
remains the original totalized zero. Splitting at `x` and applying the Legendre
derivative ladders evaluates the signed correction term in the degree
recurrence. The resulting recurrence gives `2 H_n P_n(x)`, including degrees
zero and one. No principal-value interpretation or extra integrability
hypothesis is needed.

### Remaining priorities

The four target files retain two, two, four and four admissions in `Hahn`,
`Wilson`, `Series` and `Integrals`, respectively. Useful next polynomial targets
are Mehler's Hermite formula, the remaining Jacobi generating functions, and
continuous Hahn orthogonality using the proved Barnes moments. The confluent
transform pairs and integer-parameter U expansion remain the next substantial
analytic batches. The earlier cautions about convergence, exceptional parameters,
sharp bounds and the more foundational projects continue to apply.

## Confluent additions, boundary transforms and Mehler (2026-10-19)

This batch removes eleven admissions, reducing the source census from 135 in
35 files to **124 in 34 files**. Confluent has 55 remaining admissions and
OrthogonalPolynomials has 40. All 231 Lean sources under `DLMF/` and `ToMathlib/`
were included in the census. Every original theorem statement in this batch is
unchanged, including its complex parameters, convergence domain, principal
branch and regularization. No new admission or axiom was introduced.

### Completed statements

- `kummerM_addition_shift_a`, `kummerM_addition_exp_shift_a`,
  `kummerU_addition_shift_a` and `kummerU_addition_exp_shift_a`, DLMF 13.13.3,
  13.13.5, 13.13.9 and 13.13.11, in
  [ProductsAddition.lean](DLMF/Confluent/ProductsAddition.lean).
- Their four Whittaker counterparts `whittakerM_addition_shift_kappa`,
  `whittakerM_addition_exp_shift_kappa`, `whittakerW_addition_shift_kappa` and
  `whittakerW_addition_exp_shift_kappa`, DLMF 13.26.3, 13.26.6, 13.26.9 and
  13.26.12, in the same file. `ProductsAddition.lean` is now admission-free.
- `mellin_regularizedHGFun_exp` and `mellin_whittakerM`, DLMF 13.10.5 and
  13.23.3, in [Integrals.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Integrals.lean).
- `mehler_formula`, DLMF 18.18.28, in
  [Series.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Series.lean).

[MobiusTaylor.lean](DLMF/Confluent/MobiusTaylor.lean) proves the Taylor expansions
of `(1-v)^(-s) F(x/(1-v))` on the unit disk using the contiguous differential
relations. The coefficient recurrence allows zeros and retains terminating
numerator parameters. The original addition hypotheses imply `|y/(x+y)| < 1`.
For Tricomi U, the transformed argument stays on the slit plane throughout that
disk. Explicit power-prefactor lemmas verify the principal branches in all four
Whittaker adapters. The proof uses no parameter division to exclude a terminating
series.

[Boundary.lean](DLMF/Hypergeometric/Boundary.lean) extends Gauss summation to all
regularized denominator parameters. At a negative integral denominator, its zero
initial coefficients are removed before applying ordinary Gauss summation to the
shifted tail. The reciprocal Gamma recurrence and reflected Pochhammer factors handle
removable singularities without cancelling an upper-parameter zero. The Mellin
proof bounds the absolute integral of each confluent-series term by its Gamma
moment. The proved Gamma-ratio asymptotics give a summable majorant under exactly
the original strict convergence inequality, justifying termwise integration.
Combining real-positive powers and exponential factors then gives the Whittaker
specialization, including exceptional second parameters.

[Mehler.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/Mehler.lean) proves holomorphy and a
first-order differential equation for the closed Gaussian kernel. Mathlib's
Taylor theorem gives a convergent coefficient sequence. The differential equation
determines that sequence from its constant coefficient, and the Hermite recurrence
verifies the same relation for the normalized polynomial products. The low-degree
cases are checked separately. The support theorem allows arbitrary complex spatial
arguments; the existing public theorem retains its real arguments. The existing
scalar-series uniqueness and monomial-shift helpers were moved unchanged into
[ScalarSeries.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/ScalarSeries.lean) for use by both
`Series` and `Mehler`.

### Gamma and Bessel feasibility review

A new admission-free [RatioBounds.lean](DLMF/Gamma/RatioBounds.lean) proves
`Gamma_ratio_norm_le_nat` for every natural shift and nonnegative real initial
shift. Each factor of the Pochhammer product has norm at least `|z|`, and the
Gamma recurrence gives the sharp reciprocal-power bound. This proof is independent
of the admitted general inequality.

The four original Gamma and Bessel admissions remain:

- **`Gamma_ratio_norm_le` (5.6.8):** the natural-shift case is now proved, but the
  unrestricted real difference still needs a fractional-shift argument on the
  complex half-plane. Taking norms in the ordinary real Beta integral gives a
  bound involving `Re z`, which is insufficient for the stated `|z|` bound.
  A possible next route is a rotated Beta/Laplace contour: for `d=b-a≥1`,
  rotate the integral of `exp(-(z+a)t) (1-exp(-t))^(d-1)` to the ray opposite
  `arg z`. The elementary bound `|1-exp(-t)|≤|t|` for `Re t≥0` would then give
  the desired Gamma moment. The contour deformation and its principal-power
  and decay estimates remain to be formalized; this is a proposed route, not
  an available proved theorem.
- **`mellin_barnes_selberg` (5.13.5):** this is the eight-Gamma de Branges–Wilson
  integral, with a two-Gamma denominator. The proved four-Gamma Barnes lemma
  does not evaluate it directly. A very-well-poised Barnes integral or an
  equivalent Wilson-weight evaluation remains necessary. Wilson orthogonality
  should follow this work, rather than be used to justify it circularly.
- **`besselJ_addition` (10.23.8):** the finite Legendre addition formula does
  not cover arbitrary complex Gegenbauer parameter. A plausible series route
  needs the finite Gegenbauer expansion of powers of the squared distance and
  absolute convergence of the resulting Bessel product expansion. Positive
  radii and nonzero distance must be retained in the power normalization.
- **`nicholson_integral` (AAR 4.13):** the existing Laplace integral for K does
  not by itself evaluate the product of Hankel functions represented on the
  left. A product-integral identity, its change of variables and absolute
  convergence estimates are still needed. Integer-order Y/K continuation is
  already proved in `SecondKind`; it is not the outstanding blocker. Arbitrary
  complex order must be retained.

No direct matching proved result was found in Mathlib or the pinned TauCeti
checkout for any of these four statements. Removing all four is mathematically
plausible, but none is a remaining specialization of the new eleven proofs.

### Next priorities

1. Finish the other confluent transform pair, `laplace_kummerU` followed by
   `laplace_whittakerW`. The first theorem allows unrestricted first parameter;
   its positive-real-part integral proof must be extended before the Whittaker
   adapter can count as completed.
2. Develop `kummerU_nat_denom` and the five remaining small-argument bounds.
   The integer-parameter logarithmic expansion remains a separate analytic
   development; the addition formulas above do not establish it.
3. Continue polynomial work with continuous Hahn orthogonality via the proved
   four-Gamma moments, then the three remaining Jacobi generating functions.
   Finite q-Hahn and Stieltjes–Wigert orthogonality remain separate targets.
4. For a Gamma/Bessel-focused batch, start with the fractional Gamma-ratio
   estimate and the convergent Gegenbauer/Bessel series. Keep the eight-Gamma
   integral and Nicholson product integral as dedicated projects.
