## Parallel admission-removal and reuse round (2026-10-24)

### Scope and coordination

The five requested proof tracks were pursued with three parallel workers and a
coordinating worker. An ownership registry assigns disjoint source files; advisory
file-owner locks and one exclusive integration/build lock prevent conflicting edits
and simultaneous builds against the shared Lake output. Staged proof experiments
and per-build logs are kept under `/tmp/DLMF-proof-round-20261024/`. Earlier
uncommitted work is preserved. No commit was requested or made.

### Targeted identities

- `associatedP_degree_deriv` (14.11.1) now reuses the existing regularized Gauss
  parameter-derivative machinery. The shared series lemma is generalized to a complex
  argument, and the older real-argument lemma becomes an adapter.
- `lowerIncGamma_multiplication_hasSum` (8.15.1) follows from Mathlib's entire Taylor
  theorem and the existing regularized confluent derivatives. The proof retains the
  logarithmic-plane arguments and proves convergence for every stated `u, v`.
- `goodwinStatonG_dawson` (7.5.13) follows from the proved Faddeeva Cauchy integral and
  exponential-integral representation, with an explicit logarithmic jump calculation
  and dominated convergence to the positive real axis. Its name, statement and tag
  are unchanged, but it now lives in `ErrorFunctions/Integrals.lean`, where the
  required integral theorems are available without a cyclic import. Importers of
  `ErrorFunctions.Relations` alone must additionally import `ErrorFunctions.Integrals`
  to use this theorem; no in-project callers required changes.

### Lommel foundations

Six independently proved lemmas establish the degree-two product recurrence,
small-Lommel recurrence, capital recurrence on regular parameters, and analyticity
and the ODE on that regular domain. The ODE proof reuses the existing Bessel
fundamental-system theorem. They do not invoke the admitted exceptional-parameter
limit. The four original all-parameter limit, ODE, termination and asymptotic
admissions remain: the missing step is cancellation of the Gamma poles, including
simultaneous poles, followed by continuation and the polynomial base identities.

### Orthogonality

- `qHahn_orthogonal` (18.27.4) uses the existing finite self-adjointness theorem after
  proving the terminating coefficient telescoping, finite-weight Pearson balance,
  and separation of the eigenvalues. The finite support and all parameter
  assumptions are retained.
- `stieltjesWigert_orthogonal` (18.27.20) combines TauCeti's lognormal moments with the
  existing finite q-binomial theorem. Integrability is proved before finite sum and
  integral exchanges.
- The extra `littleQJacobi_orthogonal` (18.27.14) was already supported by the project's
  unused `QOrthogonalityTools` moment-cancellation results; it now applies those
  results through the finite monomial expansion.

- `meixnerPollaczek_orthogonality` (18.19.9), including the diagonal norm, uses
  a two-Gamma Mellin–Barnes evaluation continued to the full tilt range. Rescaling
  lets the proof reuse the existing half-plane holomorphy estimate. The finite part
  reuses Chu–Vandermonde and the reverse Pochhammer basis from `HahnMoments`.

### Transform infrastructure

The Whittaker Buchholz series now has independently proved coefficient bounds,
absolute convergence, termwise cancellation of its principal-power factors, and a
representation as a regularized 0F1 sum. The equality identifying that convergent
sum with Whittaker M (13.24.2) still needs the Buchholz coefficient transformation.
The three Airy product integrals 9.11.16–18 remain: the whole-line cubic products
need oscillatory convolution/tail control, and the quartic integral is not a
consequence of the existing Mellin transform of Ai² alone.

The parabolic-cylinder product integral `pcf_integral_4` (12.12.4) is proved by
absolute convergence, dominated moment differentiation, integration by parts for
the symmetric-square ODE, and matching three initial data. The zeroth moment is a
beta integral; an integration-by-parts recurrence gives the second derivative,
and reciprocal Gamma reflection handles exceptional reflected Gamma parameters
without cancellation of a potentially zero factor. The original `Re a < 1/2`
condition and every complex argument are retained.

`DLMFMathlib/Analysis/ODE/SymmetricSquare.lean` extracts the product ODE and the
third-order Wronskian/Cramer uniqueness argument from the Airy-specific proofs.
Both Airy and parabolic-cylinder modules now reuse these Mathlib-only helpers.
The original Airy interfaces remain as short adapters.

The reciprocal-Gamma adapter `recip_Gamma_one_sub_eq` moves from `Confluent/LargeA`
to `Confluent/BasicProperties`, with its proof shortened to the existing reciprocal
reflection identity. Its public name and assumptions are unchanged, and importing
`LargeA` still provides it. This avoids introducing a large-parameter-asymptotics
dependency solely for the cylinder initial values.

### Uniform incomplete-Gamma inverse

Strip analyticity, nonvanishing derivative, and existence of a normalized local
analytic inverse are now independently proved. `gammaUniformAlpha_hasSum` and
`gammaUniformAlpha_printed` no longer have separate admissions: Taylor's theorem
and the inverse-coordinate ODE give convergence and the six printed coefficients.
Both still depend on `gammaUniformInverseLog_spec`, so this is consolidation of
assumptions, not two fully verified DLMF results.

The global inverse on the full disk of radius `2 sqrt π` remains the main gap.
The local inverse does not establish that radius. A continuation argument must
use the strip-boundary estimate and properness, or equivalent global inverse
control. The C-coefficient Maclaurin series and recurrences additionally require
removability at the origin and matching the Stirling coefficients; the uniform
remainder estimates remain separate analytic work.

Two generic helpers still live in `Bessel/ProductIntegral`: accumulation of real
points for analytic continuation and multiplication of a complex power by a
positive real factor. `PollaczekMoments` reuses them directly rather than copying
their proofs. Moving their primary APIs to a general analysis layer remains a
small follow-up opportunity; the present dependency graph is acyclic.

### Validation and final status

The final ordinary `lake build` passed (10,091 jobs). Strict direct checks with
`-DautoImplicit=false -DrelaxedAutoImplicit=false` passed for all 19 Lean files
changed in this round and for the `DLMF.lean` entry point. The compiled project
axiom audit covers 13,062 theorems: 205 still depend on `sorryAx`, down from 213.
Exactly the eight results named above leave that set; no theorem enters it, and
no nonstandard axiom is found. The two Alpha-series deductions still depend on
`sorryAx`, as recorded above.

Explicit source admissions decrease from 207 to 197. The remaining counts are
incomplete Gamma 39, Airy 37, Legendre 33, orthogonal polynomials 26, parabolic
cylinder 21, Struve 12, confluent hypergeometric 9, error functions 6, partitions
3, Selberg 3, Gamma 2, hypergeometric 2, exponential integrals 2, Bessel 1, and
q-series 1. Thus the Gamma chapter's two admissions are unchanged by this round.

The regenerated acquisition ledgers use 3,073 tag records: 2,882 proved and 191
admitted, with 178 upstream pointers. They distinguish the two Alpha-series
results as “proved from admissions”. No theorem hypotheses were weakened and
no new admissions were introduced.

The 423 modules under `DLMF/` and `ToMathlib/` have an acyclic import graph.
`ToMathlib` imports only Mathlib and its own modules; the mirrored Laplace and
Carlson files remain byte-identical to lean-codes. The shared Lake symlink,
project build directory, toolchain and dependency pins are unchanged.
`git diff --check` passes. Earlier uncommitted changes are preserved; this round
has not been committed.

## Reciprocal terminology (2026-10-24)

Project-defined names for reciprocals now use `recip` or `reciprocal`. This includes
`recipGammaDeriv`, `recipGammaCoeff`, the reciprocal-Gamma module names, the Bessel
operator `zRecipDeriv`, Airy reciprocal-integrand theorems, reciprocal-argument
hypergeometric transformations, reciprocal-factorial expansions, and local
reciprocal algebra, integrability, and asymptotic-series helpers. Callers and
acquisition-ledger references use the new names. The Barnes-residue comment now
identifies a reciprocal kernel. README records the naming convention.

Genuine functional and operator inverses retain their names, as do imported
Lean, Mathlib and TauCeti APIs. Invariant abbreviations are also retained. The
reciprocal-power asymptotic helper group for repeated erfc is `erfRecip_…`;
`invErf` and `invErfc` still denote actual inverse functions. The two mirrored
ToMathlib files are unchanged.

This is a terminology-only change. Comparing the Lean sources with the saved
pre-rename versions, after reversing identifier changes and excluding comments,
finds no mathematical-code differences. No theorem hypotheses, proofs, or
admission counts change. Old local identifiers and the two old module paths
are replaced rather than retained as aliases.

Validation passed: the full build completed 10,091 jobs, and strict direct Lean
checks passed for all 134 changed Lean files plus both library entry points.
The compiled audit finds no old renamed local declarations or module imports.
All 13,062 project theorems are retained; the 205 `sorryAx` dependencies are
exactly the renamed baseline set, with no nonstandard axioms. The source count
remains 197 admissions. Regenerated ledgers retain 3,073 tag records (2,882
proved, 191 admitted, 178 upstream pointers). `git diff --check` passes;
toolchain, dependency pins and build topology are unchanged. No commit was made.

## Proof priorities after the 24 October round (2026-10-24)

### Census and basis for the ranking

A fresh scan of declaration bodies, excluding comments, finds **197 admitted
declarations**, all theorems. The ordinary build succeeds (10,091 jobs). A fresh
compiled audit finds **13,062 project theorems, 205 depending on `sorryAx`**, and
no other nonstandard axioms. No Lean source was changed for this review.

The area counts are incomplete Gamma 39, Airy 37, Legendre 33, orthogonal
polynomials 26, parabolic cylinder 21, Struve 12, confluent hypergeometric 9,
error functions 6, partitions 3, Selberg 3, Gamma 2, hypergeometric 2,
exponential integrals 2, Bessel 1, and q-series 1. The five largest areas account
for 156 of 197 admissions, but their size alone should not determine proof order.

Rank work by DLMF relevance, reusable prerequisites, feasibility under the
existing statements, and removal of transitive admitted dependencies. A
successful build is compatible with admissions. A shorter source inventory
obtained by deriving one admitted result from another is useful consolidation,
but is not an independently verified DLMF theorem. Effort assessments below are
mathematical review estimates, not newly Lean-checked proof implementations.

### First: contained proof targets

1. **The two compact cylinder Chebyshev expansions**:
   `pcfU_chebyshev_compact` and `pcfV_chebyshev_compact` in
   [Computation.lean](DLMF/ParabolicCylinder/Computation.lean).
   `pcfU_entire` and `pcfV_entire` are proved, and
   [ChebyshevExpansion.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/ChebyshevExpansion.lean)
   already has coefficient decay on Bernstein ellipses and pointwise reconstruction
   from absolutely summable coefficients. Supply a shared complex-valued version,
   rescale the interval, and absorb the zeroth coefficient's half-weight.
   This should be moderate work with two direct applications. TauCeti's Chebyshev
   Hilbert-basis reconstruction alone gives an L² result, not the required
   pointwise `HasSum` at every point including the endpoints. Keep the half-line
   theorem separate: compactification requires regularity at the new endpoint.
   These are mathematical existence claims in the prose of [DLMF §12.20](https://dlmf.nist.gov/12.20),
   not excluded numerical tables.

2. **The finite Askey–Wilson difference equation**:
   `askeyWilson_difference_equation` in
   [QFamilies.lean](DLMF/OrthogonalPolynomials/QFamilies.lean),
   [DLMF 18.28.6_1](https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.28.E6_1).
   Expand the terminating Laurent series and telescope its shifted coefficients.
   The q-Pochhammer and terminating-sum APIs are already present. This is a finite
   algebra problem; Askey–Wilson orthogonality is not a prerequisite. Retain the
   stated denominator exclusions and check `n = 0` and `a = 0`. Moderate effort,
   with a useful difference-operator identity as the outcome.

3. **Fixed-x incomplete-Beta asymptotics**:
   `incBeta_large_a_fixed_x` in
   [BetaAsymptotics.lean](DLMF/IncompleteGamma/BetaAsymptotics.lean),
   [DLMF 8.18.1](https://dlmf.nist.gov/8.18.E1).
   Repeated integration by parts in the real Beta integral gives the finite
   expansion and a remainder bounded by a Beta moment. With x fixed strictly
   between zero and one, the residual power of `1-t` stays bounded even when
   `b-N` is negative. Use reciprocal Gamma factors at terminating integer b.
   The proved `incBeta_large_a_integer_b_exact` is a useful consistency check,
   but does not settle arbitrary positive real b. This is a more contained
   incomplete-Gamma-area target than the uniform transition expansions.

### Next: finish specific identities with substantial groundwork already present

- **Whittaker's Buchholz series**, `whittakerM_besselJ_series` in
  [Series.lean](DLMF/Confluent/Series.lean),
  [DLMF 13.24.2](https://dlmf.nist.gov/13.24.E2).
  Absolute convergence, principal-power cancellation, and the normalized 0F1
  representation are proved. The next deliverable is the Buchholz coefficient
  transformation, using a generating-function or Lagrange–Bürmann argument.
  Another convergence estimate would not close this admission. Moderate to large
  effort for a well-isolated missing identity.
- **Inverse-erf's convergent series**, `hasSum_invErf` in
  [InverseGeneralized.lean](DLMF/ErrorFunctions/InverseGeneralized.lean),
  [DLMF 7.17.2](https://dlmf.nist.gov/7.17.E2).
  The inverse and coefficient recurrence exist. Identify the local analytic
  inverse's Taylor coefficients, then establish convergence over the full stated
  interval `|x| < 1`. A local inverse theorem alone does not prove that domain.
  Moderate to large effort; keep the logarithmic inverse-erfc asymptotic as a
  separate project.
- **Whipple's terminating transformation**, `whipple_7F6` in
  [Identities.lean](DLMF/Hypergeometric/Identities.lean).
  The terminating case of [DLMF 16.4.15](https://dlmf.nist.gov/16.4.E15)
  is a DLMF target despite its current AAR-only docstring and missing tag.
  Use finite rearrangement and the proved terminating summations, or a finite
  telescoping certificate. This needs new algebra, not a direct application of
  the existing Dougall summation. Retain all denominator restrictions.
- The two remaining [Legendre hypergeometric representations](DLMF/Legendre/Hypergeometric.lean)
  and `pcfU_rotational_addition` in
  [Relations.lean](DLMF/ParabolicCylinder/Relations.lean) are secondary identity
  targets. The former need a combined cut-boundary limit and removable
  parameter singularities; the latter needs convergence at `t = π/4` under its
  endpoint hypothesis. Treat these analytic steps as explicit milestones.

### Highest-value analytic prerequisites

**Airy derivative remainder bounds.** Prioritize a derivative counterpart to the
proved Ai Stieltjes remainder machinery in
  [Asymptotics.lean](DLMF/Airy/Asymptotics.lean). Its immediate targets are
`airy_aiDeriv_complex_error`, `airy_biDeriv_real_error`, and
`airy_biDeriv_bound` (three admissions). Prove the Ai′ remainder estimate with
the required zero shift in the error multiplier, transfer it to Bi′ by the
proved rotations, then specialize the truncation to obtain the real bound.
Sharp constants and the sector boundary prevent obtaining these merely by
differentiating an existing Big-O assertion. Phase-error signs and
hyperasymptotic remainders should follow in later work.

**Lommel exceptional parameters.** In
[Lommel.lean](DLMF/Struve/Lommel.lean), prove locally uniform removal of the
parameter poles, including simultaneous poles, for
`lommelCapital_parameter_limit`. The regular-domain ODE, analyticity and
degree-two recurrence are already proved. Continuation of the derivatives and
the terminating base identities should then address `lommelCapital_ode` and
`lommelCapital_terminates`. This is a coherent three-admission target, with
`lommelCapital_asymptotic` requiring further sector estimates. Pointwise
convergence alone does not justify passing derivatives to the limit. The
limiting prescription is part of [DLMF §11.9(i)](https://dlmf.nist.gov/11.9.i).

**Incomplete-Gamma inverse and removable coefficients.** In
[UniformAsymptotics.lean](DLMF/IncompleteGamma/UniformAsymptotics.lean),
`gammaUniformInverseLog_spec` is the main structural target. Strip analyticity,
nonvanishing derivative and a normalized local inverse are proved. Global
continuation to the disk `|η| < 2 sqrt π` still needs boundary/properness and
single-valued inverse control. This radius is explicitly part of
[DLMF 8.12.11–13](https://dlmf.nist.gov/8.12), so shrinking it would leave the
original task open. Closing the specification would also independently verify
the two currently dependent theorems `gammaUniformAlpha_hasSum` and
`gammaUniformAlpha_printed`. Removability of `gammaUniformC` and its printed
zero values can be developed locally without waiting for this global inverse:
their definition uses the forward η coordinate. The full-disk
`gammaUniformC_maclaurin` and `gammaUniformD_recurrence` additionally involve
the selected inverse. Uniform S/T remainders remain further analytic work.

**Error/Fresnel zero enumeration.** `errorFunctionZero_spec` and
`fresnelFunctionZero_spec` in [Zeros.lean](DLMF/ErrorFunctions/Zeros.lean) are
the other concentrated source of transitive dependencies. Proving them would
remove two direct admissions and independently verify six existing ordering
and classification theorems. `errorFunctionZero_spec_iff` already identifies
the exact missing content: infinitely many sector zeros and distinct moduli.
Entire-function zero discreteness supplies neither. A contour zero-counting
argument with quantitative remainder estimates is a plausible route, but the
ordering must cover every index, not only sufficiently large indices. This is
high-value work, with a larger analytic investment than the first batch; see
the prose of [DLMF §7.13](https://dlmf.nist.gov/7.13).

### Orthogonality and convergence projects after the contained batch

- For q-families, target `continuousQHermite_orthogonality` and
  `qGegenbauer_orthogonal` through Fourier coefficients of the q-product weights
  and finite Laurent sums. The former includes its diagonal norm; the latter
  currently asks only for offdiagonal vanishing. `qLaguerre_orthogonal` needs
  monomial moments of its own continuous weight: the new little-q-Jacobi and
  Stieltjes–Wigert proofs provide patterns, not that moment evaluation.
- Prove `mellin_barnes_selberg`, the
  [de Branges–Wilson integral 5.13.5](https://dlmf.nist.gov/5.13.E5), before
  Wilson orthogonality. It has eight Gamma factors and is not the already proved
  four-Gamma Barnes lemma. Shifted weight moments and terminating summations
  can then address `wilson_orthogonal`; continuous dual Hahn additionally needs
  its own weight evaluation or a justified dominated limit. Avoid circularly
  using admitted Wilson orthogonality to evaluate the weight integral.
- The two complex continued fractions for upper incomplete Gamma and generalized
  exponential integrals should share convergence work. The existing
  `incGF_LF_tendsto` handles real parameters below one on the slit plane; the
  admitted public statements allow arbitrary complex parameters. Prove that
  extension with control of exceptional terminating cases, then transport
  between the two normalizations. The incomplete-Beta fraction is a separate
  convergence problem.
- `Gamma_ratio_norm_le` is useful general analysis but still needs the
  fractional-shift complex estimate. The natural-shift result already exists;
  taking absolute values in the real Beta integral gives `Re z`, not the required
  `‖z‖`. A rotated Beta/Laplace integral is a promising specific next milestone.

### Defer broad asymptotic campaigns until their common estimates exist

The 17 admissions in `Legendre/Uniform.lean`, the conical uniform expansions,
the cylinder turning-point and zero expansions, and the coalescing-parameter
Kummer expansions should be organized around a proved comparison equation,
normalization of the actual special-function solution, and a uniform remainder
bound. Existing coordinate and coefficient formulas do not by themselves supply
these estimates. Prefer one complete parameter regime before extending to the
other regimes and related functions.

Likewise, `angerScaledB_initial`, `angerScaledB_debye`, `gammaParis_printed`,
and the first/transition Beta coefficient formulas are not cheap evaluations:
their coefficients are defined using `asymptoticCoeff` of the exact functions.
They need genuine asymptotic estimates and uniqueness before the printed
algebra can be verified. The fixed-coefficient asymptotic inversion API explicitly
does not cover the polynomial logarithmic coefficients of error/Fresnel zero
asymptotics. Airy cubic product integrals also need conditional-convergence
control, and the quartic product integral needs a further product evaluation;
the newly proved cylinder product integral does not settle these automatically.

Retain the lower priority assigned by `AGENTS.md` to Apéry, partition congruences,
finite-field Selberg, Hasse–Davenport, Siegel, Vietoris, Favard and other named
supporting-source targets. Conversely, missing tags are not evidence of being
outside DLMF: besides Whipple above, `nicholson_integral` specializes
[DLMF 10.9.30](https://dlmf.nist.gov/10.9.E30) and `ramanujan_1psi1` corresponds
to [DLMF 17.8.2](https://dlmf.nist.gov/17.8.E2). Both remain legitimate later
DLMF targets. Do not prioritize an otherwise difficult isolated theorem just
to make its chapter admission-free.

For the next bounded proof round, start with the compact Chebyshev pair,
Askey–Wilson difference equation and fixed-x Beta expansion. Follow with the
Buchholz identity and inverse-erf series. For sustained infrastructure work,
prefer Airy derivative remainders, then Lommel continuation and the global
incomplete-Gamma inverse. Keep the zero-enumeration work as an explicit larger
project. Count a target as completed only when its compiled axiom dependencies
exclude `sorryAx` and preserve its full domain and exceptional parameters.

### Review validation and limits

The source inventory and fresh compiled audit agree on the 197 direct admissions;
the eight extra audit entries are exactly the six zero-enumeration consequences
and two Alpha-series consequences discussed above. Focused searches of the pinned
Mathlib and TauCeti sources did not identify direct replacements for the proposed
first targets; this is not an exhaustive search of upstream mathematics. DLMF
pages were checked for the cited scope and formulas. No new false statement was
established by this review; the admitted statements have not all received a full
correctness audit. No theorem was proved, no hypothesis or definition changed,
and no admission was removed in this documentation-only review.

## Priority proof implementation with parallel ownership (2026-10-24)

The requested parallel implementation proves **eight previously admitted
theorems**, reducing the pre-Chapter-4 source inventory from **197 to 189**.
The compiled audit has **13,111 project theorems**, of which **195 depend on
`sorryAx`**, down from 205. Closing the global incomplete-Gamma inverse also
independently verifies its two existing inverse-series consequences. No new
nonstandard axiom occurs, and the completed statements retain their hypotheses.

- `pcfU_chebyshev_compact` and `pcfV_chebyshev_compact` follow from a shared
  complex Chebyshev-series theorem for entire functions, including pointwise
  convergence at the interval endpoints. The real orthogonal-polynomial API
  now adapts this common result.
- `askeyWilson_difference_equation` follows by triangular action on the finite
  Laurent basis and telescoping, with the original denominator restrictions
  and the cases `n = 0` and `a = 0` retained.
- `incBeta_large_a_fixed_x` uses an exact finite diagonal-shift remainder and
  a uniform hypergeometric majorant. It covers arbitrary positive real `b`,
  including terminating reciprocal-Gamma factors, without restricting `b-N`.
- `whipple_7F6` is a finite exponential-base `q → 1` limit of the project's
  proved Watson transformation. It now carries the DLMF 16.4.15 tag.
- `hasSum_invErf` identifies the analytic inverse's coefficients with the
  existing recurrence and establishes convergence on the full interval
  `|x| < 1` using positivity of derivative polynomials and Taylor bounds.
- `airy_biDeriv_bound` is proved independently of the still-admitted derivative
  remainder estimates, using the Airy energy, the initial value, and an explicit
  polynomial envelope.
- `gammaUniformInverseLog_spec` now covers the full disk `|η| < 2 sqrt π`.
  Compact preimages in the logarithmic strip make the saddle coordinate a
  covering map; lifting the disk and the analytic inverse-function theorem
  produce the normalized global inverse. Consequently
  `gammaUniformAlpha_hasSum` and `gammaUniformAlpha_printed` no longer depend
  on an admission.

The generic Chebyshev machinery is in
`DLMFMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/ChebyshevSeries.lean`. The new
`DLMFMathlib/Analysis/Complex/TaylorParameter.lean` consolidates differentiation
of parameterized Taylor coefficients and uniform Cauchy bounds; the existing
hypergeometric coefficient theorem is retained as an adapter.

The remaining identities and analytic prerequisites have explicit checkpoints,
not completed admission removals: the Buchholz generating-function PDE and
Taylor-coefficient recurrence; the Ai′ Stieltjes representation and positive
Laplace-mixture estimates; and joint parameter holomorphy and exceptional-tail
reductions for Lommel functions. The final Buchholz identity, sharp Ai′/Bi′
remainders, and Lommel continuation remain open. Chapter 4 acquisition, requested
subsequently, is tracked separately and may add new source admissions.

Three workers used disjoint ownership and temporary staging directories. A
shared lock protected every compiled-import probe; the sole integrating writer
held the exclusive lock through source installation and each ordinary build.
The final build passed (10,099 jobs). Strict direct checks passed for all twelve
changed Lean files and the additional `DLMF.lean` entry point. The axiom audit
removes exactly the ten named direct/downstream dependencies above, with none
entering the admitted set. These counts precede the new Chapter 4 acquisition.

## Chapters 4, 16 and 17 acquisition and Elementary proofs (2026-10-24)

The new entry modules are `DLMF.Elementary`, `DLMF.GeneralizedHypergeometric` and
`DLMF.QHypergeometric`. The chapter ledgers enumerate 510 active in-scope numbered
Chapter 4 equations plus seven exact tables, all 101 Chapter 16 equations, and all
186 Chapter 17 equations. They also cover analytical prose and record numerical,
graphical, application and bibliographic exclusions. Existing Mathlib and TauCeti
results have checked pointers; existing project results receive native tags.
The older `Core.UpstreamElementary` import remains compatible.

The acquisition adds 447 Chapter 4 admissions before its proof round, 165 Chapter 16
admissions and 188 Chapter 17 admissions. These are new analytical statements in an
expanded scope. Branch-family and normalized-continuation constructions are admitted
explicitly; neither their derived definitions nor their downstream theorems are
counted as admission-independent merely because their own bodies contain no `sorry`.
`ToMathlib` receives general ODE-singularity and separating-contour predicates with
Mathlib-only imports and no admissions.

### Parallel admission removal in Chapter 4

Workers stage changes outside the repository. Per-file ownership and SHA-guarded
handoffs prevent edit clashes; an exclusive integration/build lock separates root
writes from shared-lock Lean probes. The completed batch removes **102 admissions**,
leaving **345** in Elementary. Every completed theorem retains its acquired hypotheses
and conclusion and has only the standard three axioms.

- Sixty trig proofs cover integer De Moivre, meromorphy of reciprocal functions,
  inverse/reciprocal identities, exact tables, norm formulas and bounds, integer
  orthogonality, elementary real and complex primitives, principal atan/acot
  calculus and oddness, and all seven Gudermannian/inverse-Gudermannian identities.
- Twenty-six hyperbolic proofs cover reciprocal derivatives, addition and subtraction,
  product/square identities, multiple angles and integer De Moivre.
- Eleven power/logarithm proofs cover magnitudes and phases, general and closed
  power-value inversion, integer powers, square-root cut cases and horizontal/vertical
  exponential images.
- Five logarithm/exponential primitives are proved directly by derivative calculus.

The remaining Chapter 4 source admissions are Hyperbolic 153, Trigonometric 71,
LogExpAnalysis 52, LambertW 43, LogExpIntegrals 11, Generalized 9 and LogExpBasic 6.
The computation and exact Bessel-connection modules have none.

### Source and domain review

The ledgers record branch correlations, both logarithm cut banks, conditional
natural-order series convergence, exceptional terminating parameters, removable values,
resonant limits and the difference between a defining tsum and analytic continuation.
Meijer G keeps its contour geometries and logarithmic sheets. The q-series APIs retain
DLMF's integer phase even when upper/lower lengths differ. Bailey chains now require
successive Bailey-lemma steps; the q-Lauricella dimension-two specialization is proved.

The printed 17.7.E19 and 17.7.E23 have exact Lean counterexamples. Their corrected
forms follow primary Gasper–Rahman references, rather than admitting the false printed
versions. The repeated left side in 17.14.E3 is corrected from its displayed constant
term; its linear coefficient already distinguishes it from the printed expression.
The acosh signs in 4.37.E11/E14 and the n=0 sign in 16.19.E5 are recorded explicitly.
The 17.13.E3 Gamma quotient has both a genuine removable-limit obligation and an
integral statement, rather than relying on a totalized limit without a limit theorem.

### Next proof priorities

1. Finish elementary algebra, derivatives and primitives in Hyperbolic, Trigonometric
   and LogExpAnalysis, checking Mathlib/TauCeti before each family. These give the best
   immediate return and need little new infrastructure.
2. Prove shared branch-qualified log/inverse-function series, product and continuation
   lemmas. They support the remaining Elementary expansions and reduce repeated work.
3. Close the basic qPhi/qPsi convergence and q-calculus bridges using the established
   QSeries and q-Gamma APIs. Prefer full-domain finite identities and exceptional-value
   adapters before broad analytic-continuation claims.
4. Establish normalized generalized-hypergeometric continuation and regularity once,
   then use it for derivatives, differential equations, transforms and Appell formulas.
   Meijer contour independence and resonant asymptotics need larger dedicated work.
5. Continue the older DLMF proof backlog with the now-proved shared tools: Buchholz
   generating identities, sharp Airy remainders and exceptional Lommel continuation.
   AAR-only results retain their lower priority. Askey–Gasper has a Chapter 16
   counterpart and is therefore in DLMF scope.

### Validation

The ordinary build passes (10,339 jobs). All 71 changed Lean files, including the
new chapter umbrellas, and both root entry modules are checked separately with
`autoImplicit=false` and `relaxedAutoImplicit=false`. The final compiled audit finds
14,148 project theorems, 900 depending on `sorryAx`, and no other nonstandard axiom.
A separate check confirms all 102 Elementary proofs, the eight original priority
proofs, their two inverse-series consequences and the 17 new q-series proofs use
only the standard axioms. All 517/101/186 in-scope numbered/table IDs of Chapters
4/16/17 have both tag coverage and ledger entries. The import graph is acyclic;
`ToMathlib` has no admission or forbidden import; the shared Laplace/Carlson files
remain identical. The build topology, toolchain and dependency pins are unchanged.
`git diff --check` and checks of newly introduced whitespace pass.

## Parallel proofs in Chapters 4, 16 and 17 (2026-10-24, second round)

Starting from commit `01ef167`, three workers and the coordinator remove **134
admissions**, all with unchanged theorem statements and only the standard three
axioms. Explicit file ownership, immutable stages, source-hash guards and shared/
exclusive integration locks prevent edits and compiled-import probes from clashing.
The source total falls from **887 to 753**. Completed targets retain their original
statements. A separate review of the remaining backlog found a false multivalued
addition statement, recorded below.

### Elementary: 92 proofs

Sixteen trig proofs establish the principal arcsin/arccos inverse identities,
full-cut-domain derivatives and analyticity, reciprocal-function analyticity,
oddness/complement identities, branch-point equations and complex primitives.
Forty-two hyperbolic proofs cover zeros, addition and multiple-angle identities,
Cartesian/norm formulas, exact values and limits, multivalued reciprocal inverses,
real and continued primitives, and full-domain arcsinh/arctanh/arccsch/arccoth
calculus and real restrictions. The hyperbolic file imports the proved trig API
for the rotated inverse-sine construction and norm formulas.

The other 34 proofs cover both logarithms on the negative cut, general powers
of exp(1), exact roots of unity and imaginary powers, logarithmic/exponential
inequalities, complex-power limits on the real axis, a global exponential norm
bound, and general logarithm/power sheet identities. The logarithmic, first-order
linear and second-order constant-coefficient ODE solution formulas now have proofs
on their original connected open domains, including the empty-domain case.

Elementary has 253 remaining admissions: Hyperbolic 111, Trigonometric 55,
LogExpAnalysis 20, LambertW 43, LogExpIntegrals 11, Generalized 9 and LogExpBasic 4.

### Generalized hypergeometric: 14 proofs and a definition cleanup

The new proofs establish uniqueness of a normalized principal continuation,
the Gamma/Pochhammer coefficient bridge, disk and entire series sums, equality
with terminating polynomials, exact radii one/zero, natural-order divergence at
nonzero arguments in the overbalanced case, the regularized 0F1 contiguous adapter,
and the finite cancelled unity-sum family of 16.4.E2_5, including its removable
limit. Exceptional parameters and the full acquired domains are retained.

`genRegularizedHG` now guards the existence of a continuation before selecting it,
with the native series as fallback. Local and entire series proofs can therefore
avoid the global existence admission. A proved equality identifies this definition
with the previous one under the existing existence theorem, preserving the germ,
upper cut values and endpoint convention. That equivalence explicitly inherits
`sorryAx`; continuation existence remains admitted. The existing `genHG_entire`
becomes admission-independent in addition to the 14 source removals.

Finite-product ratio estimates adapt Moritz Doll's private Mathlib proofs, with
module-header and inline attribution, and extend them to positive degree.
There are 151 remaining admissions in this chapter.

### q-hypergeometric: 28 proofs

Twenty-one Basic proofs cover complex-order/natural shifted factorials, reversed
base and index formulas, positive/negative shifts, even/block/square products,
termination with the general integer phase, idem expansion, full complex parameter
limits and the general qPhi series sum on its complete disk/entire domains.
The latter uses the exact term recurrence and a norm-ratio convergence argument;
zero or terminating upper parameters remain allowed.

Seven Calculus proofs establish the Gaussian quotient and integer-upper bridge,
fixed and jointly growing-index limits (including the exact s=0 boundary), the
convergent q-binomial series and Jackson-series summability. They reuse the proved
QSeries APIs. This chapter has 160 remaining admissions.

### Validation and next priorities

The ordinary build passes (10,339 jobs). All ten changed Lean files and both root
entry modules pass separate checks with `autoImplicit=false` and
`relaxedAutoImplicit=false`. The compiled audit confirms all 134 removed admissions
are now independent of `sorryAx`, and separately confirms the newly pure
`genHG_entire`. The intentional old-definition equivalence dependency remains
visible. The full environment contains 14,169 project theorems, of which 766 depend
on `sorryAx`, with no other nonstandard axiom. The acquisition ledgers are refreshed
from the final compiled declarations, including inherited dependencies of untagged
prose links. The import graph, Mathlib-only layer, mirrored files, unchanged pins,
source census, file modes and whitespace checks pass.

The strongest next steps are qPhi holomorphy and analytic coefficient APIs built
on the new full-domain summability theorem; remaining q-calculus finite differences,
Leibniz identities and Jackson limits; Elementary branch-qualified series/products
and remaining inverse-hyperbolic formulas; and Chapter 16 unit-circle convergence
and continuation foundations. The global continuation existence theorem remains
the central Chapter 16 analytical obligation.

### Nature of the remaining Chapter 4 admissions (second-round snapshot)

At this snapshot there are 253: 166 in trigonometric/hyperbolic modules, 43 for Lambert W,
nine for generalized exponentials/logarithms, and 35 in logarithm/power analysis
and integrals. They are a mixture of routine API adaptation and substantial
complex analysis, rather than a uniform measure of proof difficulty.

Within the 166 trig/hyperbolic admissions, a classification by main proof task gives
18 algebra/table/adapters; 12 inequalities, limits and pole statements; 23 series;
eight product/partial-fraction statements; eight continued fractions; 67 inverse-
function and branch results; seven general ODE classifications; and 23 primitives
or definite integrals. Boundary convergence and compatible branch choices account
for much of the work beyond the corresponding real elementary identities.

Lambert W needs construction and uniqueness of its normalized complex branch
family, followed by real properties, derivatives, coefficient polynomials, nine
series, asymptotics and integral representations. The generalized exponential/log
pair needs finite-iteration and least-iterate arguments, its inverse specification,
continuous differentiability and nonuniqueness. Neither family has a direct API in
the pinned Mathlib or TauCeti.

The other 35 admissions comprise three absolutely continuous path/log integral
statements and a power-value uniqueness criterion; eight bounds, two complex
asymptotic limits, eight log/binomial series and two higher derivative formulas;
six continued fractions, four definite integrals and one primitive. Some are good
adapter targets: Mathlib already proves `iteratedDeriv_succ_log`, for
example, while the logarithm-sheet statement needs the constant-shift bridge.

At this snapshot one acquired statement was identified as **false and needing correction**:
`DLMF.Elementary.arcsinh_values_add` (4.38.E15). At u=v=I, both auxiliary square
roots on its right side may be zero, so the right side contains w=0. On the left,
a+b=0 and sinh(a)=sinh(b)=I would imply I=-I. The set formulation therefore loses
branch compatibility. This is a mathematical counterexample from the definitions;
no Lean counterexample or corrected theorem was claimed in the second round.
The third round below corrects it and related acquisitions, with proved counterexamples.


## Parallel proofs in Chapters 4, 16 and 17 (2026-10-24, third round)

This round removes **47 direct admissions**, taking the source total from
**753 to 706**. Immutable stages, source-hash guards, file ownership and shared/
exclusive locks serialize repository writes and builds while proofs run in parallel.
Every removed admission is independently proved with only `propext`,
`Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.

### Elementary: 28 removals

Six logarithm series (4.6.E1–E6) now converge on their complete acquired domains,
including the admissible unit-circle points, the reciprocal half-plane boundary
and the imaginary axis in the Cayley transform. Their statements remain limits
of natural partial sums. Odd series use adjacent finite-term grouping before
passing to an even subsequence. The new Mathlib-only helper
[LogBoundary.lean](DLMFMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Complex/LogBoundary.lean)
proves convergence through an integral remainder with a compact-path denominator
bound; its adapted Mathlib argument retains Michael Stoll's attribution.

Seven further analysis proofs establish arbitrary-order derivatives of logarithm
sheets and full complex powers, the full complex and terminating binomial series,
the unit-disk logarithm norm bound, the two-sided punctured-unit-disk exponential
bound, and the complex zero-limit for positive-real-part powers times log.
The power-value uniqueness criterion now proves that general powers are single-
valued exactly for integer exponents. Two further proofs give the exponential-
ratio primitive and the improper Fermi integral with exact value log 2.

Four trig proofs give the complete arcsin/arccos/arctan inverse families and the
multivalued inverse-cosine addition formula. Eight hyperbolic removals give the
complete sinh/cosh inverse families and inverse-function sum/difference sets.
Four former full-set acquisitions were false:
`arcsinh_values_add/sub` (4.38.E15) and
`arcsinh_arccosh_values_add/sub` (4.38.E18). They discarded correlations between
square-root choices and inverse branches. The corrected characterizations retain
all parameters and shared square-root witnesses, and require both the sinh and
cosh equations. Three explicit Lean counterexample declarations cover the former
claims (u=v=i for the pure pair; u=i,v=1 for the mixed pair). These diagnose our
set-valued transcription, not a false printed DLMF identity. All other existing
theorem statements and parameter domains are unchanged.

Elementary now has **225 admissions**: Hyperbolic 103, Trigonometric 51,
Lambert W 43, Generalized 9, LogExpIntegrals 9, LogExpAnalysis 7 and LogExpBasic 3.

### Generalized hypergeometric: six removals

The proofs cover both finite inversion formulas (16.2.E3/E4), odd terminating
Dixon (16.4.E5), normalized bilateral Dougall (16.4.E17), the general analytic
raising-operator identity (16.3.E5), and nominal differential-equation order.
Finite inversions retain only the original retained-product nonvanishing conditions,
including degree zero. The integer Pochhammer/Gamma bridge handles shifted poles
explicitly, so bilateral Dougall includes positive integral lower parameters.
There are **145 remaining admissions**. General continuation existence is still
admitted and is unused by these proofs.

### q-hypergeometric: 13 removals

Three Basic proofs establish qPhi holomorphy on its full disk/entire domains and
entireness of the infinite q-Pochhammer product. Scalar power-series helpers now
provide the coefficient, radius, analytic expansion and all derivatives at zero.
Six q-exponential/sine/cosine expansions retain their original radii and q=0 cases.
Two finite Gaussian bridges prove the terminating ratio and negative-integer upper
parameter identities. The analytic product and germ-continuation pair identifies
the series with the product on the convergent germ, then continues the analytic
function. A divergent totalized tsum is never assigned a continuation value.
The existing proved `zero_phi_zero` declaration moves into Basic to avoid an
import cycle; its name, statement and DLMF tag remain unchanged.
There are **147 remaining admissions**.

### Validation and remaining acquisition defects

The ordinary full build passes (10,341 jobs). All 15 changed Lean files and the
unchanged root DLMF entry point pass strict individual checks with both implicit-
variable options disabled. The compiled audit confirms all 47 removed admissions
are independent of `sorryAx`; all newly introduced declarations have only the
standard axioms. It finds 14,211 project theorems, 719 depending on `sorryAx`,
including 13 inherited dependencies. The old guarded-continuation equivalence
remains explicit. The final tag dump, acquisition links and statuses, import graph,
Mathlib-only layer, identical shared mirrors, unchanged pins and build topology,
source census, file modes and whitespace checks pass.

Two existing hyperbolic acquisitions need correction next:
`hasSum_arctanh` (4.38.E5) and `hasSum_arctanh_large` (4.38.E6) assert unordered
`HasSum` through their unit-circle boundaries. At z=i every term norm is
1/(2n+1), whose series diverges, so those boundary claims are false. The DLMF
formulas require natural-order convergence there; unordered convergence remains
appropriate in the strict interior/exterior. Both statements remain admitted and
are flagged in the ledger. A compiled audit finds no callers of either statement,
so no proved local theorem depends on them. Further priorities are the remaining
inverse-hyperbolic branch calculus and series, q-calculus finite differences and
Jackson limits, and Chapter 16 unit-circle/continuation foundations.

**Definition defect (corrected):** in `genAsymptoticEkm` (16.11.E4),
`∏ i, a i - genAsymptoticAugment b j` parses as `(∏ i, a i) - …` (big operators bind
at precedence 67), and likewise the denominator; DLMF means ∏(aᵢ − bⱼ) and
∏(bᵢ − bⱼ). With p = 1, q = 2, a = (0), b = (2, 1/2) the perturbed raw coefficient
grows like 1/(3ε), so `genAsymptoticC_limit` and `genAsymptoticC_recurrence` are
false as defined. **Corrected 2026-10-25 with the user's approval** by parenthesizing
both products, `∏ i, (a i - …)` and `∏ i ∈ univ.erase j, (… - …)`; this is a
transcription defect, not a DLMF erratum. Both statements remain admitted.

## Chapters 20–23 acquisition (2026-10-25)

Five agents with exclusive directories acquired DLMF Chapters 20–23 (version of
2026-10-25, section pages downloaded once and read locally). New entry modules:
`DLMF.Theta`, `DLMF.RiemannTheta`, `DLMF.JacobiElliptic`, `DLMF.Weierstrass`; ledgers
DLMF20–DLMF23Acquisition.md. Excluded throughout: graphics; all Applications sections
(20.12–20.13, 21.7–21.9, 22.18–22.19, 23.20–23.21; their purely mathematical results,
such as Fay's and Frobenius' identities, Euler's addition theorem, the elliptic-curve
chord–tangent law and modular equations, are listed in the ledgers for a later decision);
methods of computation, tables and software. All formulas were checked with mpmath before
acquisition.

- **Chapter 20** (107 numbered IDs, all covered): nome-form θ_j(z,q) wrappers over the
  existing `jacobiThetaOneTau…FourTau`, products from the project's triple product,
  identities, Watson's expansion, relations, integrals and generalizations. 70 admissions;
  all of 20.2, the zero set, 20.5.E1–E4, 20.7.E26–E33 and the modular properties of k² are proved.
  `th20_carlsonRF` was a local real R_F placeholder; the Chapter 19 acquisition replaced it
  by `DLMF.EllipticIntegrals.symEllIntRF` the same day.
- **Chapter 21** (37 IDs): Riemann theta on any `Fintype` index with the Siegel condition,
  characteristics, absolute and locally uniform convergence and analyticity (proved),
  quasi-periodicity (all proved), modular transformations, Riemann's identity and the
  addition and four-product formulas (proved). 10 admissions. No upstream Siegel space exists.
- **Chapter 22** (235 numbered IDs and 9 exact tables): all twelve Jacobi functions in a
  lattice form `jacobiPQTau` (theta quotients, identities proved, including all of
  Table 22.4.3) and a modulus form `jacobiPQk` using the principal complex K(k), K′(k) on
  `jacobiModulusDomain` (Re k > 0, k ∉ [1,∞)). The modulus form rests on two admitted
  foundations, `jacobiTauk_im_pos` and the inversion theorem `jacobi_inversion_k` (22.2.2);
  51 proved results and 67 compiled dependencies inherit them. Real bridges to
  `Real.jacobiSnm/Cnm/Dnm` are admitted. 215 admissions. Existing real Ch. 22 tags were
  corrected: `Real.hasDerivAt_jacobiSnm/Cnm/Dnm` are Table 22.13.1 entries, not 22.13.E1–E3.
- **Chapter 23** (150 IDs, four by Mathlib pointers only): DLMF half periods (ω1, ω3)
  bridged to Mathlib's `PeriodPair`; Weierstrass ζ and σ, e_j, η_j, invariants, special
  lattices, relations to theta and Jacobi functions, series, addition theorems, integrals,
  asymptotics; λ, Klein's J, Dedekind η (Mathlib's `ModularForm.eta`), Dedekind sums.
  123 admissions. ℘(z; g₂, g₃) chooses a lattice; uniqueness of that lattice is admitted.

### DLMF errata and source corrections

All of the following were found by the acquisition agents and then checked again by an
independent agent with its own scripts from the DLMF text (16 confirmed, two partly; none refuted).
Corrected forms are acquired; docstrings and ledgers record each correction.

- 20.7.E34 holds only up to sign for general τ with principal roots (fails at
  τ = −2.19+1.29i); acquired squared for all τ and as printed for τ = it.
- 20.9.E2 (K = (π/2)θ₃²) fails with the principal K(k) for some complex τ (58 of 308 samples);
  acquired for τ = it. 20.9.E3 needs a branch condition (fails at real z = 1.6, τ = 0.8i);
  acquired for real 0 < |z| ≤ π/2, τ = it, which is correct but narrower than necessary.
- 20.10.E3 has a removable point at s = 1 (0·ζ(1) in Lean's totalization); stated for s ≠ 1
  with the value ln 2 separately.
- 21.5.E4 fails for general symplectic Γ (z-dependent ratio for g = 2) and contradicts 21.5.E7;
  it needs the theta-group condition. 21.5.E8's principal square root fails for g ≥ 3.
  21.6.E4: c_j, d_j are g-dimensional, not h-dimensional. (Representatives of 𝒦 in ℤ^{g×h}T
  are DLMF's own definition, recorded as a formalization note.) 21.2.E6 needs Ω symmetric and
  21.5.E5 needs A ∈ GL_g(ℤ).
- Table 22.5.1: the derivative of dc at K+iK′ is −k, not k. 22.8.E23: the printed determinant
  is nonzero (≈ −0.03+0.04i); a third column sn (or second column sn·dn) makes it vanish.
  22.9.E23: the last summand should be s_{3,3}d_{3,3}.
- 23.6.E29: the last sign is +. 23.6.E17 fails for some generator pairs with the principal
  K(k). 23.8.E1–E2 need |Im(z/ω1)| < 2 Im(ω3/ω1) (absolute value missing). 23.10.E14: the
  product runs over (j, ℓ) ≠ (0, 0), not ℓ ≠ j. 23.10.E15: π² should be π. 23.11.E2 holds
  for 2ω1 = 1 and needs |Re z| + |Re τ| < 1 rather than −1 < Re(z+τ) < 1. 23.11.E3 needs a
  factor 4. 23.18.E4: J has a pole at q = 0, so it is a modular function, not a modular form
  in DLMF 23.15(i)'s sense.
- Nonvanishing hypotheses are added wherever DLMF divides by a theta or elliptic function.

## Chapter 19 acquisition, errata corrections and the lean-codes dependency (2026-10-25)

### Chapter 19

Five agents (parts A–E, exclusive files) acquired DLMF Chapter 19 (version 1.2.8) into the
new area `DLMF/EllipticIntegrals` (26 modules, umbrella `DLMF.EllipticIntegrals`, imported by
`DLMF.lean`); ledger DLMF19Acquisition.md. Part A: Legendre's integrals for complex φ and k
along the straight path (`incEllIntFk/Ek/Dk/Pik`, complete and complementary integrals,
Bulirsch's cel/el1–el3, Cauchy principal values), §§19.2, 19.4–19.8. Part B: §§19.9–19.14
(inequalities, addition, asymptotics, reduction; 19.9.E10 excluded as numerical). Part C:
Carlson's symmetric integrals `symEllIntRF/RJ/RG/RD/R` and the integrand `symEllIntS`, defined
through or bridged to lean-codes' proved `Carlson.carlsonR`, `carlsonRF`, `carlsonRG`,
`carlsonRC` (the R_G bridge 19.16.E17 is admitted); §§19.16, 19.18–19.22. Part D:
§§19.23–19.26. Part E: §§19.27–19.29. Excluded: 19.3, 19.17 (graphics), 19.30–19.35
(applications), 19.36–19.39 (computation). The real `DLMF.Elliptic` integrals, the complex K of
`DLMF.JacobiElliptic` and the AGM are reused; only tags were added in `Elliptic/AGM.lean`.

Totals: 426 tagged IDs on 528 declarations (66 definitions, 163 proved, 299 depending on
admissions); 281 direct admissions. The Chapter 20 placeholder `th20_carlsonRF` was replaced by
`symEllIntRF`, with two new admitted 20.9.E3 forms. Integration fixed one collision: parts A and
D both declared `hasDerivAt_incEllIntFk/Ek`; part D's R_D forms (19.25.E6, E12) are now
`hasDerivAt_incEllIntFk_symEllIntRD`, `hasDerivAt_incEllIntEk_symEllIntRD`.

### DLMF errata

An independent verifier re-checked the eleven Chapter 19 claims numerically against the printed
pages. Confirmed (missing conditions or wrong formulas): 19.7.E2 (b, d) need Re k > 0;
19.25.E30–E31 need 0 < u ≤ K; 19.25.E32, E34 need x ≥ 0 (E33 is fine); 19.26.E9 needs p > 0;
19.26.E13 needs α, β > 0; 19.27.E8 error term O(√(z/g)); 19.27.E12 replaced by the
Carlson–Gustafson bounds (checked numerically, not against the paper); 19.28.E9 needs
x + y ≤ π/2 (sufficient). Corrected after verification:

- 19.20.E3/E23: the acquisition's description "off by a factor −i for complex arguments" was
  wrong; the formulas hold for Re x, Re y > 0 and fail elsewhere in the 19.16(i) domain. Added
  the admitted complex forms `symEllIntRF_lemniscatic_complex`, `symEllIntRD_lemniscatic_complex`.
- 19.26.E27: the acquired statement (with Re x > 0) was false at x = 1, θ = 2, where the left
  side is a principal value. The hypothesis x² − θ ∈ ℂ \ (−∞, 0] was added
  (`symEllIntRC_duplication_theta`; no failure at 3000 random points).
- §19.25(i): DLMF's range is already the complex strip 0 ≤ Re φ ≤ π/2; the failure is for φ and
  k² both nonreal. The Lean statements are for real φ, as before.

Earlier the same day the errata found in Chapters 4 and 20–23 were corrected in docstrings and
statements ("Corrected from the printed DLMF formula: …"), with two new admitted full-domain
forms (`jacobiTheta_reduction_iq_eta`, 20.7.E34; `compEllIntKk_eq_jacobiThetaThreeNullTau_fundamental`,
20.9.E2), and the false `hasSum_arctanh`, `hasSum_arctanh_large` (4.38.E5/E6) were corrected to
the open domains and proved, with natural-order `tendsto_arctanh_series`,
`tendsto_arctanh_large_series` for the DLMF domains. The project-wide register the errata report (reports/DLMFErrata.tex)
now has 62 genuine DLMF errata, 19 fixed transcription defects and 32 formalization notes.

### lean-codes dependency and `DLMFMathlib`

With the user's approval lean-codes is a Lake path dependency (package renamed `Main` →
`LeanCodes`; its modules build into `../lean-codes/.lake/build-codes`). The former mirrors of
`ToMathlib/Analysis/Integral/Laplace.lean` and `ToMathlib/Analysis/Complex/Carlson.lean` were
deleted and are imported from lean-codes. lean-codes' duplicate of TauCeti's
`DifferentiableOn.analyticOnNhd_of_finiteDimensional` was removed in favour of TauCeti's, so
TauCeti's `CauchyIntegralPolydisc` (and the project's Appell module) can be imported together
with lean-codes. Because Lean resolves a module's build files by its top-level prefix, two
packages cannot both own `ToMathlib`; this project's 50 general modules moved to
`DLMFMathlib/` (root module `DLMFMathlib.lean`; namespaces unchanged), and both lakefiles again
use prefix globs. Scripts (`DumpDLMF.lean`, `dlmf_ledgers.py`) and AGENTS.md follow.

### Validation

The full build passes (11,260 jobs). Strict checks (`-DautoImplicit=false
-DrelaxedAutoImplicit=false`) pass on all Chapter 19 modules, the umbrella, the edited Theta,
Elementary and older files, and `DLMFMathlib.lean`; `lake env lean` now resolves lean-codes'
`ToMathlib` modules. The compiled audit finds 16,253 project theorems, 1,328 depending on
`sorryAx`, no other nonstandard axiom; source admissions are 1,173. The tag dump (5,649
entries, 397 upstream pointers) regenerates all ledgers.

### Next steps

Replace the inline Appell F₁ of 19.5.E4 by `DLMF.Hypergeometric.appF1` now that the import
clash is gone. Prove the domain extensions of the Carlson bridges (one zero argument, limits on
the cut), on which many §§19.20–19.25 statements rest.

## Proof round 1 for Chapters 19–23 (2026-10-25)

Five agents with disjoint files worked from the committed state (commit 81e21fe): p22
(`DLMF/JacobiElliptic`, `Theta/Relations.lean`), p23 (`DLMF/Weierstrass`), p19s (the
`Symmetric*` files of `DLMF/EllipticIntegrals`), p19l (the Legendre-side files) and p20 (the other
`DLMF/Theta` files and `DLMF/RiemannTheta`). Priorities came from a dependency count: four admissions
blocked most of the others (`jacobiTauk_im_pos` 52 dependents, `jacobi_inversion_k` 42,
`HalfPeriods.e_distinct` 10, the R_G bridge 6); everything else was a leaf.

Source admissions in the five areas fell from 703 to 334 (EllipticIntegrals 281 → 187, Theta
74 → 22, RiemannTheta 10 → 10, JacobiElliptic 215 → 62, Weierstrass 123 → 53); project-wide
1,173 → 804.

### Highlights

- **Chapter 22.** Both foundations are proved: Im(iK′/K) > 0 on the modulus domain and the inversion
  22.2.2 (k = θ₂²/θ₃², k′ = θ₄²/θ₃², K = (π/2)θ₃² at τ = iK′/K), with 20.9.4–5. Then the twelve
  addition theorems 22.8.1–18 and 22.8.24–25, Table 22.13.1 and 22.13.1–24, all of 22.9 (cyclic
  identities; p = 3 by polynomial certificates), Tables 22.5.1–22.5.4 with the real bridges
  (moved unchanged from `Basic` to `SpecialValues` to avoid an import cycle; proved by ODE
  uniqueness), Table 22.6.1, 22.6.1 and 22.6.14–21, all of 22.15 and most of 22.16.
- **Chapter 23.** 23.3.12 from Mathlib's ℘′² equation; `e_distinct` and lattice uniqueness from
  the invariants via a Liouville lemma for elliptic functions; 23.10.1–10; σ in theta form and
  Legendre's relation 23.2.14 (moved unchanged with 23.2.17 from `Basic` to `Relations`); most of
  §23.6; the lemniscatic roots and invariants (K(1/√2) = Γ(1/4)²/(4√π)); rectangular lattices;
  η³ = θ₁′/2, Euler's pentagonal series, 23.19.2–4 and 23.17.1–2.
- **Chapter 19.** All of §19.4; 19.5.1–3 and 19.5.10; 19.7.1 for complex k, 19.7.2 and 19.7.4–7 by
  the identity theorem and substitutions; 19.8.6, 19.8.11–12; the R_G bridge 19.16.17; most of the
  19.25 Legendre–Carlson bridges for complex k; 19.21 connection formulas; the lemniscate constants
  19.20.2, 19.20.22; 19.18.12, 14, 16; 19.19.2–3; 19.22.2–3, 9–10; 19.26.11. New fully proved
  infrastructure: complex homogeneity of `symEllIntR` (`p19sR1_symEllIntR_mul`, in
  `SymmetricSeries`) and the modules `P19sR1Bounds`, `P19sR1Connection`, `P19sR1Legendre`.
- **Chapter 20.** 20.7.1–24 and 20.7.34 (all forms), the products 20.5.5–8 and log-derivative
  series 20.5.10–13, Jacobi's derivative formula and 20.4.8–12, 20.10.1–2, and the §20.6 lattice
  sums. Chapter 21 was assessed (Siegel transformations, the Riemann identity) and left for later.

### Statements corrected in this round

Recorded in the errata report (reports/DLMFErrata.tex); each was found by an agent and confirmed numerically by the coordinator
or agent before the change.

- 19.8.6 (project defect): the summand used the real root cₙ, so c₀ = 0 for k² < 0; now
  aₙ² − gₙ².
- 19.22.9–14 (project defect): `agmA`/`agmG` skipped a₁, g₁ (Mathlib's `agmSequences n` holds the
  (n+1)-th means); definitions re-indexed.
- 19.19.3 (DLMF erratum): needs ph zₙ + ph(zⱼ/zₙ) ∈ (−π, π), inherited from 19.16.11; hypothesis
  added, then proved.

One weak statement was found: `DLMF.Theta.jacobiThetaOneTau_div_sin_analytic` said nothing at the
zeros of sin z and was proved trivially. With the user's approval it was restated in
removable-singularity form (one F(z, τ), entire in z and analytic in τ, equal to θ₁(z|τ)/sin z where
sin z ≠ 0; likewise θ₂/cos z) and then proved from the Fourier series 20.2.1 with the Dirichlet
kernel sin((2n+1)z) = sin z · Σ_{|k|≤n} e^{2ikz} and Mathlib's locally uniform tsum lemma.

`JacobiElliptic` umbrella docstring updated; three duplicated bridge lemmas in `Quadratic.lean`

(e.g. `4.14`) were not recognized and were re-inserted on every run, and declarations were appended
to every one of several hand-written lines sharing an ID. Both are fixed, the duplicate lines were
removed, and regeneration is now idempotent.

## Chapters 24–27 acquisition (2026-10-25)

Seven agents with disjoint files acquired DLMF Chapters 24–27 (analytical results only, as
for Chapters 19–23; rules in the coordinator's RULES-ACQ2427 notes): ACQ24A (§§24.1–24.10)
and ACQ24B (§§24.11–24.16) in `DLMF/BernoulliEuler`, ACQ25A (§§25.1–25.10, Riemann zeta) and
ACQ25B (§§25.11–25.15) in `DLMF/Zeta`, ACQ26A (§§26.1–26.8) and ACQ26B (§§26.9–26.18) in
`DLMF/Combinatorics`, and ACQ27 (§§27.1–27.14) in `DLMF/NumberTheory`. Applications
(24.17–18, 25.16–17, 26.19–20, 27.15–17), graphics, computation, tables, approximations and
software sections are excluded; the ledgers list the pure mathematics found in the excluded
Applications sections (Euler–Maclaurin and Boole summation, Euler sums, the Chinese remainder
theorem, Euler–Fermat). Open conjectures (RH, GRH, Goldbach, Hardy–Littlewood's G(k), Lehmer's
τ(n) ≠ 0) are named as `Prop`s or recorded as "conjecture: not acquired"; results conditional on
RH appear only as implications.

Reuse: Mathlib's `bernoulli`, `Polynomial.bernoulli`, `riemannZeta`, `completedRiemannZeta`,
`hardyZ` (proved equal to DLMF's Z), `hurwitzZeta`, `expZeta` (DLMF's F(x, s)),
`DirichletCharacter.LFunction`, `gaussSum`, `catalan`, `Nat.bell`, `Nat.stirlingFirst/Second`
(DLMF's signed s(n, k) is a new ℤ-valued `signedStirlingFirst`), `Nat.largeSchroder`,
`Nat.Partition`, `Equiv.Perm`, `ArithmeticFunction`, Chebyshev ψ/θ, `legendreSym`, `jacobiSym`,
`ModularForm.eta`; the project's `eulerNumber`, `Polynomial.euler`, `polylogSeries`, Dilog,
`dirichletEta`, `catalanConstant`, `partitionFunction`, `qPochhammerInf`, `qBinom`,
`DLMF.Confluent.hurwitzZetaComplex`, `dedekindSum` and `logIntLi`. New definitions include
complex Bₙ(x), Eₙ(x) wrappers, periodic B̃ₙ, Nörlund polynomials, Bernoulli numbers of the second
kind, degenerate and character Bernoulli numbers, Genocchi, tangent and Lucas numbers, Stieltjes
constants, Riemann ξ, Riemann–Siegel θ and Z, N(T), general-order Li_s(z) and Lerch's Φ (their
continuations to ℂ \ [1, ∞) chosen from admitted existence theorems — flagged in the ledger),
Delannoy, Motzkin and Narayana numbers, lattice paths, restricted partition counts, plane
partitions, permutation statistics, rook and hit numbers, the twelvefold-way counts, Jordan's
function, complex σ_α, Waring's g(k) and G(k), r_k(n), Ramanujan's τ and DLMF's Δ = (2π)¹²η²⁴.
The existing Ramanujan congruences in `DLMF/Partitions.lean` are DLMF 27.14(v) and now carry that
tag, so they are no longer AAR-only.

Counts (compiled tag dump): Chapter 24: 192 IDs, 238 tagged declarations (28 definitions, 98
proved, 112 admitted or depending on admissions), 37 upstream pointers; Chapter 25: 171 IDs, 212
declarations (22, 45, 145), 43 pointers; Chapter 26: 220 IDs, 295 declarations (49, 84, 162), 82
pointers; Chapter 27: 149 IDs, 212 declarations (33, 83, 105), 49 pointers. New source
admissions: BernoulliEuler 111, Zeta 124, Combinatorics 157, NumberTheory 97 (489 in all).

### DLMF errata and source corrections

The acquisition agents reported 29 suspected errata or missing conditions, each with numerical or
brute-force evidence; an independent verifier re-derived every one from the DLMF page text. 25
were confirmed (some in part) and entered in the errata report (reports/DLMFErrata.tex) (a), Chapters 24–27: among them
24.4.24 (false for every n; corrected by partial fractions), 25.11.23 (denominator), 25.12.13
(branch error introduced by DLMF's V1.2.3 modification), 25.14.7 (no conditions printed),
26.8.31 (insufficient hypothesis; counterexample sin πz proved), 26.9.7, 26.10.9, 26.10.18,
26.12.24, 26.18.4, the inequality claim in §26.10(iv), the transpose-complement definition in
26.12, 27.12.3 (Legendre's formula computes π(x) − π(√x)) and the multiplicativity claim in the
first sentence of §27.3 (false for φ_k, k ≥ 1). The verifier did not confirm 24.14.10 (DLMF
already requires positive indices), 26.8.5 and 26.8.27/33 (k ≤ n is a Lean artifact; listed under
(c)); it found 25.14.7 valid on a wider domain (0 < Re a ≤ 1) than the acquired statement uses.

  Euler–Maclaurin argument (holomorphy in s and in a, residue at s = 1) and proves the bridge

  identity theorem, giving the corrected 25.15(ii) symmetry and `LFunction_zero_of_ne_one`.

### Statements corrected in this round

Both corrections rest on proved counterexamples; recorded in the errata report (reports/DLMFErrata.tex).

- 24.12.11 `tendsto_eulerRealZero_odd` (DLMF erratum, (a)): DLMF prints y_{2m}^{(n)} → m − 1/2 for
  odd n; the zeros tend to 1/2, 3/2, 5/2, …, so y_{2m} → 2m − 1/2 and the printed form fails for every
  m ≥ 1. Restated as y_m^{(n)} → m − 1/2 and proved; the counterexample
  `r24b_tendsto_eulerRealZero_odd_counterexample` is proved. Numerical evidence was recomputed
  independently by the coordinator (n = 21, 41, 61).
- 25.11.10 `hasSum_hurwitzZetaComplex_taylor` (formalization note, (c)): at s = 0, −1, −2, … the
  term with n + s = 1 is DLMF's limit, while Lean's `poch s n · ζ(1)` is 0; hypothesis
  s ≠ 0, −1, −2, … added (counterexample at s = 0, a = 1/2 proved). Still admitted.

functions, 26.10.4/10 (staircase bijection), Rogers–Ramanujan and Schur, plane partitions (23
admissions untouched). Chapter 27: 27.11.2 (hyperbola method), 27.11.3, 27.11.5, Mertens 27.11.8–11,
σ multiplicativity 27.3.7, the Waring statements once `waring_hilbert` is available; PNT-equivalent
results remain out of reach.

## Proof round 3 for Chapters 24–27 (2026-10-26)

Seven agents with the file split of rounds 1–2 (every file of the four areas assigned to one agent)
first wrapped every line over 100 characters in their files (about 170 lines, layout only; no line
in the four areas now exceeds 100 characters), then worked through the round-2 Next steps. Direct
source admissions in the four areas fell from 208 to 150: BernoulliEuler 35 → 25, Zeta 62 → 46,
Combinatorics 62 → 37, NumberTheory 49 → 42 (the three Ramanujan congruences in
`DLMF/Partitions.lean` remain). One Chapter 8 admission was also closed. Project-wide 1,012 → 953.
No statement was changed and no statement was found false.

### Highlights

- **Chapter 24.** The corrected roots-of-unity multiplication formula 24.4.24 (a periodic
  polynomial is constant), so `Properties.lean` is free of admissions; 24.6.2, 24.6.6; the
  quantitative zero bounds 24.12.2, 24.12.3, 24.12.9, 24.12.10 (Fourier series with explicit tail
  bounds; y₂ = (1+√5)/2 is a double zero for n = 5); 24.16.9 in both forms (a holomorphic logarithm
  of t/(eᵗ − 1) on the disk, then identification with the principal power); 24.16.8 (expansion in
  log(1+λt)/λ with the Stirling generating function 26.8.8, coefficient uniqueness, and a polynomial
  identity in λ). `Generalizations.lean` is free of admissions.
- **Chapter 25.** 25.2.4, the Laurent series with Stieltjes constants (the regular part of ζ is
  entire; its Taylor coefficients by Tannery's theorem from 25.2.7); 25.5.13 (theta split of Mathlib's
  completed zeta); 25.8.2–4 and 25.8.8 (25.8.2–3 continued from Re s > 1 by the identity theorem);
  25.11.8, 25.11.9, Lerch's formula 25.11.18, 25.11.19, 25.11.27, 25.11.31, 25.11.38, 25.11.39;
  Periodic 25.13.1 (Dirichlet test, new Mathlib-only module `DLMF/Zeta/S25bDirichletTest.lean`);
  Clausen 25.12.9. The definition `DLMF.Confluent.hurwitzZetaComplex` moved from
  `DLMF/IncompleteGamma/ZerosIntegrals.lean` into the new `DLMF/Zeta/S25bHurwitzCore.lean` (same
  name, namespace and body), which lets `ZerosIntegrals` close its admitted bridge
  `hurwitzZetaComplex_mathlib` from the round-2 continuation results.
- **Chapter 26.** Lattice paths 26.6.2, 26.6.4–7, 26.6.9, 26.6.13–14 (Motzkin/Catalan, Delannoy
  and Schröder generating functions; new Mathlib-only modules `S26aQuadraticSeries` and
  `S26aSeriesTransform`); Touchard 26.5.5; the Stirling generating functions 26.8.8–9; 26.10.4 and
  26.10.10 (staircase bijection, new `DLMF/Combinatorics/S26aStaircase.lean`) and the
  Rogers–Ramanujan partition identities 26.10.13–14 (from the project's proved analytic identities
  in `DLMF/Bailey.lean`); MacMahon's box formula 26.12.4 and 26.12.21 (bounded semistandard
  tableaux and TauCeti's Jacobi bialternant), the generating function 26.12.20 and recurrence
  26.12.25; §26.13 minimal transposition factorizations (TauCeti); rook numbers 26.15.9–11, 26.15.13.
- **Chapter 27.** The hyperbola method 27.11.2, 27.11.3, 27.11.5; Mertens 27.11.8 and 27.11.10
  (Legendre's formula for ln N! and Mathlib's Chebyshev bound); σ multiplicativity 27.3.7;
  primitivity from separability (§27.10).

## Chapters 28–31 acquisition (2026-10-26)

Six agents with disjoint files acquired DLMF Chapters 28–31 (analytical results only; rules as for
Chapters 24–27): ACQ28A (§§28.1–28.11, Mathieu functions of integer order), ACQ28B (§§28.12–28.19,
noninteger order, and §§28.29–28.31, Hill's equation, Whittaker–Hill and Ince) and ACQ28C
(§§28.20–28.28, modified Mathieu functions) in `DLMF/Mathieu`; ACQ29 (§§29.1–29.17) in `DLMF/Lame`;
ACQ30 (§§30.1–30.12) in `DLMF/Spheroidal`; ACQ31 (§§31.1–31.15) in `DLMF/Heun`. Excluded:
graphics (28.3, 28.13, 28.21, 29.4, 29.13, 30.7), Applications (28.32–28.33, 29.18–29.19,
30.13–30.15, 31.16–31.17; the ledgers list their pure mathematics, such as separation of variables
and Slepian's integral equation 30.15.3) and computation, tables and software sections. Mathlib and
TauCeti have none of these functions, so there are no upstream pointers.

Design. These functions are solutions of linear ODEs, often normalized eigenfunctions, and every
definition is genuine. Explicit constructions are used where they exist: Heun's local solution Hl
(a power series with its three-term recurrence), the Lamé polynomials (from the Chebyshev series of
§29.15 and finite tridiagonal eigenproblems), the radial Mathieu functions (Bessel cross-product
series 28.24.1), the spheroidal coefficients a^m_{n,k} and the Ince polynomials. Elsewhere the
functions are chosen from flagged existence theorems, admitted where not proved, as for the earlier
Lerch and polylog continuations: the Mathieu fundamental solutions w_I, w_II (uniqueness from
initial data proved, so the choice is immaterial), a_n(q) and b_n(q) for real q (ordered elements of
the periodic spectrum), λ_ν(q) and me_ν (endpoint of a continuous continuation along [0, q]), Hill's
global existence, the Lamé eigenvalues and Ec/Es (regular Sturm–Liouville theory), λ^m_n(γ²) and
𝖯s, 𝖰s, 𝑄s, and the Heun function and Stieltjes polynomials. The ledgers flag each choice.
Domain choices: §§28.12(ii)–(iii) and 28.14 for ν ∉ ℤ; Mathieu eigenvalues for real q; spheroidal
radial asymptotics for real γ > 0.

Counts (compiled tag dump; declarations "admitted" include those depending on admissions):
Chapter 28: 369 IDs on 497 declarations (83 definitions, 52 proved, 362 admitted); Chapter 29: 212
IDs on 198 declarations (32, 21, 145); Chapter 30: 76 IDs on 135 declarations (39, 20, 76);
Chapter 31: 121 IDs on 139 declarations (42, 24, 73). New source admissions: Mathieu 314, Lamé 128,
Spheroidal 56, Heun 72 (570 in all). Among the proved results: the equivalence of the N = 3
Fuchsian equation with Heun's equation and Fuchs's exponent relations (31.14), van Vleck's zeros
and the accessory relation of §31.15, the eight finite Fourier series of the Lamé polynomials
(29.15), the Whittaker–Hill to Ince reduction, symmetry relations of λ_ν and me_ν, and the
Mathieu reduction of the spheroidal equation.

statement was corrected accordingly), §28.31(iii) (powers swapped), 30.11.6 (e^{|ℑ(γz)|}), 31.10.8,
31.10.17, 31.10.22–23 (Fuchs's relation violated) and 31.10 Example 2 (accessory parameter not
updated after DLMF's own 31.3.10 erratum). Notes in (c): 28.6.16 at n = 0, 28.5.5 at n = 0, and the
29.8 Ferrers/Legendre notation. Not confirmed: 28.6.12–13, where the agent had misread DLMF's digit
grouping; the statements agree with DLMF and the correction notes were removed.

### Next steps

Cheap targets: the existence theorems for linear ODEs with entire coefficients (Mathieu w_I, w_II,
Hill), from which many Mathieu statements follow; Heun's Hl convergence on |z| < min(1, |a|) from the
recurrence; the Lamé polynomial links to Ec/Es; the spheroidal parity and Wronskian relations from
the existence theorem; the Ince polynomial existence. Harder: Sturm–Liouville ordering and zero
counts for the eigenvalue characterizations (DLMFMathlib's Sturm modules), the small-q expansions,
and the Bessel-series expansions of the modified Mathieu functions.

## Ramanujan's τ and the prime number theorem (2026-10-26)

TauCeti was re-pinned from `5cdecf40` to `a049b4264` (Mathlib unchanged at `911003dd`), jointly
in lean-DLMF, lean-codes, lean-CA, lean-LCS and lean-SCV, because they share one TauCeti checkout
in `.lake/packages` (user decision). At the old pin TauCeti's field-theory modules failed against
our Mathlib (deprecations under `warningAsError`); `a049b4264` was tested against a Mathlib only
five commits ahead of ours, and a search showed it uses none of the names those commits add. The
local `ContinuousMap.coe_coe` patch (`TauCeti-4.36.patch`) is no longer needed. All five
projects build at the new pin.

Two agents then proved, without statement changes:
- q27b (Partitions.lean, new `DLMF/NumberTheory/T27bHecke.lean`): multiplicativity of τ
  (§27.14(vi)), the Hecke relation 27.14.19, and τ(n) ≡ σ₁₁(n) (mod 691) (27.14.20) from
  E₁₂ − E₆² = cΔ (Mathlib's dimension formula) and TauCeti's discriminant eigenform.
- q27a (new `DLMFMathlib/NumberTheory/T27aPrimeNumberTheorem.lean`, Mathlib and TauCeti only):
  the bridge from TauCeti's prime ideal theorem for ℚ (height-one primes of 𝓞 ℚ counted by norm)
  to `Nat.primeCounting`, giving π(x) ~ x/log x (27.2.3), p_n ~ n log n (27.2.4, by inversion),
  27.12.1, θ(x) ~ x and ψ(x) ~ x, and from TauCeti's natural-density Chebotarev theorem the prime
  number theorem in arithmetic progressions (`primeCounting_ap_isEquivalent`, §27.11). Every
  NumberTheory file now imports TauCeti's PNT modules through `Functions.lean`.

Not reachable from these results: 27.11.13–15 (M(x) = o(x) needs a Landau-type argument),
27.11.9 and 27.11.11 (O(1) error terms), 27.12.4–8 (error terms; TauCeti has no zero-free
region) and Rosser's inequality 27.12.2. Admissions in DLMF/NumberTheory: 42 → 36. CREDITS lists
the TauCeti and Mathlib declarations used.

Validation: full build 13,416 jobs (the TauCeti PNT and Chebotarev closure adds about 1,140);
audit 20,301 theorems, 1,700 depending on `sorryAx`, no other nonstandard axiom; tag dump 7,998
entries; import survey: nothing new; ledger 27 regenerated. Project source admissions: 1,517.

## Proof round 2 for Chapters 28–31 (2026-10-26)

Eight agents with disjoint files (later resumed on freed files), helper prefixes `p2s_` (Mathieu

Mathieu functions), `p2x_` (noninteger order, second solutions), `p2l_` (Lamé eigenvalues and

- `Analysis/ODE/P2sSturmSpectrum`, `P2sGrowth`, `P2sOscillation`: spectra of real Sturm–Liouville
  problems via entire boundary functions (enumeration of a discrete set bounded below, Grönwall
  energy estimates, sign changes for large λ, infinitely many zeros when the coefficient tends to
  +∞).

  (spectrum, discreteness, parity, comparison, enumeration).

  Dirichlet/Neumann counts and interlacing, signs at the endpoint.
